ŚB 2.1.1

श्रीशुक उवाच
वरीयानेष ते प्रश्न: कृतो लोकहितं
नृप ।
आत्मवित्सम्मत: पुंसां श्रोतव्यादिषु य: पर: ॥ १ ॥

Śrī Śukadeva Gosvāmī said: My dear King, your question is glorious because it is very beneficial to all kinds of people. The answer to this question is the prime subject matter for hearing, and it is approved by all transcendentalists.

Word-for-word
Bhāgavatam — Word-for-word
śrī-śukaḥ uvāca — Śrī Śukadeva Gosvāmī said; varīyān — glorious; eṣaḥ — this; te — your; praśnaḥ — question; kṛtaḥ — made by you; loka-hitam — beneficial for all men; nṛpa — O King; ātmavit — transcendentalist; sammataḥ — approved; puṁsām — of all men; śrotavya-ādiṣu — in all kinds of hearing; yaḥ — what is; paraḥ — the supreme.
Śrīdhara — Word-for-word
rājñaḥ — of the king; praśnam — the question; abhinandati — praises; varīyān iti — with the word “varīyān”; te — “te”; tvayā — by you; puṁsāṁ śrotavyādiṣu madhye — among things to be heard by people and the like; yaḥ paraḥ śreṣṭha-gocaraḥ — which has the supreme, the highest, as its object; praśnaḥ kṛtaḥ — the question asked; eṣaḥ varīyān — this is most excellent; yataḥ loka-hitam etat — because this benefits the world; mokṣa-hetutvāt — because it is a cause of liberation; ātma-vidām muktānām ca — of knowers of the Self and of the liberated; saṁmataḥ — approved.
Transliteration
Bhāgavatam — Transliteration
śrī-śuka uvāca
varīyān eṣa te praśnaḥ
kṛto loka-hitaṁ nṛpa
ātmavit-sammataḥ puṁsāṁ
śrotavyādiṣu yaḥ paraḥ
Śrīdhara — Transliteration
rājñaḥ praśnam abhinandati—varīyān iti | te tvayā | puṁsāṁ śrotavyādiṣu madhye yaḥ paraḥ śreṣṭhagocaraḥ praśnaḥ kṛta eṣa varīyān | yato lokahitam etat, mokṣahetutvāt | ātmavidāṁ muktānāṁ ca saṁmato yataḥ ||1||
Śrīdhara Sanskrit
राज्ञः प्रश्नम् अभिनन्दति—वरीयान् इति । ते त्वया । पुंसां श्रोतव्यादिषु मध्ये यः परः श्रेष्ठगोचरः प्रश्नः कृत एष वरीयान् । यतो लोकहितम् एतत्, मोक्षहेतुत्वात् । आत्मविदां मुक्तानां च संमतो यतः ॥१॥

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. He praises the king’s question with the word varīyān. Te means “by you.” Among the things people may hear about, this question asked by you has the supreme—the highest—as its object and is therefore most excellent. It benefits the world because it is a cause of liberation, and it is approved by knowers of the Self and by the liberated.

ŚB 2.1.2

श्रोतव्यादीनि राजेन्द्र नृणां सन्ति सहस्रश: ।
अपश्यतामात्मतत्त्वं गृहेषु गृहमेधिनाम् ॥ २ ॥

Those persons who are materially engrossed, being blind to the knowledge of ultimate truth, have many subject matters for hearing in human society, O Emperor.

Word-for-word
Bhāgavatam — Word-for-word
śrotavya-ādīni — subject matters for hearing; rāja-indra — O Emperor; nṛṇām — of human society; santi — there are; sahasraśaḥ — hundreds and thousands; apaśyatām — of the blind; ātma-tattvam — knowledge of self, the ultimate truth; gṛheṣu — at home; gṛha-medhinām — of persons too materially engrossed.
Śrīdhara — Word-for-word
tatra tāvat — there, first of all; svābhāvika-kriyāṇām — of habitual activities; anartha-hetutvam — being a cause of harm; vadan — stating; uttaram āha — he gives the answer; śrotavyādīni iti tribhiḥ — by three verses beginning “śrotavyādīni”; gṛheṣu saktānām — of those attached to homes; gṛha-medhinām — householders absorbed in the home; pañca-sūnā-parāṇām — occupied with the five household sources of injury; medhatiḥ hiṁsārthaḥ — the root medh has the sense of harming.
Transliteration
Bhāgavatam — Transliteration
śrotavyādīni rājendra
nṛṇāṁ santi sahasraśaḥ
apaśyatām ātma-tattvaṁ
gṛheṣu gṛha-medhinām
Śrīdhara — Transliteration
tatra tāvat svābhāvikakriyāṇām anarthahetutvaṁ vadan brūhi yad vā, viparyayam ityasyottaram āha—śrotavyādīnīti tribhiḥ | gṛheṣu saktānām ata eva gṛhamedhināṁ tadgatapañcasūnāparāṇām | medhatir hiṁsārthaḥ ||2||
Śrīdhara Sanskrit
तत्र तावत् स्वाभाविकक्रियाणाम् अनर्थहेतुत्वं वदन् ब्रूहि यद् वा, विपर्ययम् इत्यस्योत्तरम् आह—श्रोतव्यादीनीति त्रिभिः । गृहेषु सक्तानाम् अत एव गृहमेधिनां तद्गतपञ्चसूनापराणाम् । मेधतिर् हिंसार्थः ॥२॥

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. First he states that ordinary habitual activities become causes of harm, and with the three verses beginning śrotavyādīni he answers the contrary possibility. “Householders” are those attached to their homes and occupied with the household activities involving the five sources of injury. The root medh carries the sense of harming.

ŚB 2.1.3

निद्रया ह्रियते नक्तं व्यवायेन च वा वय: ।
दिवा चार्थेहया राजन् कुटुम्बभरणेन वा ॥ ३ ॥

The lifetime of such an envious householder is passed at night either in sleeping or in sex indulgence, and in the daytime either in making money or maintaining family members.

Word-for-word
Bhāgavatam — Word-for-word
nidrayā — by sleeping; hriyate — wastes; naktam — night; vyavāyena — sex indulgence; ca — also; vā — either; vayaḥ — duration of life; divā — days; ca — and; artha — economic; īhayā — development; rājan — O King; kuṭumba — family members; bharaṇena — maintaining; vā — either.
Śrīdhara — Word-for-word
taiḥ ca — and by those; vṛthā eva āyuḥ-vyayaḥ — life is spent in vain; naktam rātrau — at night; yat vayaḥ āyuḥ — whatever span of life; nidrayā — by sleep; vyavāyena ratyā vā — or by sexual enjoyment; hriyate — is taken away; divā ahni — by day; artha-artham udyamena — by effort for wealth; siddhe arthe — once wealth is obtained; kuṭumba-poṣaṇena — by maintaining the family; ca-kārau — the two words “ca”; anukta-samuccaya-arthau — include other unmentioned items.
Transliteration
Bhāgavatam — Transliteration
nidrayā hriyate naktaṁ
vyavāyena ca vā vayaḥ
divā cārthehayā rājan
kuṭumba-bharaṇena vā
Śrīdhara — Transliteration
taiś ca vṛthaivāyur vyayo bhavatīty āha | naktaṁ rātrau yad vaya āyus tan nidrayā vyavāyena ratyā vā hriyate | divā ahni yad vayas tad arthārtham udyamena, siddhe’rthe kuṭumbapoṣaṇena veti | cakārāv anuktasamuccayārthau ||3||
Śrīdhara Sanskrit
तैश् च वृथैवायुर् व्ययो भवतीत्य् आह । नक्तं रात्रौ यद् वय आयुस् तन् निद्रया व्यवायेन रत्या वा ह्रियते । दिवा अह्नि यद् वयस् तद् अर्थार्थम् उद्यमेन, सिद्धेऽर्थे कुटुम्बपोषणेन वेति । चकाराव् अनुक्तसमुच्चयार्थौ ॥३॥

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. He says that life is simply wasted through these activities. At night, one’s span of life is taken away by sleep or sexual enjoyment. By day it is spent striving for wealth, and when wealth has been obtained, maintaining the family. The two occurrences of ca also include other similar activities not explicitly mentioned.

ŚB 2.1.4

देहापत्यकलत्रादिष्वात्मसैन्येष्वसत्स्वपि ।
तेषां प्रमत्तो निधनं पश्यन्नपि न पश्यति ॥ ४ ॥

Persons devoid of ātma-tattva do not inquire into the problems of life, being too attached to the fallible soldiers like the body, children and wife. Although sufficiently experienced, they still do not see their inevitable destruction.

Word-for-word
Bhāgavatam — Word-for-word
deha — body; apatya — children; kalatra — wife; ādiṣu — and in everything in relation to them; ātma — own; sainyeṣu — fighting soldiers; asatsu — fallible; api — in spite of; teṣām — of all of them; pramattaḥ — too attached; nidhanam — destruction; paśyan — having been experienced; api — although; na — does not; paśyati — see it.
Śrīdhara — Word-for-word
nanu — but then; naśvara-kuṭumba-ādi-artham — for a perishable family and the like; katham āyur-vyayam kuryāt — why would one spend one’s life?; deha-ādiṣu — in the body and the rest; ātmanaḥ sainyeṣu parikareṣu — in one’s forces and attendants; asatsu mithyā-bhūteṣu api — although impermanent and unreal; pramattaḥ prasaktaḥ — heedless and attached; teṣām nāśam paśyan api — though seeing their destruction; pitṛ-ādi-dṛṣṭāntena — by examples such as one’s forefathers; na anusandhatte — does not reflect upon it.
Transliteration
Bhāgavatam — Transliteration
dehāpatya-kalatrādiṣv
ātma-sainyeṣv asatsv api
teṣāṁ pramatto nidhanaṁ
paśyann api na paśyati
Śrīdhara — Transliteration
nanu naśvarakuṭumbādyarthaṁ katham āyurvyayaṁ kuryāt ? tatrāha | dehādiṣu ātmanaḥ sainyeṣu parikareṣv asatsu mithyābhūteṣv api pramattaḥ prasaktas teṣāṁ pitrādidṛṣṭāntena nāśaṁ paśyann api nānusandhatte ||4||
Śrīdhara Sanskrit
ननु नश्वरकुटुम्बाद्यर्थं कथम् आयुर्व्ययं कुर्यात् ? तत्राह । देहादिषु आत्मनः सैन्येषु परिकरेष्व् असत्सु मिथ्याभूतेष्व् अपि प्रमत्तः प्रसक्तस् तेषां पित्रादिदृष्टान्तेन नाशं पश्यन्न् अपि नानुसन्धत्ते ॥४॥

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. One might ask: why would anyone spend life for a perishable family and the like? He answers that a heedless person remains attached to the body and related supports as though they were his own forces, even though they are impermanent and unreal. Though he sees their destruction in examples such as his forefathers, he does not reflect on it.

ŚB 2.1.5

तस्माद्भारत सर्वात्मा भगवान् ईश्वरो हरि: ।
श्रोतव्य: कीर्तितव्यश्च स्मर्तव्यश्चेच्छताभयम् ॥ ५ ॥

O descendant of King Bharata, one who desires to be free from all miseries must hear about, glorify and also remember the Personality of Godhead, who is the Supersoul, the controller and the savior from all miseries.

Word-for-word
Bhāgavatam — Word-for-word
tasmāt — for this reason; bhārata — O descendant of Bharata; sarva-ātmā — the Supersoul; bhagavān — the Supreme Personality of Godhead; īśvaraḥ — the controller; hariḥ — the Lord, who vanquishes all miseries; śrotavyaḥ — is to be heard; kīrtitavyaḥ — to be glorified; ca — also; smartavyaḥ — to be remembered; ca — and; icchatā — of one who desires; abhayam — freedom.
Śrīdhara — Word-for-word
evam — thus; viparyaya-praśna-uttaram uktvā — having answered the contrary question; śrotavyādi-praśnasya uttaram āha — he answers what should be heard and so on; he bhārata — O descendant of Bharata; sarvātmā — “Self of all,” indicating dearness; bhagavān — “Lord,” indicating beauty; īśvaraḥ — “controller,” indicating necessity; hariḥ — “Hari,” indicating the remover of bondage; abhayam mokṣam icchatā — by one desiring fearlessness, liberation.
Transliteration
Bhāgavatam — Transliteration
tasmād bhārata sarvātmā
bhagavān īśvaro hariḥ
śrotavyaḥ kīrtitavyaś ca
smartavyaś cecchatābhayam
Śrīdhara — Transliteration
evaṁ viparyayapraśnottaram uktvā, śrotavyādipraśnasyottaram āha—tasmād iti | he bhārata bharatavaṁśya | sarvātmeti preṣṭhatvam āha | bhagavān iti saundaryam | īśvara ity āvaśyakatvam | harir iti bandhahāritvam | abhayaṁ mokṣam icchatā ||5||
Śrīdhara Sanskrit
एवं विपर्ययप्रश्नोत्तरम् उक्त्वा, श्रोतव्यादिप्रश्नस्योत्तरम् आह—तस्माद् इति । हे भारत भरतवंश्य । सर्वात्मेति प्रेष्ठत्वम् आह । भगवान् इति सौन्दर्यम् । ईश्वर इत्य् आवश्यकत्वम् । हरिर् इति बन्धहारित्वम् । अभयं मोक्षम् इच्छता ॥५॥

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. Having answered the contrary question, he now answers what should be heard and so forth with tasmāt. “O Bhārata” means descendant of Bharata. Sarvātmā indicates supreme dearness, Bhagavān beauty, Īśvara indispensability, and Hari the remover of bondage. “Desiring fearlessness” means desiring liberation.

ŚB 2.1.6

एतावान् सांख्ययोगाभ्यां स्वधर्मपरिनिष्ठया ।
जन्मलाभ: पर: पुंसामन्ते नारायणस्मृति: ॥ ६ ॥

The highest perfection of human life, achieved either by complete knowledge of matter and spirit, by practice of mystic powers, or by perfect discharge of occupational duty, is to remember the Personality of Godhead at the end of life.

Word-for-word
Bhāgavatam — Word-for-word
etāvān — all these different kinds of processes; sāṅkhya — complete knowledge of matter and spirit; yogābhyām — knowledge of mystic power; sva-dharma — particular occupational duty; pariniṣṭhayā — by full perception; janma — birth; lābhaḥ — gain; paraḥ — the supreme; puṁsām — of a person; ante — at the end; nārāyaṇa — the Personality of Godhead; smṛtiḥ — remembrance.
Śrīdhara — Word-for-word
ataḥ param anyat śreyaḥ na asti — there is no higher good beyond this; janmanaḥ lābhaḥ phalam — the gain or fruit of birth; nārāyaṇa-smṛtiḥ — remembrance of Nārāyaṇa; sāṅkhyādibhiḥ sādhyaḥ — attained through Sāṅkhya and other disciplines; teṣām svātantryeṇa lābhatvam vārayati — those disciplines are not independently the final gain; sāṅkhyam — discrimination between self and non-self; yogaḥ aṣṭāṅgaḥ — eight-limbed yoga; ante smṛtiḥ — remembrance at the end; paraḥ lābhaḥ — the supreme gain.
Transliteration
Bhāgavatam — Transliteration
etāvān sāṅkhya-yogābhyāṁ
sva-dharma-pariniṣṭhayā
janma-lābhaḥ paraḥ puṁsām
ante nārāyaṇa-smṛtiḥ
Śrīdhara — Transliteration
ataḥ param anyac chreyo nāstīty āha—etāvān eva janmano lābhaḥ phalam | tam āha—nārāyaṇasmṛtir iti | sāṅkhyādibhiḥ sādhya iti teṣāṁ svātantryeṇa lābhatvaṁ vārayati | sāṅkhyam ātmānātmavivekaḥ | yogo’ṣṭāṅgaḥ | ante tu smṛtiḥ paro lābhaḥ, na tanmahimā vaktuṁ śakya ity arthaḥ ||6||
Śrīdhara Sanskrit
अतः परम् अन्यच् छ्रेयो नास्तीत्य् आह—एतावान् एव जन्मनो लाभः फलम् । तम् आह—नारायणस्मृतिर् इति । साङ्ख्यादिभिः साध्य इति तेषां स्वातन्त्र्येण लाभत्वं वारयति । साङ्ख्यम् आत्मानात्मविवेकः । योगोऽष्टाङ्गः । अन्ते तु स्मृतिः परो लाभः, न तन्महिमा वक्तुं शक्य इत्य् अर्थः ॥६॥

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. He says that there is no good higher than this: the true gain and fruit of birth is remembrance of Nārāyaṇa. By saying it is accomplished through Sāṅkhya and the other disciplines, he denies that those disciplines are independently the final gain. Sāṅkhya is discrimination between self and non-self; yoga is the eight-limbed path. Remembrance at the end is the supreme gain, whose greatness cannot adequately be stated.

ŚB 2.1.7

प्रायेण मुनयो राजन्निवृत्ता विधिषेधत: ।
नैर्गुण्यस्था रमन्ते स्म गुणानुकथने हरे: ॥ ७ ॥

O King Parīkṣit, mainly the topmost transcendentalists, who are above the regulative principles and restrictions, take pleasure in describing the glories of the Lord.

Word-for-word
Bhāgavatam — Word-for-word
prāyeṇa — mainly; munayaḥ — all sages; rājan — O King; nivṛttāḥ — above; vidhi — regulative principles; ṣedhataḥ — from negative injunctions; nairguṇya-sthāḥ — situated on the transcendental platform; ramante — take pleasure in; sma — distinctly; guṇa-anukathane — describing the glories of the Lord; hareḥ — of the Personality of Godhead.
Śrīdhara — Word-for-word
tatra — in this matter; sad-ācāram pramāṇayati — he cites the conduct of the good as authority; prāyeṇa iti — with the word “prāyeṇa”; vidhi-ṣedhataḥ — from prescriptions and prohibitions; nivṛttāḥ — withdrawn; nairguṇye brahmaṇi sthitāḥ api — even though established in transcendent Brahman; sma — indicates something well known.
Transliteration
Bhāgavatam — Transliteration
prāyeṇa munayo rājan
nivṛttā vidhi-ṣedhataḥ
nairguṇya-sthā ramante sma
guṇānukathane hareḥ
Śrīdhara — Transliteration
tatra sadācāraṁ pramāṇayati—prāyeṇeti | vidhiṣedhato vidhiniṣedhābhyāṁ nivṛttā nairguṇye brahmaṇi sthitā api | sma prasiddham ||7||
Śrīdhara Sanskrit
तत्र सदाचारं प्रमाणयति—प्रायेणेति । विधिषेधतो विधिनिषेधाभ्यां निवृत्ता नैर्गुण्ये ब्रह्मणि स्थिता अपि । स्म प्रसिद्धम् ॥७॥

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. Here he cites the conduct of the good as evidence. Those sages have withdrawn from prescriptions and prohibitions and are established in transcendence, in Brahman, yet they delight in Hari’s qualities. The particle sma indicates that this is well known.

ŚB 2.1.8

इदं भागवतं नाम पुराणं ब्रह्मसम्मितम् ।
अधीतवान् द्वापरादौ पितुर्द्वैपायनादहम् ॥ ८ ॥

At the end of the Dvāpara-yuga, I studied this great supplement of Vedic literature named Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam, which is equal to all the Vedas, from my father, Śrīla Dvaipāyana Vyāsadeva.

Word-for-word
Bhāgavatam — Word-for-word
idam — this; bhāgavatam — Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam; nāma — of the name; purāṇam — Vedic supplement; brahma-sammitam — approved as the essence of the Vedas; adhītavān — studied; dvāpara-ādau — at the end of Dvāpara-yuga; pituḥ — from my father; dvaipāyanāt — Dvaipāyana Vyāsadeva; aham — myself.
Śrīdhara — Word-for-word
kim idam apūrvam kathayasi — what unprecedented thing are you telling?; satyam apūrvam eva idam — indeed, this is extraordinary; bhagavat-proktam — spoken by the Lord; tat-nāma-eka-pradhānam — with His name as its chief subject; purāṇam — the Purāṇa; brahma-saṁmitam — equal to all the Vedas; brahma samyak mitam yena — alternatively, that by which Brahman is properly known; adhītavān — I studied; dvaipāyanāt pituḥ — from father Dvaipāyana; dvāpara-ante — at the end of Dvāpara.
Transliteration
Bhāgavatam — Transliteration
idaṁ bhāgavataṁ nāma
purāṇaṁ brahma-sammitam
adhītavān dvāparādau
pitur dvaipāyanād aham
Śrīdhara — Transliteration
kim idam apūrvaṁ kathayasi ? satyam apūrvam evedam ity āha | idaṁ bhagavatproktaṁ tannāmaikapradhānaṁ purāṇaṁ brahmasaṁmitaṁ sarvavedatulyam | yad vā, brahma samyak mitaṁ yena | kutas tvayā prāptam ata āha—adhītavān iti | dvaipāyanāt pituḥ | kadā ? dvāparādau dvāparaḥ ādir yasya kālasya tasmin dvāparānte ity arthaḥ | śantanusamakāle vyāsāvatāraprasiddheḥ ||8||
Śrīdhara Sanskrit
किम् इदम् अपूर्वं कथयसि ? सत्यम् अपूर्वम् एवेदम् इत्य् आह । इदं भगवत्प्रोक्तं तन्नामैकप्रधानं पुराणं ब्रह्मसंमितं सर्ववेदतुल्यम् । यद् वा, ब्रह्म सम्यक् मितं येन । कुतस् त्वया प्राप्तम् अत आह—अधीतवान् इति । द्वैपायनात् पितुः । कदा ? द्वापरादौ द्वापरः आदिर् यस्य कालस्य तस्मिन् द्वापरान्ते इत्य् अर्थः । शन्तनुसमकाले व्यासावतारप्रसिद्धेः ॥८॥

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. “What unprecedented thing are you speaking of?” Indeed, he says, this is extraordinary. This Purāṇa was spoken by the Lord, has His name as its principal subject, and is equal to all the Vedas. Alternatively, brahma-saṁmitam can mean that by which Brahman is properly known. He then explains how he received it: he studied it from his father Dvaipāyana, at the end of Dvāpara, consistent with the known time of Vyāsa’s appearance.

ŚB 2.1.9

परिनिष्ठितोऽपि नैर्गुण्य उत्तमश्लोकलीलया ।
गृहीतचेता राजर्षे आख्यानं यदधीतवान् ॥ ९ ॥

O saintly King, I was certainly situated perfectly in transcendence, yet I was still attracted by the delineation of the pastimes of the Lord, who is described by enlightened verses.

Word-for-word
Bhāgavatam — Word-for-word
pariniṣṭhitaḥ — fully realized; api — in spite of; nairguṇye — in transcendence; uttama — enlightened; śloka — verse; līlayā — by the pastimes; gṛhīta — being attracted; cetāḥ — attention; rāja-ṛṣe — O saintly King; ākhyānam — delineation; yat — that; adhītavān — I studied.
Śrīdhara — Word-for-word
siddhasya tava — for you, already perfected; kutaḥ adhyayane pravṛttiḥ — why engagement in study?; pariniṣṭhitaḥ api iti — he answers with “although fully established”; gṛhīta-cetāḥ — mind seized; ākṛṣṭa-cittaḥ — mind attracted.
Transliteration
Bhāgavatam — Transliteration
pariniṣṭhito ’pi nairguṇya
uttama-śloka-līlayā
gṛhīta-cetā rājarṣe
ākhyānaṁ yad adhītavān
Śrīdhara — Transliteration
siddhasya tava kuto’dhyayane pravṛttiḥ ? tatrāha—pariniṣṭhito’pīti | gṛhītacetā ākṛṣṭacittaḥ ||9||
Śrīdhara Sanskrit
सिद्धस्य तव कुतोऽध्ययने प्रवृत्तिः ? तत्राह—परिनिष्ठितोऽपीति । गृहीतचेता आकृष्टचित्तः ॥९॥

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. “If you were already perfected, why did you take up study?” He answers with the words “although fully established.” Gṛhīta-cetāḥ means that his mind had been attracted.

ŚB 2.1.10

तदहं तेऽभिधास्यामि महापौरुषिको भवान् ।
यस्य श्रद्दधतामाशु स्यान्मुकुन्दे मति: सती ॥ १० ॥

That very Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam I shall recite before you because you are the most sincere devotee of Lord Kṛṣṇa. One who gives full attention and respect to hearing Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam achieves unflinching faith in the Supreme Lord, the giver of salvation.

Word-for-word
Bhāgavatam — Word-for-word
tat — that; aham — I; te — unto you; abhidhāsyāmi — shall recite; mahā-pauruṣikaḥ — the most sincere devotee of Lord Kṛṣṇa; bhavān — your good self; yasya — of which; śraddadhatām — of one who gives full respect and attention; āśu — very soon; syāt — it so becomes; mukunde — unto the Lord, who awards salvation; matiḥ — faith; satī — unflinching.
Śrīdhara — Word-for-word
Śrīdhara Svāmī gives no separate word-gloss for 2.1.10 in the Bhāvārthadīpikā; the source groups 2.1.9–10, but his gloss itself is explicitly marked for verse 9.
Transliteration
Bhāgavatam — Transliteration
tad ahaṁ te ’bhidhāsyāmi
mahā-pauruṣiko bhavān
yasya śraddadhatām āśu
syān mukunde matiḥ satī
Śrīdhara — Transliteration
No separate Śrīdhara gloss is given for 2.1.10 in the Bhāvārthadīpikā source used here.
Śrīdhara Sanskrit
No separate Śrīdhara gloss is given for 2.1.10 in the Bhāvārthadīpikā source used here.

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. Śrīdhara Svāmī gives no separate explanatory sentence on verse 2.1.10; under the combined 2.1.9–10 heading his own gloss is explicitly attached to verse 9.

ŚB 2.1.11

एतन्निर्विद्यमानानामिच्छतामकुतोभयम् ।
योगिनां नृप निर्णीतं हरेर्नामानुकीर्तनम् ॥ ११ ॥

O King, constant chanting of the holy name of the Lord after the ways of the great authorities is the doubtless and fearless way of success for all, including those who are free from all material desires, those who are desirous of all material enjoyment, and also those who are self-satisfied by dint of transcendental knowledge.

Word-for-word
Bhāgavatam — Word-for-word
etat — it is; nirvidyamānānām — of those who are completely free from all material desires; icchatām — of those who are desirous of all sorts of material enjoyment; akutaḥ-bhayam — free from all doubts and fear; yoginām — of all who are self-satisfied; nṛpa — O King; nirṇītam — decided truth; hareḥ — of the Lord, Śrī Kṛṣṇa; nāma — holy name; anu — after someone, always; kīrtanam — chanting.
Śrīdhara — Word-for-word
sādhakānām siddhānām ca — for practitioners and perfected persons; na ataḥ param anyat śreyaḥ asti — there is no higher good than this; icchatām kāminām — for those with desires; tat-tat-phala-sādhanam — the means to their respective results; etat eva — this alone; nirvidyamānānām mumukṣūṇām — for the detached who seek liberation; mokṣa-sādhanam — means to liberation; yoginām jñāninām — for yogīs and knowers; phalam — the fruit; nirṇītam — concluded.
Transliteration
Bhāgavatam — Transliteration
etan nirvidyamānānām
icchatām akuto-bhayam
yogināṁ nṛpa nirṇītaṁ
harer nāmānukīrtanam
Śrīdhara — Transliteration
sādhakānāṁ siddhānāṁ ca nātaḥ param anyac chreyo’stīty āha—etad iti | icchatāṁ kāmināṁ tattatphalasādhanam etad eva | nirvidyamānānāṁ mumukṣūṇāṁ mokṣasādhanam etad eva | yogināṁ jñānināṁ phalaṁ caitad eva nirṇītam | nātra pramāṇaṁ vaktavyam ity arthaḥ ||11||
Śrīdhara Sanskrit
साधकानां सिद्धानां च नातः परम् अन्यच् छ्रेयोऽस्तीत्य् आह—एतद् इति । इच्छतां कामिनां तत्तत्फलसाधनम् एतद् एव । निर्विद्यमानानां मुमुक्षूणां मोक्षसाधनम् एतद् एव । योगिनां ज्ञानिनां फलं चैतद् एव निर्णीतम् । नात्र प्रमाणं वक्तव्यम् इत्य् अर्थः ॥११॥

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. He says that for practitioners and perfected persons alike there is no higher good than this. For people with desires, it is the means to their respective goals; for the detached who seek liberation, it is the means to liberation; and for yogīs and knowers, it is itself the fruit. This has been settled, so no further proof need be stated.

ŚB 2.1.12

किं प्रमत्तस्य बहुभि: परोक्षैर्हायनैरिह ।
वरं मुहूर्तं विदितं घटते श्रेयसे यत: ॥ १२ ॥

What is the value of a prolonged life which is wasted, inexperienced by years in this world? Better a moment of full consciousness, because that gives one a start in searching after his supreme interest.

Word-for-word
Bhāgavatam — Word-for-word
kim — what is; pramattasya — of the bewildered; bahubhiḥ — by many; parokṣaiḥ — inexperienced; hāyanaiḥ — years; iha — in this world; varam — better; muhūrtam — a moment; viditam — conscious; ghaṭate — one can try for; śreyase — in the matter of the supreme interest; yataḥ — by that.
Śrīdhara — Word-for-word
alpam eva āyuḥ avaśiṣṭam — only a little life remains; kim ahaṁ sādhayeyam — what can I accomplish?; mā śucaḥ — do not grieve; kim iti tribhiḥ — through three verses beginning “kim”; parokṣaiḥ alakṣitaiḥ hāyanaiḥ varṣaiḥ — unnoticed years; viditam — consciously known; vṛthā yāti iti jñātam — known not to pass uselessly; yataḥ yena jñānena — because through that awareness; ghaṭeta — one should strive; yatnam kuryāt — should make effort.
Transliteration
Bhāgavatam — Transliteration
kiṁ pramattasya bahubhiḥ
parokṣair hāyanair iha
varaṁ muhūrtaṁ viditaṁ
ghaṭate śreyase yataḥ
Śrīdhara — Transliteration
alpam evāyur avaśiṣṭaṁ kim ahaṁ sādhayeyam ? iti mā śuca ity āha—kim iti tribhiḥ | parokṣair alakṣitair hāyanair varṣaiḥ | viditaṁ vṛthā yātīti jñātam | yato yena jñānena | ghaṭeta yatnaṁ kuryāt ||12||
Śrīdhara Sanskrit
अल्पम् एवायुर् अवशिष्टं किम् अहं साधयेयम् ? इति मा शुच इत्य् आह—किम् इति त्रिभिः । परोक्षैर् अलक्षितैर् हायनैर् वर्षैः । विदितं वृथा यातीति ज्ञातम् । यतो येन ज्ञानेन । घटेत यत्नं कुर्यात् ॥१२॥

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. “Only a little life remains—what can I accomplish?” He says not to grieve, through the three verses beginning with kim. The years are “unseen” because they pass unnoticed. A consciously known moment is known not to pass uselessly, because with that awareness one can make deliberate effort.

ŚB 2.1.13

खट्‍वाङ्गो नाम राजर्षिर्ज्ञात्वेयत्तामिहायुष: ।
मुहूर्तात्सर्वमुत्सृज्य गतवानभयं हरिम् ॥ १३ ॥

The saintly King Khaṭvāṅga, after being informed that the duration of his life would be only a moment more, at once freed himself from all material activities and took shelter of the supreme safety, the Personality of Godhead.

Word-for-word
Bhāgavatam — Word-for-word
khaṭvāṅgaḥ — King Khaṭvāṅga; nāma — name; rāja-ṛṣiḥ — saintly king; jñātvā — by knowing; iyattām — duration; iha — in this world; āyuṣaḥ — of one’s life; muhūrtāt — within only a moment; sarvam — everything; utsṛjya — leaving aside; gatavān — had undergone; abhayam — fully safe; harim — the Personality of Godhead.
Śrīdhara — Word-for-word
khaṭvāṅgaḥ — Khaṭvāṅga; deva-pakṣe sthitvā — siding with the gods; daityān ajayat — defeated the Daityas; prasannaiḥ devaiḥ — by the pleased gods; varam vṛṇīṣva — “choose a boon”; mama āyuḥ kathyatām — “tell me my lifespan”; muhūrta-mātram asti — only a moment remains; vimānena bhuvam āgatya — returning to earth by celestial vehicle; harim śaraṇam gataḥ — took refuge in Hari; svarga-bhūmiḥ rajaḥ-adhikā — heaven is more dominated by rajas; karma-bhūmiḥ pṛthvī — earth is the land of action.
Transliteration
Bhāgavatam — Transliteration
khaṭvāṅgo nāma rājarṣir
jñātveyattām ihāyuṣaḥ
muhūrtāt sarvam utsṛjya
gatavān abhayaṁ harim
Śrīdhara — Transliteration
khaṭvāṅgo hi devapakṣe sthitvā daityān ajayat | tataḥ prasannair devair varaṁ vṛṇīṣva ity ukte tenoktaṁ prathamaṁ tāvan mamāyuḥ kathyatām iti | tato devair uktaṁ tat tu muhūrtamātram astīti | tato’tiśīghraṁ vimānena bhuvam āgatya hariṁ śaraṇaṁ gata iti | yata iyaṁ svargabhūmī rajo’dhikā | karmabhūmiḥ pṛthvī ||13||
Śrīdhara Sanskrit
खट्वाङ्गो हि देवपक्षे स्थित्वा दैत्यान् अजयत् । ततः प्रसन्नैर् देवैर् वरं वृणीष्व इत्य् उक्ते तेनोक्तं प्रथमं तावन् ममायुः कथ्यताम् इति । ततो देवैर् उक्तं तत् तु मुहूर्तमात्रम् अस्तीति । ततोऽतिशीघ्रं विमानेन भुवम् आगत्य हरिं शरणं गत इति । यत इयं स्वर्गभूमी रजोऽधिका । कर्मभूमिः पृथ्वी ॥१३॥

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. Khaṭvāṅga fought on the gods’ side and defeated the Daityas. When the pleased gods offered him a boon, he first asked how much life remained. They told him only a moment. He immediately returned to earth by celestial vehicle and took refuge in Hari. Heaven is more dominated by passion, whereas earth is the field of action.

ŚB 2.1.14

तवाप्येतर्हि कौरव्य सप्ताहं जीवितावधि: ।
उपकल्पय तत्सर्वं तावद्यत्साम्परायिकम् ॥ १४ ॥

Mahārāja Parīkṣit, now your duration of life is limited to seven more days, so during this time you can perform all those rituals which are needed for the best purpose of your next life.

Word-for-word
Bhāgavatam — Word-for-word
tava — your; api — also; etarhi — therefore; kauravya — O one born in the family of Kuru; saptāham — seven days; jīvita — duration of life; avadhiḥ — up to the limit of; upakalpaya — get them performed; tat — those; sarvam — all; tāvat — so long; yat — which are; sāmparāyikam — rituals for the next life.
Śrīdhara — Word-for-word
tava tu — as for you; etarhi api idānīm api — even now; tāvat iti — “within that much”; tāvatā kālena — in that amount of time; sāmparāyikam — pertaining to what is beyond this life; pāralaukikam sādhanam — the means for the next world; sampādaya — accomplish.
Transliteration
Bhāgavatam — Transliteration
tavāpy etarhi kauravya
saptāhaṁ jīvitāvadhiḥ
upakalpaya tat sarvaṁ
tāvad yat sāmparāyikam
Śrīdhara — Transliteration
tava tu etarhy apīdānīm api | tāvad iti tāvatā kālena sāmparāyikaṁ pāralaukikaṁ sādhanaṁ sampādaya ||14||
Śrīdhara Sanskrit
तव तु एतर्ह्य् अपीदानीम् अपि । तावद् इति तावता कालेन साम्परायिकं पारलौकिकं साधनं सम्पादय ॥१४॥

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. As for you, even now—at this very time. Tāvat means: within that amount of time, accomplish the means pertaining to the next world.

ŚB 2.1.15

अन्तकाले तु पुरुष आगते गतसाध्वस: ।
छिन्द्यादसङ्गशस्त्रेण स्पृहां देहेऽनु ये च तम् ॥ १५ ॥

At the last stage of one’s life, one should be bold enough not to be afraid of death. But one must cut off all attachment to the material body and everything pertaining to it and all desires thereof.

Word-for-word
Bhāgavatam — Word-for-word
anta-kāle — at the last stage of life; tu — but; puruṣaḥ — a person; āgate — having arrived; gata-sādhvasaḥ — without any fear of death; chindyāt — must cut off; asaṅga — nonattachment; śastreṇa — by the weapon of; spṛhām — all desires; dehe — in the matter of the material tabernacle; anu — pertaining; ye — all that; ca — also; tam — them.
Śrīdhara — Word-for-word
vairāgyam tāvat āha — first he teaches detachment; anta-kāle iti — with the verse beginning “anta-kāle”; gata-sādhvasaḥ — fear gone; mṛtyu-bhaya-śūnyaḥ — free of fear of death; asaṅgaḥ nāma anāsaktiḥ — nonattachment means absence of attachment; tena śastreṇa — with that weapon; spṛhām sukha-icchām — desire for pleasure; deham anu ye putra-kalatra-ādayaḥ — children, spouse and others connected with the body; teṣu api spṛhām chindyāt — should cut desire toward them also.
Transliteration
Bhāgavatam — Transliteration
anta-kāle tu puruṣa
āgate gata-sādhvasaḥ
chindyād asaṅga-śastreṇa
spṛhāṁ dehe ’nu ye ca tam
Śrīdhara — Transliteration
tatra vairāgyaṁ tāvad āha—antakāla iti | gatasādhvaso mṛtyu bhayaśūnyaḥ | asaṅgo nāma anāsaktis tena śastreṇa spṛhāṁ sukhecchāṁ, taṁ deham anu ye putrakalatrādayas teṣv api spṛhāṁ chindyāt ||15||
Śrīdhara Sanskrit
तत्र वैराग्यं तावद् आह—अन्तकाल इति । गतसाध्वसो मृत्यु भयशून्यः । असङ्गो नाम अनासक्तिस् तेन शस्त्रेण स्पृहां सुखेच्छां, तं देहम् अनु ये पुत्रकलत्रादयस् तेष्व् अपि स्पृहां छिन्द्यात् ॥१५॥

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. He first teaches detachment with the verse beginning anta-kāle. Gata-sādhvasaḥ means free from fear of death. Asaṅga means nonattachment; with that as a weapon one should cut desire for bodily pleasure and also desire for children, spouse and others connected with the body.

ŚB 2.1.16

गृहात् प्रव्रजितो धीर: पुण्यतीर्थजलाप्लुत: ।
शुचौ विविक्त आसीनो विधिवत्कल्पितासने ॥ १६ ॥

One should leave home and practice self-control. In a sacred place he should bathe regularly and sit down in a lonely place duly sanctified.

Word-for-word
Bhāgavatam — Word-for-word
gṛhāt — from one’s home; pravrajitaḥ — having gone out; dhīraḥ — self-controlled; puṇya — pious; tīrtha — sacred place; jala-āplutaḥ — fully washed; śucau — cleansed; vivikte — solitary; āsīnaḥ — seated; vidhivat — according to regulations; kalpita — having done; āsane — on a sitting place.
Śrīdhara — Word-for-word
gṛhāt pravrajitaḥ nirgataḥ — gone forth from the house; gṛhe sthitasya punaḥ api āsakti-sambhavāt — because attachment can arise again while staying at home; aṣṭāṅga-yogam āha — he teaches eight-limbed yoga; dhīraḥ — implies restraints such as celibacy; puṇya-tīrtha — implies observances such as bathing; śucau — in a clean place; vivikte — in solitude; vidhivat — properly; kuśa-ajina-cailaiḥ krameṇa nirmitam — made in order from kuśa grass, skin and cloth.
Transliteration
Bhāgavatam — Transliteration
gṛhāt pravrajito dhīraḥ
puṇya-tīrtha-jalāplutaḥ
śucau vivikta āsīno
vidhivat kalpitāsane
Śrīdhara — Transliteration
kiṁ ca gṛhāt pravrajito nirgataḥ | gṛhe sthitasya punar apy āsaktisambhavāt | tatrāṣṭāṅgayogam āha | dhīra iti brahmacaryādiyamopalakṣaṇam | puṇyatīrtheti snānādiniyamopalakṣaṇam | āsanam āha—śucāv iti | vivikte ekānte | vidhivat kuśājinacailaiḥ krameṇa nirmite ||16||
Śrīdhara Sanskrit
किं च गृहात् प्रव्रजितो निर्गतः । गृहे स्थितस्य पुनर् अप्य् आसक्तिसम्भवात् । तत्राष्टाङ्गयोगम् आह । धीर इति ब्रह्मचर्यादियमोपलक्षणम् । पुण्यतीर्थेति स्नानादिनियमोपलक्षणम् । आसनम् आह—शुचाव् इति । विविक्ते एकान्ते । विधिवत् कुशाजिनचैलैः क्रमेण निर्मिते ॥१६॥

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. He should go out from the house, because attachment can arise again while remaining there. Śrīdhara now begins the eight-limbed yoga. “Steady” implies restraints such as celibacy; bathing at a holy place indicates observances. The seat is in a clean, solitary place, properly arranged in layers of kuśa grass, skin and cloth.

ŚB 2.1.17

अभ्यसेन्मनसा शुद्धं त्रिवृद्ब्रह्माक्षरं परम् ।
मनो यच्छेज्जितश्वासो ब्रह्मबीजमविस्मरन् ॥ १७ ॥

After sitting in the above manner, make the mind remember the three transcendental letters [a-u-m], and by regulating the breathing process, control the mind so as not to forget the transcendental seed.

Word-for-word
Bhāgavatam — Word-for-word
abhyaset — one should practice; manasā — by the mind; śuddham — sacred; tri-vṛt — composed of the three; brahma-akṣaram — transcendental letters; param — the supreme; manaḥ — mind; yacchet — get under control; jita-śvāsaḥ — by regulating the breathing air; brahma — absolute; bījam — seed; avismaran — without being forgotten.
Śrīdhara — Word-for-word
japa-garbham prāṇāyāmam — breath-control containing japa; japyam āha — he states what is to be repeated; tri-vṛt brahma-akṣaram — the threefold sacred syllable; akāra-ādibhiḥ — with A and the other components; praṇavam — Oṁ; manasā abhyaset āvartayet — mentally practice and repeat; prāṇāyāmena mano-niyamanam — restraining the mind by breath control; mano yacchet vaśīkuryāt — bring the mind under control; brahma-bījam praṇavam — the seed of Brahman, Oṁ; avismaran — without forgetting; jita-śvāsaḥ — with breath conquered.
Transliteration
Bhāgavatam — Transliteration
abhyasen manasā śuddhaṁ
trivṛd-brahmākṣaraṁ param
mano yacchej jita-śvāso
brahma-bījam avismaran
Śrīdhara — Transliteration
japagarbhaṁ prāṇāyāmaṁ vaktuṁ japyam āha | trivṛdbrahmākṣaraṁ trivṛt tribhir akārādibhir vartitaṁ grathitaṁ brahmākṣaraṁ praṇavaṁ manasā’bhyased āvartayet | manasendriyapratyāhāraṁ vaktuṁ prāṇāyāmena manoniyamanam āha | mano yacched vaśīkuryāt | brahmabījaṁ praṇavam avismarann eva jitaśvāsaḥ san ||17||
Śrīdhara Sanskrit
जपगर्भं प्राणायामं वक्तुं जप्यम् आह । त्रिवृद्ब्रह्माक्षरं त्रिवृत् त्रिभिर् अकारादिभिर् वर्तितं ग्रथितं ब्रह्माक्षरं प्रणवं मनसाऽभ्यसेद् आवर्तयेत् । मनसेन्द्रियप्रत्याहारं वक्तुं प्राणायामेन मनोनियमनम् आह । मनो यच्छेद् वशीकुर्यात् । ब्रह्मबीजं प्रणवम् अविस्मरन्न् एव जितश्वासः सन् ॥१७॥

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. To explain breath-control containing japa, he first states the syllable to be repeated. The threefold sacred syllable is Oṁ, composed of A and the other elements; one should mentally repeat it. To prepare for withdrawal of mind and senses, he teaches restraint of the mind through breath control: bring the mind under control while mastering the breath and never forgetting Oṁ, the seed-syllable of Brahman.

ŚB 2.1.18

नियच्छेद्विषयेभ्योऽक्षान्मनसा बुद्धिसारथि: ।
मन: कर्मभिराक्षिप्तं शुभार्थे धारयेद्धिया ॥ १८ ॥

Gradually, as the mind becomes progressively spiritualized, withdraw it from sense activities, and by intelligence the senses will be controlled. The mind too absorbed in material activities can be engaged in the service of the Personality of Godhead and become fixed in full transcendental consciousness.

Word-for-word
Bhāgavatam — Word-for-word
niyacchet — withdraw; viṣayebhyaḥ — from sense engagements; akṣān — the senses; manasā — by dint of the mind; buddhi — intelligence; sārathiḥ — driver; manaḥ — the mind; karmabhiḥ — by the fruitive work; ākṣiptam — being absorbed in; śubha-arthe — for the sake of the Lord; dhārayet — hold up; dhiyā — in full consciousness.
Śrīdhara — Word-for-word
pratyāhāram āha — he teaches withdrawal of the senses; niyacchet nigṛhṇīyāt — should restrain, hold back; akṣān indriyāṇi — the senses; niścaya-ātmikā buddhiḥ sārathiḥ — decisive intelligence is the charioteer; dhāraṇām āha — he teaches concentration; manaḥ — the mind; karmabhiḥ tat-vāsanābhiḥ — by karmic impressions; ākṣiptam ākṛṣṭam — dragged; śubha-arthe — toward the auspicious object; bhagavad-rūpe — the form of the Lord.
Transliteration
Bhāgavatam — Transliteration
niyacched viṣayebhyo ’kṣān
manasā buddhi-sārathiḥ
manaḥ karmabhir ākṣiptaṁ
śubhārthe dhārayed dhiyā
Śrīdhara — Transliteration
pratyāhāram āha | niyacchen nigṛhṇīyāt | akṣān indriyāṇi | niścayātmikā buddhiḥ sārathir yasya saḥ | dhāraṇām āha—mana iti | punaś ca karmabhis tadvāsanābhir ākṣiptam ākṛṣṭam | śubhārthe bhagavadrūpe ||18||
Śrīdhara Sanskrit
प्रत्याहारम् आह । नियच्छेन् निगृह्णीयात् । अक्षान् इन्द्रियाणि । निश्चयात्मिका बुद्धिः सारथिर् यस्य सः । धारणाम् आह—मन इति । पुनश् च कर्मभिस् तद्वासनाभिर् आक्षिप्तम् आकृष्टम् । शुभार्थे भगवद्रूपे ॥१८॥

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. He teaches withdrawal of the senses: niyacchet means to restrain, and akṣān means the senses. The charioteer is decisive intelligence. Then he teaches concentration: the mind, repeatedly dragged by karmic impressions, should be held on the auspicious object—the form of the Lord.

ŚB 2.1.19

तत्रैकावयवं ध्यायेदव्युच्छिन्नेन चेतसा ।
मनो निर्विषयं युक्त्वा तत: किञ्चन न स्मरेत् ।
पदं तत्परमं विष्णोर्मनो यत्र प्रसीदति ॥ १९ ॥

Thereafter, you should meditate upon the limbs of Viṣṇu, one after another, without being deviated from the conception of the complete body. Thus the mind becomes free from all sense objects. There should be no other thing to be thought upon. Because the Supreme Personality of Godhead, Viṣṇu, is the Ultimate Truth, the mind becomes completely reconciled in Him only.

Word-for-word
Bhāgavatam — Word-for-word
tatra — thereafter; eka — one by one; avayavam — limbs of the body; dhyāyet — should be concentrated upon; avyucchinnena — without being deviated from the complete form; cetasā — by the mind; manaḥ — mind; nirviṣayam — without being contaminated by sense objects; yuktvā — being dovetailed; tataḥ — after that; kiñcana — anything; na — do not; smaret — think of; padam — personality; tat — that; paramam — Supreme; viṣṇoḥ — of Viṣṇu; manaḥ — the mind; yatra — whereupon; prasīdati — becomes reconciled.
Śrīdhara — Word-for-word
dhyānam āha — he teaches meditation; ekam ekam pāda-ādi-avayavam — each limb beginning with the feet; avyucchinnena — not separated; samagra-rūpāt aviyuktena — not disconnected from the whole form; citta-sthirīkaraṇam dhāraṇā — stabilizing the mind is concentration; avayava-vibhāvanayā tat-dārḍhyam dhyānam — strengthening it by contemplation of the limbs is meditation; samādhim āha — he teaches samādhi; nirviṣayam manaḥ yuktvā — fixing the mind free from objects; paramānanda-mātra-ākāram — taking only the form of supreme bliss; prasīdati upaśāmyati — becomes serene and subsides.
Transliteration
Bhāgavatam — Transliteration
tatraikāvayavaṁ dhyāyed
avyucchinnena cetasā
mano nirviṣayaṁ yuktvā
tataḥ kiñcana na smaret
padaṁ tat paramaṁ viṣṇor
mano yatra prasīdati
Śrīdhara — Transliteration
dhyānam āha—tatra iti | ekam ekaṁ pādādyavayavam | avyucchinnena samagrarūpād aviyuktena | āśrayaviśeṣeṇa sāmānyataś cittasthirīkaraṇaṁ dhāraṇā | avayavavibhāvanayā taddārḍhyaṁ dhyānam iti bhedaḥ | samādhim āha | nirviṣayaṁ mano yuktvā samādhāya | sthirībhūte manasi sphuratparamānandamātrākāraṁ kṛtvety arthaḥ | prasīdati upaśāmyati ||19||
Śrīdhara Sanskrit
ध्यानम् आह—तत्र इति । एकम् एकं पादाद्यवयवम् । अव्युच्छिन्नेन समग्ररूपाद् अवियुक्तेन । आश्रयविशेषेण सामान्यतश् चित्तस्थिरीकरणं धारणा । अवयवविभावनया तद्दार्ढ्यं ध्यानम् इति भेदः । समाधिम् आह । निर्विषयं मनो युक्त्वा समाधाय । स्थिरीभूते मनसि स्फुरत्परमानन्दमात्राकारं कृत्वेत्य् अर्थः । प्रसीदति उपशाम्यति ॥१९॥

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. He now teaches meditation: contemplate each limb, beginning with the feet, without separating it from the complete form. Concentration is stabilizing the mind generally upon a particular support; meditation strengthens that stability through vivid contemplation of the limbs. He then teaches samādhi: fix the mind free of sense objects so that, once steady, it takes the form only of manifest supreme bliss. Prasīdati means that the mind becomes serene and subsides.

ŚB 2.1.20

रजस्तमोभ्यामाक्षिप्तं विमूढं मन आत्मन: ।
यच्छेद्धारणया धीरो हन्ति या तत्कृतं मलम् ॥ २० ॥

One’s mind is always agitated by the passionate mode of material nature and bewildered by the ignorant mode of nature. But one can rectify such conceptions by the relation of Viṣṇu and thus become pacified by cleansing the dirty things created by them.

Word-for-word
Bhāgavatam — Word-for-word
rajaḥ — the passionate mode of nature; tamobhyām — as well as by the ignorant mode of material nature; ākṣiptam — agitated; vimūḍham — bewildered; manaḥ — the mind; ātmanaḥ — of one’s own; yacchet — get it rectified; dhāraṇayā — by conception (of Viṣṇu); dhīraḥ — the pacified; hanti — destroys; yā — all those; tat-kṛtam — done by them; malam — dirty things.
Śrīdhara — Word-for-word
guṇa-vaśāt — under the power of the modes; punaḥ api kṣobhe sati — if agitation again occurs; dhāraṇām eva sthirīkuryāt — one should stabilize concentration; rajasā ākṣiptam — agitated by passion; tamasā vimūḍham — bewildered by ignorance; svīyam manaḥ nirundhyāt — restrain one’s own mind; tat-kṛtam — produced by them; rajas-tamobhyām kṛtam — produced by passion and ignorance.
Transliteration
Bhāgavatam — Transliteration
rajas-tamobhyām ākṣiptaṁ
vimūḍhaṁ mana ātmanaḥ
yacched dhāraṇayā dhīro
hanti yā tat-kṛtaṁ malam
Śrīdhara — Transliteration
guṇavaśāt punar api kṣobhe sati dhāraṇām eva sthirīkuryād ity āha | rajasā ākṣiptaṁ tamasā vimūḍhaṁ svīyaṁ mano nirundhyāt | tatkṛtaṁ rajastamobhyāṁ kṛtam ||20||
Śrīdhara Sanskrit
गुणवशात् पुनर् अपि क्षोभे सति धारणाम् एव स्थिरीकुर्याद् इत्य् आह । रजसा आक्षिप्तं तमसा विमूढं स्वीयं मनो निरुन्ध्यात् । तत्कृतं रजस्तमोभ्यां कृतम् ॥२०॥

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. If agitation returns under the power of the modes, one should stabilize concentration again. One should restrain one’s own mind when it is agitated by passion and bewildered by ignorance. “Produced by them” means produced by passion and ignorance.

ŚB 2.1.21

यस्यां सन्धार्यमाणायां योगिनो भक्तिलक्षण: ।
आशु सम्पद्यते योग आश्रयं भद्रमीक्षत: ॥ २१ ॥

O King, by this system of remembrance and by being fixed in the habit of seeing the all-good personal conception of the Lord, one can very soon attain devotional service to the Lord, under His direct shelter.

Śrīdhara — Word-for-word
yataḥ — because; yasyām — in which; kriyamāṇāyām — while being practiced; bhadram — auspicious, pleasant; sukha-ātmakam — of the nature of happiness; āśrayam — the support, object; viṣayam — object; paśyataḥ — of one seeing; tatra eva — precisely there; prītiḥ — affection, delight; bhavati — arises.
Transliteration

yasyāṁ sandhāryamāṇāyāṁ

yato yasyāṁ kriyamāṇāyāṁ bhadraṁ sukhātmakam āśrayaṁ viṣayaṁ paśyatas tatraiva prītir bhavati ||21||
Śrīdhara Sanskrit
यतो यस्यां क्रियमाणायां भद्रं सुखात्मकम् आश्रयं विषयं पश्यतस् तत्रैव प्रीतिर् भवति ॥२१॥

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. Because, when that concentration is practiced, the yogī sees its auspicious object, which is of the nature of happiness, and affection arises precisely for that object.

ŚB 2.1.22

राजोवाच
यथा सन्धार्यते ब्रह्मन् धारणा यत्र सम्मता ।
याद‍ृशी वा हरेदाशु पुरुषस्य मनोमलम् ॥ २२ ॥

The fortunate King Parīkṣit, inquiring further, said: O brāhmaṇa, please describe in full detail how and where the mind has to be applied and how the conception can be fixed so that the dirty things in a person’s mind can be removed.

Word-for-word
Bhāgavatam — Word-for-word
rājā uvāca — the fortunate King said; yathā — as it is; sandhāryate — the conception is made; brahman — O brāhmaṇa; dhāraṇā — conception; yatra — where and how; sammatā — in a summary; yādṛśī — the way by which; vā — or; haret — extricated; āśu — without delay; puruṣasya — of a person; manaḥ — of the mind; malam — dirty things.

yathā — how; yatra — where, in what; yādṛśī — of what kind; ca — and; itikartavyatā — the manner in which it is to be done; viṣaya — object, subject; tat-tad-viśeṣāṇām — of those respective particulars; praśnāḥ — the questions.
Transliteration
Bhāgavatam — Transliteration

Śrīdhara — Transliteration

Śrīdhara Sanskrit
यथा यत्र यादृशी चेतिकर्तव्यताविषयतत्तद्विशेषाणां प्रश्नाः ॥२२॥

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. “How,” “where,” and “of what kind” are questions concerning the respective particulars of its procedure and object.

ŚB 2.1.23

श्रीशुक उवाच
जितासनो जितश्वासो जितसङ्गो जितेन्द्रिय: ।
स्थूले भगवतो रूपे मन: सन्धारयेद्धिया ॥ २३ ॥

Śukadeva Gosvāmī answered: One should control the sitting posture, regulate the breathing process by the yogic prāṇāyāma and thus control the mind and senses and with intelligence apply the mind to the gross potencies of the Lord [called the virāṭ-rūpa].

Word-for-word
Bhāgavatam — Word-for-word
śrī-śukaḥ uvāca — Śukadeva Gosvāmī said; jita-āsanaḥ — controlled sitting posture; jita-śvāsaḥ — controlled breathing process; jita-saṅgaḥ — controlled association; jita-indriyaḥ — controlled senses; sthūle — in the gross matter; bhagavataḥ — unto the Personality of Godhead; rūpe — in the feature of; manaḥ — the mind; sandhārayet — must apply; dhiyā — by intelligence.
Śrīdhara — Word-for-word
yathā iti — to the question “how”; asya uttaram — its answer; jita-āsanaḥ iti — with the verse beginning “jita-āsanaḥ”; viṣayam — the object; āha — he states; sthūle iti — with “sthūle”; sārdhena — in one and a half verses.
Transliteration
Bhāgavatam — Transliteration
śrī-śuka uvāca
jitāsano jita-śvāso
jita-saṅgo jitendriyaḥ
sthūle bhagavato rūpe
manaḥ sandhārayed dhiyā
Śrīdhara — Transliteration
yathety asyottaraṁ—jitāsana iti | viṣayam āha—sthūla iti sārdhena ||23||
Śrīdhara Sanskrit
यथेत्य् अस्योत्तरं—जितासन इति । विषयम् आह—स्थूल इति सार्धेन ॥२३॥

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. He answers the question “how?” with the verse beginning jita-āsanaḥ. He then states the object of concentration with sthūle, extending through one and a half verses.

ŚB 2.1.24

विशेषस्तस्य देहोऽयं स्थविष्ठश्च स्थवीयसाम् ।
यत्रेदं व्यज्यते विश्वं भूतं भव्यं भवच्च सत् ॥ २४ ॥

This gigantic manifestation of the phenomenal material world as a whole is the personal body of the Absolute Truth, wherein the universal resultant past, present and future of material time is experienced.

Word-for-word
Bhāgavatam — Word-for-word
viśeṣaḥ — personal; tasya — His; dehaḥ — body; ayam — this; sthaviṣṭhaḥ — grossly material; ca — and; sthavīyasām — of all matter; yatra — wherein; idam — all these phenomena; vyajyate — is experienced; viśvam — universe; bhūtam — past; bhavyam — future; bhavat — present; ca — and; sat — resultant.
Śrīdhara — Word-for-word
viśeṣaḥ — the particular manifestation; virāṭ-dehaḥ — the Virāṭ body; ati-sthūlānām api — even among the very gross; sthūla-taraḥ — grosser, more massive; sat — existent; kārya-mātram — the totality of effects, manifested products.
Transliteration
Bhāgavatam — Transliteration
viśeṣas tasya deho ’yaṁ
sthaviṣṭhaś ca sthavīyasām
yatredaṁ vyajyate viśvaṁ
bhūtaṁ bhavyaṁ bhavac ca sat
Śrīdhara — Transliteration
viśeṣo virāṭdehaḥ atisthūlānām api sthūlataraḥ | sat kāryamātram ||24||
Śrīdhara Sanskrit
विशेषो विराट्देहः अतिस्थूलानाम् अपि स्थूलतरः । सत् कार्यमात्रम् ॥२४॥

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. The “particular” is the Virāṭ body, grosser even than the very gross. “Sat” means the totality of manifested effects.

ŚB 2.1.25

अण्डकोशे शरीरेऽस्मिन् सप्तावरणसंयुते ।
वैराज: पुरुषो योऽसौ भगवान् धारणाश्रय: ॥ २५ ॥

The gigantic universal form of the Personality of Godhead, within the body of the universal shell, which is covered by sevenfold material elements, is the subject for the virāṭ conception.

Word-for-word
Bhāgavatam — Word-for-word
aṇḍa-kośe — within the universal shell; śarīre — in the body of; asmin — this; sapta — sevenfold; āvaraṇa — coverings; saṁyute — having so done; vairājaḥ — the gigantic universal; puruṣaḥ — form of the Lord; yaḥ — that; asau — He; bhagavān — the Personality of Godhead; dhāraṇā — conception; āśrayaḥ — object of.
Śrīdhara — Word-for-word
asya — of this; ca — and; upalakṣaṇatvena — by way of indication; viṣayatvam — being the object; vastutaḥ tu — but in reality; virāṭ-jīva-niyantā — the controller of the living being of the Virāṭ; bhagavān eva — Bhagavān alone; viṣayaḥ — the object; iti āha — thus he says; aṇḍa-kośa-antar-vartini — situated within the cosmic egg; kaṭāhe eva — in the shell itself; pṛthivī-āvaraṇam — the earth covering; tataḥ — thereafter; ap-tejo-vāyu-ākāśa-ahaṅkāra-mahat-tattvāni — water, fire, air, ether, ego, and mahat; iti sapta — thus seven.
Transliteration
Bhāgavatam — Transliteration
aṇḍa-kośe śarīre ’smin
saptāvaraṇa-saṁyute
vairājaḥ puruṣo yo ’sau
bhagavān dhāraṇāśrayaḥ
Śrīdhara — Transliteration
asya copalakṣaṇatvena viṣayatvaṁ, vastutas tu virāḍjīvaniyantā bhagavān eva viṣaya ity āha | aṇḍakośāntarvartini kaṭāha eva pṛthivyāvaraṇam | tataḥ aptejovāyvākāśāhaṁkāramahattattvānīti sapta ||25||
Śrīdhara Sanskrit
अस्य चोपलक्षणत्वेन विषयत्वं, वस्तुतस् तु विराड्जीवनियन्ता भगवान् एव विषय इत्य् आह । अण्डकोशान्तर्वर्तिनि कटाह एव पृथिव्यावरणम् । ततः अप्तेजोवाय्वाकाशाहंकारमहत्तत्त्वानीति सप्त ॥२५॥

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. The Virāṭ body is an object only by way of indication; in reality Bhagavān, the controller of the Virāṭ living being, is the object. Within the cosmic egg, the shell itself is the earth covering; beyond it are water, fire, air, ether, ego, and mahat—seven coverings in all.

ŚB 2.1.26

पातालमेतस्य हि पादमूलं
पठन्ति पार्ष्णिप्रपदे रसातलम् ।
महातलं विश्वसृजोऽथ गुल्फौ
तलातलं वै पुरुषस्य जङ्घे ॥ २६ ॥

Persons who have realized it have studied that the planets known as Pātāla constitute the bottoms of the feet of the universal Lord, and the heels and the toes are the Rasātala planets. The ankles are the Mahātala planets, and His shanks constitute the Talātala planets.

Word-for-word
Bhāgavatam — Word-for-word
pātālam — the planets at the bottom of the universe; etasya — of His; hi — exactly; pāda-mūlam — soles of the feet; paṭhanti — they study it; pārṣṇi — the heels; prapade — the toes; rasātalam — the planets named Rasātala; mahātalam — the planets named Mahātala; viśva-sṛjaḥ — of the creator of the universe; atha — thus; gulphau — the ankles; talātalam — the planets named Talātala; vai — as they are; puruṣasya — of the gigantic person; jaṅghe — the shanks.
Śrīdhara — Word-for-word
virāṭ-deha — the Virāṭ body; tat-jīva — its living being; tat-antaryāmiṇām — and their Inner Controller; abhedam āropya — superimposing non-difference; upāsanam kartavyam — worship/meditation is to be performed; iti āśayena āha — with this intention he says; pātālam — Pātāla; pāda-mūlam — the sole/base of the foot; pādasya adho-bhāgam — the lower part of the foot; pātāla-ādīnām — of Pātāla and the others; tat-avayavatā — being His limbs; vidhīyate — is prescribed; pātāla-ādīni atala-antāni — from Pātāla through Atala; adhastanāt ārabhya — beginning from the lowest; sapta bhū-vivarāṇi — the seven subterranean regions; paṭhanti gṛṇanti iti-ādi — “they recite,” “they call,” and similar expressions; pramāṇa-pradarśanam — indication of scriptural authority; pārṣṇi-prapade — heel and forepart of the foot; pādasya paścāt-puro-bhāgau — the back and front portions of the foot.
Transliteration
Bhāgavatam — Transliteration
pātālam etasya hi pāda-mūlaṁ
paṭhanti pārṣṇi-prapade rasātalam
mahātalaṁ viśva-sṛjo ’tha gulphau
talātalaṁ vai puruṣasya jaṅghe
Śrīdhara — Transliteration
virāḍdehatajjīvatadantaryāmiṇām abhedam āropyopāsanaṁ kartavyam ity āśayenāha | pātālaṁ pādamūlaṁ pādasyādhobhāgam | pātālādīnāṁ tadavayavatā vidhīyate | pātālādīny atalāntāny adhastanād ārabhya sapta bhūvivarāṇi | paṭhanti gṛṇantītyādipramāṇapradarśanam | pārṣṇiprapade pādasya paścātpurobhāgau ||26||
Śrīdhara Sanskrit
विराड्देहतज्जीवतदन्तर्यामिणाम् अभेदम् आरोप्योपासनं कर्तव्यम् इत्य् आशयेनाह । पातालं पादमूलं पादस्याधोभागम् । पातालादीनां तदवयवता विधीयते । पातालादीन्य् अतलान्तान्य् अधस्तनाद् आरभ्य सप्त भूविवराणि । पठन्ति गृणन्तीत्यादिप्रमाणप्रदर्शनम् । पार्ष्णिप्रपदे पादस्य पश्चात्पुरोभागौ ॥२६॥

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. With the intention that one should meditate by superimposing non-difference among the Virāṭ body, its living being, and their Inner Controller, he begins this description. Pātāla is the lower part of the foot. Pātāla through Atala are the seven subterranean regions, counted upward from below, and are prescribed as His limbs. Expressions such as “they recite” and “they call” indicate scriptural authority. The heel and forepart are respectively the back and front portions of the foot.

ŚB 2.1.27

द्वे जानुनी सुतलं विश्वमूर्ते-
रूरुद्वयं वितलं चातलं च ।
महीतलं तज्जघनं महीपते
नभस्तलं नाभिसरो गृणन्ति ॥ २७ ॥

The knees of the universal form are the planetary system of the name Sutala, and the two thighs are the Vitala and Atala planetary systems. The hips are Mahītala, and outer space is the depression of His navel.

ūru-dvayasya — of the pair of thighs; adho-bhāge — in the lower part; vitalam — Vitala; uttara-bhāge — in the upper part; atalam — Atala; iti jñeyam — thus it is to be understood; nābhiḥ eva — the navel itself; saraḥ — the lake.

dve jānunī sutalaṁ viśva-mūrter
ūru-dvayaṁ vitalaṁ cātalaṁ ca

Śrīdhara Sanskrit
ऊरुद्वयस्याधोभागे वितलं, उत्तरभागे अतलम् इति ज्ञेयम् । नाभिर् एव सरः ॥२७॥

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. Vitala is to be understood as the lower part of the two thighs and Atala as their upper part. The navel itself is the lake.

ŚB 2.1.28

उर:स्थलं ज्योतिरनीकमस्य
ग्रीवा महर्वदनं वै जनोऽस्य ।
तपो वराटीं विदुरादिपुंस:
सत्यं तु शीर्षाणि सहस्रशीर्ष्ण: ॥ २८ ॥

The chest of the Original Personality of the gigantic form is the luminary planetary system, His neck is the Mahar planets, His mouth is the Janas planets, and His forehead is the Tapas planetary system. The topmost planetary system, known as Satyaloka, is the head of He who has one thousand heads.

Word-for-word
Bhāgavatam — Word-for-word
uraḥ — high; sthalam — place (the chest); jyotiḥ-anīkam — the luminary planets; asya — of Him; grīvā — the neck; mahaḥ — the planetary system above the luminaries; vadanam — mouth; vai — exactly; janaḥ — the planetary system above Mahar; asya — of Him; tapaḥ — the planetary system above the Janas; varāṭīm — forehead; viduḥ — is known; ādi — the original; puṁsaḥ — personality; satyam — the topmost planetary system; tu — but; śīrṣāṇi — the head; sahasra — one thousand; śīrṣṇaḥ — one with heads.
Śrīdhara — Word-for-word
jyotir-anīkam — the host of lights; jyotiṣām samūham — the aggregate of luminous bodies; svargam — heaven; mahar-lokam — Maharloka; grīvā iti gṛṇanti — they call the neck; tapo-lokam — Tapoloka; rarāṭīm — the forehead; lalāṭam — forehead; satyam — Satya; satya-lokam — Satyaloka.
Transliteration
Bhāgavatam — Transliteration
uraḥ-sthalaṁ jyotir-anīkam asya
grīvā mahar vadanaṁ vai jano ’sya
tapo rarāṭīṁ vidur ādi-puṁsaḥ
satyaṁ tu śīrṣāṇi sahasra-śīrṣṇaḥ
Śrīdhara — Transliteration
jyotiranīkaṁ jyotiṣāṁ samūhaṁ svargam | maharlokaṁ grīveti gṛṇanti | tapolokaṁ rarāṭīṁ lalāṭam | satyaṁ satyalokam ||28||
Śrīdhara Sanskrit
ज्योतिरनीकं ज्योतिषां समूहं स्वर्गम् । महर्लोकं ग्रीवेति गृणन्ति । तपोलोकं रराटीं ललाटम् । सत्यं सत्यलोकम् ॥२८॥

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. “The host of lights” means heaven, the aggregate of luminous bodies. They call Maharloka His neck. Tapoloka is the forehead, and “Satya” means Satyaloka.

ŚB 2.1.29

इन्द्रादयो बाहव आहुरुस्रा:
कर्णौ दिश:श्रोत्रममुष्य शब्द: ।
नासत्यदस्रौ परमस्य नासे
घ्राणोऽस्य गन्धो मुखमग्निरिद्ध: ॥ २९ ॥

His arms are the demigods headed by Indra, the ten directional sides are His ears, and physical sound is His sense of hearing. His nostrils are the two Aśvinī-kumāras, and material fragrance is His sense of smell. His mouth is the blazing fire.

Word-for-word
Bhāgavatam — Word-for-word
indra-ādayaḥ — demigods headed by the heavenly king, Indra; bāhavaḥ — arms; āhuḥ — are called; usrāḥ — the demigods; karṇau — the ears; diśaḥ — the four directions; śrotram — the sense of hearing; amuṣya — of the Lord; śabdaḥ — sound; nāsatya-dasrau — the demigods known as the Aśvinī-kumāras; paramasya — of the Supreme; nāse — nostrils; ghrāṇaḥ — the sense of smell; asya — of Him; gandhaḥ — fragrance; mukham — the mouth; agniḥ — fire; iddhaḥ — blazing.
Śrīdhara — Word-for-word
usrāḥ — the gods; tejomaya-śarīratvāt — because their bodies are made of radiance; te bāhavaḥ iti āhuḥ — they say those are His arms; diśaḥ — the directions; asmad-ādi-śrotra-adhiṣṭhātryaḥ devatāḥ — the deities presiding over hearing for us and others; karṇau — the two ears; śrotrasya adhiṣṭhānam — the seat of hearing; śabdaḥ tu — sound, however; śrotra-viṣayaḥ — the object of hearing; saḥ tasya śrotrendriyam — that is His faculty of hearing; evam — likewise; nāsikā-ādiṣu api — in the nose and the rest also; nāsatya-dasrau — Nāsatya and Dasra; aśvinau — the Aśvins; nāse — the two nostrils; nāsā-puṭe — the nasal openings; iddhaḥ — kindled, blazing.
Transliteration
Bhāgavatam — Transliteration
indrādayo bāhava āhur usrāḥ
karṇau diśaḥ śrotram amuṣya śabdaḥ
nāsatya-dasrau paramasya nāse
ghrāṇo ’sya gandho mukham agnir iddhaḥ
Śrīdhara — Transliteration
usrā devāḥ tejomayaśarīratvāt te bāhava ity āhuḥ | diśo’smadādiśrotrādhiṣṭhātryo devatāḥ karṇau śrotrasyādhiṣṭhānam | śabdas tu śrotraviṣayaḥ | sa tasya śrotrendriyam | evaṁ nāsikādiṣv api | nāsatyadasrāv aśvinau nāse nāsāpuṭe | iddho dīptaḥ ||29||
Śrīdhara Sanskrit
उस्रा देवाः तेजोमयशरीरत्वात् ते बाहव इत्य् आहुः । दिशोऽस्मदादिश्रोत्राधिष्ठात्र्यो देवताः कर्णौ श्रोत्रस्याधिष्ठानम् । शब्दस् तु श्रोत्रविषयः । स तस्य श्रोत्रेन्द्रियम् । एवं नासिकादिष्व् अपि । नासत्यदस्राव् अश्विनौ नासे नासापुटे । इद्धो दीप्तः ॥२९॥

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. “Usrāḥ” means the gods, so called here because their bodies are radiant; they are said to be His arms. The directions are the deities presiding over hearing, the ears are its seat, and sound is its object; that corresponds to His auditory faculty. The same pattern applies to the nose and the other senses. Nāsatya and Dasra are the two Aśvins and are His nostrils. Iddhaḥ means blazing.

ŚB 2.1.30

द्यौरक्षिणी चक्षुरभूत्पतङ्ग:
पक्ष्माणि विष्णोरहनी उभे च ।
तद्भ्रूविजृम्भ: परमेष्ठिधिष्ण्य-
मापोऽस्य तालु रस एव जिह्वा ॥ ३० ॥

The sphere of outer space constitutes His eyepits, and the eyeball is the sun as the power of seeing. His eyelids are both the day and night, and in the movements of His eyebrows, Brahmā and similar supreme personalities reside. His palate is the director of water, Varuṇa, and the juice or essence of everything is His tongue.

Word-for-word
Bhāgavatam — Word-for-word
dyauḥ — the sphere of outer space; akṣiṇī — the eyeballs; cakṣuḥ — of eyes (senses); abhūt — it so became; pataṅgaḥ — the sun; pakṣmāṇi — eyelids; viṣṇoḥ — of the Personality of Godhead, Śrī Viṣṇu; ahanī — day and night; ubhe — both; ca — and; tat — His; bhrū — eyebrows; vijṛmbhaḥ — movements; parameṣṭhi — the supreme entity (Brahmā); dhiṣṇyam — post; āpaḥ — Varuṇa, the director of water; asya — His; tālū — palate; rasaḥ — juice; eva — certainly; jihvā — the tongue.
Śrīdhara — Word-for-word
dyauḥ — the sky, mid-region; antarikṣam — atmosphere; akṣiṇī — the two eyes; netra-golake — the eyeballs; cakṣuḥ — sight; indriyam — the sense faculty; pataṅgaḥ — the sun; ahanī — the two days/time-periods; rātri-ahanī — night and day; parameṣṭhi-dhiṣṇyam — the station of Parameṣṭhin; brahma-padam — Brahmā’s abode; tāluḥ — palate; adhiṣṭhānam — the seat; jihvā — tongue; indriyam — the sense faculty.
Transliteration
Bhāgavatam — Transliteration
dyaur akṣiṇī cakṣur abhūt pataṅgaḥ
pakṣmāṇi viṣṇor ahanī ubhe ca
tad-bhrū-vijṛmbhaḥ parameṣṭhi-dhiṣṇyam
āpo ’sya tālu rasa eva jihvā
Śrīdhara — Transliteration
dyaur antarikṣam | akṣiṇī netragolake | cakṣur indriyam | pataṅgaḥ sūryaḥ | ahanī rātryahaṇī | parameṣṭhidhiṣṇyaṁ brahmapadam | tālur adhiṣṭhānam | jihvā indriyam ||30||
Śrīdhara Sanskrit
द्यौर् अन्तरिक्षम् । अक्षिणी नेत्रगोलके । चक्षुर् इन्द्रियम् । पतङ्गः सूर्यः । अहनी रात्र्यहणी । परमेष्ठिधिष्ण्यं ब्रह्मपदम् । तालुर् अधिष्ठानम् । जिह्वा इन्द्रियम् ॥३०॥

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. “Dyauḥ” here is the mid-region. The two eyes are the eyeballs, while cakṣus is the faculty of sight; pataṅga is the sun. The two “days” are night and day. The station of Parameṣṭhin is Brahmā’s abode. The palate is the seat, and the tongue is the sense faculty.

ŚB 2.1.31

छन्दांस्यनन्तस्य शिरो गृणन्ति
दंष्ट्रा यम: स्‍नेहकला द्विजानि ।
हासो जनोन्मादकरी च माया
दुरन्तसर्गो यदपाङ्गमोक्ष: ॥ ३१ ॥

They say that the Vedic hymns are the cerebral passage of the Lord, and His jaws of teeth are Yama, god of death, who punishes the sinners. The art of affection is His set of teeth, and the most alluring illusory material energy is His smile. This great ocean of material creation is but the casting of His glance over us.

Word-for-word
Bhāgavatam — Word-for-word
chandāṁsi — the Vedic hymns; anantasya — of the Supreme; śiraḥ — the cerebral passage; gṛṇanti — they say; daṁṣṭrāḥ — the jaws of teeth; yamaḥ — Yamarāja, the director of sinners; sneha-kalāḥ — the art of affection; dvijāni — the set of teeth; hāsaḥ — smile; jana-unmāda-karī — the most alluring; ca — also; māyā — illusory energy; duranta — unsurpassable; sargaḥ — the material creation; yat-apāṅga — whose glance; mokṣaḥ — casting over.
Śrīdhara — Word-for-word
chandāṁsi — the metres; vedāḥ — the Vedas; śiraḥ — head; brahma-randhram — the brahmarandhra; sneha-kalāḥ — portions of affection; putra-ādi-sneha-leśāḥ — slight attachments to sons and others; dvijāni — the twice-born ones; dantāḥ — the teeth; ṣaṇḍhatvam — neuter gender/form; ārṣam — Vedic/authoritative usage; durantaḥ — without end; apāraḥ — boundless; sargaḥ — creation; iti yat — that which is so described; saḥ tasya kaṭākṣaḥ — that is His sidelong glance.
Transliteration
Bhāgavatam — Transliteration
chandāṁsy anantasya śiro gṛṇanti
daṁṣṭrā yamaḥ sneha-kalā dvijāni
hāso janonmāda-karī ca māyā
duranta-sargo yad-apāṅga-mokṣaḥ
Śrīdhara — Transliteration
chandāṁsi vedāḥ | śiro brahmarandhram | snehakalāḥ putrādisnehaleśāḥ | dvijāni dantāḥ, ṣaṇḍhatvam ārṣam | duranto’pāraḥ sarga iti yat sa tasya kaṭākṣaḥ ||31||
Śrīdhara Sanskrit
छन्दांसि वेदाः । शिरो ब्रह्मरन्ध्रम् । स्नेहकलाः पुत्रादिस्नेहलेशाः । द्विजानि दन्ताः, षण्ढत्वम् आर्षम् । दुरन्तोऽपारः सर्ग इति यत् स तस्य कटाक्षः ॥३१॥

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. The metres denote the Vedas, and “head” means the brahmarandhra. The portions of affection are slight attachments toward sons and others. The “twice-born” are the teeth; the neuter form is an authoritative usage. The endless, boundless creation is His sidelong glance.

ŚB 2.1.32

व्रीडोत्तरौष्ठोऽधर एव लोभो
धर्म: स्तनोऽधर्मपथोऽस्य पृष्ठम् ।
कस्तस्य मेढ्रं वृषणौ च मित्रौ
कुक्षि: समुद्रा गिरयोऽस्थिसङ्घा: ॥ ३२ ॥

Modesty is the upper portion of His lips, hankering is His chin, religion is the breast of the Lord, and irreligion is His back. Brahmājī, who generates all living beings in the material world, is His genitals, and the Mitrā-varuṇas are His two testicles. The ocean is His waist, and the hills and mountains are the stacks of His bones.

Word-for-word
Bhāgavatam — Word-for-word
vrīḍa — modesty; uttara — upper; oṣṭha — lip; adharaḥ — chin; eva — certainly; lobhaḥ — hankering; dharmaḥ — religion; stanaḥ — breast; adharma — irreligion; pathaḥ — way; asya — His; pṛṣṭham — back; kaḥ — Brahmā; tasya — His; meḍhram — genitals; vṛṣaṇau — testicles; ca — also; mitrau — the Mitrā-varuṇas; kukṣiḥ — waist; samudrāḥ — the oceans; girayaḥ — the hills; asthi — bones; saṅghāḥ — stack.
Śrīdhara — Word-for-word
adharma-mārgaḥ — the path of irreligion; asya — His; pṛṣṭha-bhāgaḥ — back portion; kaḥ — “Ka”; prajāpatiḥ — Prajāpati; mitrau — the two Mitras; mitra-varuṇau — Mitra and Varuṇa.
Transliteration
Bhāgavatam — Transliteration
vrīḍottarauṣṭho ’dhara eva lobho
dharmaḥ stano ’dharma-patho ’sya pṛṣṭham
