adharmamārgo’sya pṛṣṭhabhāgaḥ | kaḥ prajāpatiḥ | mitrau mitrāvaruṇau ||32||
Śrīdhara Sanskrit
अधर्ममार्गोऽस्य पृष्ठभागः । कः प्रजापतिः । मित्रौ मित्रावरुणौ ॥३२॥

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. The path of irreligion is His back. “Ka” means Prajāpati. The two “Mitras” are Mitra and Varuṇa.

ŚB 2.1.33

नद्योऽस्य नाड्योऽथ तनूरुहाणि
महीरुहा विश्वतनोर्नृपेन्द्र ।
अनन्तवीर्य: श्वसितं मातरिश्वा
गतिर्वय: कर्म गुणप्रवाह: ॥ ३३ ॥

O King, the rivers are the veins of the gigantic body, the trees are the hairs of His body, and the omnipotent air is His breath. The passing ages are His movements, and His activities are the reactions of the three modes of material nature.

Word-for-word
Bhāgavatam — Word-for-word
nadyaḥ — the rivers; asya — of Him; nāḍyaḥ — veins; atha — and thereafter; tanū-ruhāṇi — hairs on the body; mahī-ruhāḥ — the plants and trees; viśva-tanoḥ — of the universal form; nṛpa-indra — O King; ananta-vīryaḥ — of the omnipotent; śvasitam — breathing; mātariśvā — air; gatiḥ — movement; vayaḥ — passing ages; karma — activity; guṇa-pravāhaḥ — reactions of the modes of nature.
Śrīdhara — Word-for-word
anantam vīryam — limitless power; yasya saḥ — He whose power is such; vayaḥ — age; kālaḥ — time; tasya gamanam — His movement; guṇa-pravāhaḥ — the flow of the guṇas; prāṇinām saṁsāraḥ — the worldly course of living beings; karma — action; tasya krīḍā — His play.
Transliteration
Bhāgavatam — Transliteration
nadyo ’sya nāḍyo ’tha tanū-ruhāṇi
mahī-ruhā viśva-tanor nṛpendra
ananta-vīryaḥ śvasitaṁ mātariśvā
gatir vayaḥ karma guṇa-pravāhaḥ
Śrīdhara — Transliteration
anantaṁ vīryaṁ yasya saḥ | vayaḥ kālas tasya gamanam | guṇapravāhaḥ prāṇināṁ saṁsāraḥ karma tasya krīḍā ||33||
Śrīdhara Sanskrit
अनन्तं वीर्यं यस्य सः । वयः कालस् तस्य गमनम् । गुणप्रवाहः प्राणिनां संसारः कर्म तस्य क्रीडा ॥३३॥

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. He is “of limitless power.” Age is time, and time is His movement. The flow of the guṇas is the saṁsāra of living beings, while action is His play.

ŚB 2.1.34

ईशस्य केशान् विदुरम्बुवाहान्
वासस्तु सन्ध्यां कुरुवर्य भूम्न: ।
अव्यक्तमाहुर्हृदयं मनश्च
स चन्द्रमा: सर्वविकारकोश: ॥ ३४ ॥

O best amongst the Kurus, the clouds which carry water are the hairs on His head, the terminations of days or nights are His dress, and the supreme cause of material creation is His intelligence. His mind is the moon, the reservoir of all changes.

Word-for-word
Bhāgavatam — Word-for-word
īśasya — of the supreme controller; keśān — hairs on the head; viduḥ — you may know it from me; ambu-vāhān — the clouds which carry water; vāsaḥ tu — the dress; sandhyām — termination of day and night; kuru-varya — O best of the Kurus; bhūmnaḥ — of the Almighty; avyaktam — the prime cause of material creation; āhuḥ — it is said; hṛdayam — intelligence; manaḥ ca — and the mind; saḥ — He; candramāḥ — the moon; sarva-vikāra-kośaḥ — the reservoir of all changes.
Śrīdhara — Word-for-word
bhūmnaḥ — of the abundant, all-pervading One; vibhoḥ — of the Lord; avyaktam — the unmanifest; pradhānam — primordial nature; saḥ prasiddhaḥ candramāḥ — that well-known moon; tadīyam manaḥ — His mind; sarveṣām vikārāṇām — of all transformations; kośa iva — like a receptacle; āśraya-bhūtam — serving as the support.
Transliteration
Bhāgavatam — Transliteration
īśasya keśān vidur ambuvāhān
vāsas tu sandhyāṁ kuru-varya bhūmnaḥ
avyaktam āhur hṛdayaṁ manaś ca
sa candramāḥ sarva-vikāra-kośaḥ
Śrīdhara — Transliteration
bhūmno vibhoḥ | avyaktaṁ pradhānam | saḥ prasiddhaś candramās tadīyaṁ manaḥ | sarveṣāṁ vikārāṇāṁ kośa ivāśrayabhūtam ||34||
Śrīdhara Sanskrit
भूम्नो विभोः । अव्यक्तं प्रधानम् । सः प्रसिद्धश् चन्द्रमास् तदीयं मनः । सर्वेषां विकाराणां कोश इवाश्रयभूतम् ॥३४॥

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. “Bhūman” means the all-pervading Lord. The unmanifest is Pradhāna. The well-known moon is His mind, which is like a receptacle and support for all transformations.

ŚB 2.1.35

विज्ञानशक्तिं महिमामनन्ति
सर्वात्मनोऽन्त:करणं गिरित्रम् ।
अश्वाश्वतर्युष्ट्रगजा नखानि
सर्वे मृगा: पशव: श्रोणिदेशे ॥ ३५ ॥

The principle of matter [mahat-tattva] is the consciousness of the omnipresent Lord, as asserted by the experts, and Rudradeva is His ego. The horse, mule, camel and elephant are His nails, and wild animals and all quadrupeds are situated in the belt zone of the Lord.

Word-for-word
Bhāgavatam — Word-for-word
vijñāna-śaktim — consciousness; mahim — the principle of matter; āmananti — they call it so; sarva-ātmanaḥ — of the omnipresent; antaḥ-karaṇam — ego; giritram — Rudra (Śiva); aśva — horse; aśvatari — mule; uṣṭra — camel; gajāḥ — elephant; nakhāni — nails; sarve — all other; mṛgāḥ — stags; paśavaḥ — quadrupeds; śroṇi-deśe — on the region of the belt.
Śrīdhara — Word-for-word
vijñāna-śaktim — the power of cognition; cittam — the citta; mahim — “mahi”; mahat-tattvam — the great principle; antaḥ-karaṇam — the inner organ; ahaṅkāram — ego; giritram — Giritra; śrī-rudram — Śrī Rudra; gardabhāt — from a donkey; vaḍavāyām — in a mare; utpannā — born; aśvatarī — a mule.
Transliteration
Bhāgavatam — Transliteration
vijñāna-śaktiṁ mahim āmananti
sarvātmano ’ntaḥ-karaṇaṁ giritram
aśvāśvatary-uṣṭra-gajā nakhāni
sarve mṛgāḥ paśavaḥ śroṇi-deśe
Śrīdhara — Transliteration
vijñānaśaktiṁ cittam | mahiṁ mahattattvam | antaḥkaraṇam ahaṁkāram | giritraṁ śrīrudram | gardabhād vaḍavāyām utpannā aśvatarī ||35||
Śrīdhara Sanskrit
विज्ञानशक्तिं चित्तम् । महिं महत्तत्त्वम् । अन्तःकरणम् अहंकारम् । गिरित्रं श्रीरुद्रम् । गर्दभाद् वडवायाम् उत्पन्ना अश्वतरी ॥३५॥

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. The “power of cognition” is citta; mahi is the mahat-tattva; the inner organ is ahaṅkāra; and Giritra is Śrī Rudra. An aśvatarī, a mule, is born from a donkey and a mare.

ŚB 2.1.36

वयांसि तद्व्याकरणं विचित्रं
मनुर्मनीषा मनुजो निवास: ।
गन्धर्वविद्याधरचारणाप्सर:
स्वरस्मृतीरसुरानीकवीर्य: ॥ ३६ ॥

Varieties of birds are indications of His masterful artistic sense. Manu, the father of mankind, is the emblem of His standard intelligence, and humanity is His residence. The celestial species of human beings, like the Gandharvas, Vidyādharas, Cāraṇas and angels, all represent His musical rhythm, and the demoniac soldiers are representations of His wonderful prowess.

Word-for-word
Bhāgavatam — Word-for-word
vayāṁsi — varieties of birds; tat-vyākaraṇam — vocables; vicitram — artistic; manuḥ — the father of mankind; manīṣā — thoughts; manujaḥ — mankind (the sons of Manu); nivāsaḥ — residence; gandharva — the human beings named Gandharvas; vidyādhara — the Vidyādharas; cāraṇa — the Cāraṇas; apsaraḥ — the angels; svara — musical rhythm; smṛtīḥ — remembrance; asura-anīka — the demoniac soldiers; vīryaḥ — prowess.
Śrīdhara — Word-for-word
vayāṁsi — birds; pakṣiṇaḥ — winged creatures; tasya vyākaraṇam — His articulation/differentiation; nāma-rūpe vyākaravāṇi iti — “I shall differentiate name and form”; śruti-uktam — stated by revelation; śilpa-naipuṇyam — skill in artistry; yathā āhuḥ — as they say; yena — by whom; śuklī-kṛtāḥ haṁsāḥ — swans were made white; śukāḥ ca haritī-kṛtāḥ — and parrots made green; mayūrāḥ citritāḥ — peacocks were variegated; saḥ te vṛttim vidhāsyati — He will arrange your livelihood.
Transliteration
Bhāgavatam — Transliteration
vayāṁsi tad-vyākaraṇaṁ vicitraṁ
manur manīṣā manujo nivāsaḥ
gandharva-vidyādhara-cāraṇāpsaraḥ
svara-smṛtīr asurānīka-vīryaḥ
Śrīdhara — Transliteration
vayāṁsi pakṣiṇaḥ | tasya vyākaraṇaṁ nāmarūpe vyākaravāṇīti śrutyuktaṁ śilpanaipuṇyam | yathāhuḥ— yena śuklīkṛtā haṁsāḥ śukāś ca haritīkṛtāḥ | mayūrāś citritā yena sa te vṛttiṁ vidhāsyati || iti ||36||
Śrīdhara Sanskrit
वयांसि पक्षिणः । तस्य व्याकरणं नामरूपे व्याकरवाणीति श्रुत्युक्तं शिल्पनैपुण्यम् । यथाहुः— येन शुक्लीकृता हंसाः शुकाश् च हरितीकृताः । मयूराश् चित्रिता येन स ते वृत्तिं विधास्यति ॥ इति ॥३६॥

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. “Vayāṁsi” means birds. His “grammar” is the artistic skill described by the revelation, “I shall differentiate name and form.” Thus it is said: “He by whom swans were made white, parrots green, and peacocks variegated—He will arrange your livelihood.”

ŚB 2.1.37

ब्रह्माननं क्षत्रभुजो महात्मा
विडूरुरङ्‌घ्रिश्रितकृष्णवर्ण: ।
नानाभिधाभीज्यगणोपपन्नो
द्रव्यात्मक: कर्म वितानयोग: ॥ ३७ ॥

The virāṭ-puruṣa’s face is the brāhmaṇas, His arms are the kṣatriyas, His thighs are the vaiśyas, and the śūdras are under the protection of His feet. All the worshipable demigods are also overtaken by Him, and it is the duty of everyone to perform sacrifices with feasible goods to appease the Lord.

Word-for-word
Bhāgavatam — Word-for-word
brahma — the brāhmaṇas; ānanam — the face; kṣatra — the kṣatriyas; bhujaḥ — the arms; mahātmā — the virāṭ-puruṣa; viṭ — the vaiśyas; ūruḥ — the thighs; aṅghri-śrita — under the protection of His feet; kṛṣṇa-varṇaḥ — the śūdras; nānā — various; abhidhā — by names; abhījya-gaṇa — the demigods; upapannaḥ — being overtaken; dravya-ātmakaḥ — with feasible goods; karma — activities; vitāna-yogaḥ — performances of sacrifice.
Śrīdhara — Word-for-word
brahmā — brāhmaṇa; vipraḥ — a brāhmaṇa; tasya ānanam — His face; mukham — mouth; kṣatram — kṣatriya class; kṣatriyaḥ — kṣatriya; bhujau yasya — whose two arms; viṭ — vaiśya; vaiśyaḥ — a vaiśya; ūrū yasya — whose thighs; aṅghri-śritaḥ — situated at the feet; kṛṣṇa-varṇaḥ — dark-colored class; śūdraḥ yasya — whose śūdra; nānā-abhidhāḥ — having many names; nāmāni yeṣām — whose names are many; abhījyāḥ devāḥ — deities to be worshiped; teṣām gaṇaiḥ — by their groups; vasu-rudra-ādibhiḥ — the Vasus, Rudras, and others; upapannaḥ yuktaḥ — endowed, connected; dravya-ātmakaḥ haviḥ — oblation consisting of substances; sādhyaḥ — accomplished by; vitāna-yogaḥ — sacrificial arrangement; yajña-prayogaḥ — performance of sacrifice; tasya karma kāryam — His action/effect; āvaśyakaḥ abhipretaḥ — “necessary” is intended.
Transliteration
Bhāgavatam — Transliteration
brahmānanaṁ kṣatra-bhujo mahātmā
viḍ ūrur aṅghri-śrita-kṛṣṇa-varṇaḥ
nānābhidhābhījya-gaṇopapanno
dravyātmakaḥ karma vitāna-yogaḥ
Śrīdhara — Transliteration
brahmā vipraḥ, tasyānanaṁ mukham | kṣatraṁ kṣatriyo bhujau yasya | viḍ vaiśya ūrū yasya | aṅghriśritaḥ kṛṣṇavarṇaḥ śūdro yasya | nānābhidhā nāmāni yeṣāṁ te ca te abhījyā devāḥ, teṣāṁ gaṇair vasurudrādibhir upapanno yukto dravyātmako haviḥ sādhyo vitānayogo yajñaprayogas tasya karma kāryam | āvaśyako’bhipreta ity arthaḥ ||37||
Śrīdhara Sanskrit
ब्रह्मा विप्रः, तस्याननं मुखम् । क्षत्रं क्षत्रियो भुजौ यस्य । विड् वैश्य ऊरू यस्य । अङ्घ्रिश्रितः कृष्णवर्णः शूद्रो यस्य । नानाभिधा नामानि येषां ते च ते अभीज्या देवाः, तेषां गणैर् वसुरुद्रादिभिर् उपपन्नो युक्तो द्रव्यात्मको हविः साध्यो वितानयोगो यज्ञप्रयोगस् तस्य कर्म कार्यम् । आवश्यकोऽभिप्रेत इत्य् अर्थः ॥३७॥

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. “Brahmā” means the brāhmaṇa, who is His mouth; the kṣatriya is His arms; the vaiśya His thighs; and the dark-colored śūdra rests at His feet. The deities to be worshiped have many names and occur in groups such as the Vasus and Rudras. Sacrificial performance, accomplished with material oblations, is His action. The intended sense is that it is necessary.

ŚB 2.1.38

इयानसावीश्वरविग्रहस्य
य: सन्निवेष: कथितो मया ते ।
सन्धार्यतेऽस्मिन् वपुषि स्थविष्ठे
मन: स्वबुद्ध्या न यतोऽस्ति किञ्चित् ॥ ३८ ॥

I have thus explained to you the gross material gigantic conception of the Personality of Godhead. One who seriously desires liberation concentrates his mind on this form of the Lord, because there is nothing more than this in the material world.

Word-for-word
Bhāgavatam — Word-for-word
iyān — all these; asau — that; īśvara — Supreme Lord; vigrahasya — of the form; yaḥ — whatsoever; sanniveśaḥ — as they are located; kathitaḥ — explained; mayā — by me; te — unto you; sandhāryate — one may concentrate; asmin — in this; vapuṣi — form of virāṭ; sthaviṣṭhe — in the gross; manaḥ — mind; sva-buddhyā — by one’s intelligence; na — not; yataḥ — beyond Him; asti — there is; kiñcit — anything else.
Śrīdhara — Word-for-word
iyān — so great, this much; etāvān — of this extent; sanniveśaḥ — arrangement; avayava-saṁsthānam — disposition of the limbs; asmin — in this; sva-buddhyā — by one’s own intelligence; manaḥ sandhāryate — the mind is concentrated; mumukṣubhiḥ — by those desiring liberation; yataḥ — because; vyatiriktam — separate from; kiñcit na asti — nothing exists; tasmin — apart from Him/in Him.
Transliteration
Bhāgavatam — Transliteration
iyān asāv īśvara-vigrahasya
yaḥ sanniveśaḥ kathito mayā te
sandhāryate ’smin vapuṣi sthaviṣṭhe
manaḥ sva-buddhyā na yato ’sti kiñcit
Śrīdhara — Transliteration
iyān etāvān sanniveśo’vayavasaṁsthānam | asmin svabuddhyā manaḥ sandhāryate mumukṣubhiḥ | yato vyatiriktaṁ kiṁcin nāsti tasmin ||38||
Śrīdhara Sanskrit
इयान् एतावान् सन्निवेशोऽवयवसंस्थानम् । अस्मिन् स्वबुद्ध्या मनः सन्धार्यते मुमुक्षुभिः । यतो व्यतिरिक्तं किंचिन् नास्ति तस्मिन् ॥३८॥

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. “Iyān” means this much: the described arrangement of the limbs. Those desiring liberation should concentrate the mind on this form with their own intelligence, because nothing exists separate from Him.

ŚB 2.1.39

स सर्वधीवृत्त्यनुभूतसर्व
आत्मा यथा स्वप्नजनेक्षितैक: ।
तं सत्यमानन्दनिधिं भजेत
नान्यत्र सज्जेद् यत आत्मपात: ॥ ३९ ॥

One should concentrate his mind upon the Supreme Personality of Godhead, who alone distributes Himself in so many manifestations just as ordinary persons create thousands of manifestations in dreams. One must concentrate the mind on Him, the only all-blissful Absolute Truth. Otherwise one will be misled and will cause his own degradation.

Word-for-word
Bhāgavatam — Word-for-word
saḥ — He (the Supreme Person); sarva-dhī-vṛtti — the process of realization by all sorts of intelligence; anubhūta — cognizant; sarve — everyone; ātmā — the Supersoul; yathā — as much as; svapna-jana — a person dreaming; īkṣita — seen by; ekaḥ — one and the same; tam — unto Him; satyam — the Supreme Truth; ānanda-nidhim — the ocean of bliss; bhajeta — must one worship; na — never; anyatra — anything else; sajjet — be attached; yataḥ — whereby; ātma-pātaḥ — degradation of oneself.
Śrīdhara — Word-for-word
tat evam — thus, in this way; citta-sthairya-artham — for steadiness of the mind; virāṭ-deha-jīva-īśvarāṇām — of the Virāṭ body, the living being, and the Lord; abhedena upāsanam — meditation through non-difference; uktam — has been taught; tatra tu — but there; deha-jīvau — the body and living being; īśvare pravilāpya — dissolving into the Lord; saḥ eva dhyeyaḥ — He alone is to be meditated upon; iti nirdhārayati — thus he determines; saḥ iti — with the word “saḥ”; sarveṣām dhī-vṛttibhiḥ — through all functions of intelligence; anubhūtam sarvam yena — by whom everything is experienced; saḥ ekaḥ eva — He alone; sarva-antaraḥ ātmā — the inner Self of all; tam eva satyam bhajet — one should worship Him alone as the Real; anyatra upalakṣaṇe na sajjeta — one should not become attached to the indicative representation elsewhere; yataḥ āsaṅgāt — because from attachment; ātmanaḥ pātaḥ — the fall of the self; saṁsāraḥ bhavati — saṁsāra arises; ekasya — of one; tat-tat-indriyaiḥ — through the various senses; sarva-anubhūtau — in experiencing everything; dṛṣṭāntaḥ — the example; svapna-janānām īkṣitā yathā — like the one observer of dream persons; svapne hi — for in a dream; kadācit — sometimes; bahūn dehān prakalpya — imagining many bodies; jīvaḥ — the living being; tat-tat-indriyaiḥ sarvam paśyati — sees everything through their respective senses; tadvat — similarly; īśvarasya tu — but for the Lord; vidyā-śaktitvāt — because His power is knowledge; na bandhaḥ — there is no bondage.
Transliteration
Bhāgavatam — Transliteration
sa sarva-dhī-vṛtty-anubhūta-sarva
ātmā yathā svapna-janekṣitaikaḥ
taṁ satyam ānanda-nidhiṁ bhajeta
nānyatra sajjed yata ātma-pātaḥ
Śrīdhara — Transliteration
tad evaṁ cittasthairyārthaṁ virāṭdehajīveśvarāṇām abhedenopāsanam uktaṁ, tatra tu dehajīvāv īśvare pravilāpya sa eva dhyeya iti nirdhārayati—sa iti | sarveṣāṁ dhīvṛttibhir anubhūtaṁ sarvaṁ yena, sa eka eva sarvāntara ātmā, tam eva satyaṁ bhajeta | anyatra upalakṣaṇe na sajjet | yata āsaṅgād ātmanaḥ pātaḥ saṁsāro bhavati | ekasya tattadindriyaiḥ sarvānubhūtau dṛṣṭāntaḥ—svapnajanānām īkṣitā yatheti | svapne hi kadācid bahūn dehān prakalpya jīvas tattadindriyaiḥ sarvaṁ paśyati tadvat | īśvarasya tu vidyāśaktitvān na bandhaḥ ||39||
Śrīdhara Sanskrit
तद् एवं चित्तस्थैर्यार्थं विराट्देहजीवेश्वराणाम् अभेदेनोपासनम् उक्तं, तत्र तु देहजीवाव् ईश्वरे प्रविलाप्य स एव ध्येय इति निर्धारयति—स इति । सर्वेषां धीवृत्तिभिर् अनुभूतं सर्वं येन, स एक एव सर्वान्तर आत्मा, तम् एव सत्यं भजेत । अन्यत्र उपलक्षणे न सज्जेत् । यत आसङ्गाद् आत्मनः पातः संसारो भवति । एकस्य तत्तदिन्द्रियैः सर्वानुभूतौ दृष्टान्तः—स्वप्नजनानाम् ईक्षिता यथेति । स्वप्ने हि कदाचिद् बहून् देहान् प्रकल्प्य जीवस् तत्तदिन्द्रियैः सर्वं पश्यति तद्वत् । ईश्वरस्य तु विद्याशक्तित्वान् न बन्धः ॥३९॥

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. Thus, for steadiness of mind, meditation was taught by superimposing non-difference on the Virāṭ body, its living being, and the Lord. Here Śrīdhara determines that the body and living being are to be dissolved into the Lord, and He alone is to be meditated upon. He alone, the inner Self of all, experiences everything through all functions of intelligence; one should worship Him as the Real and not become attached to the indicative representation, because attachment leads to the self’s fall into saṁsāra. The example of one experiencer seeing through many senses is the single observer of dream persons: in a dream the living being may imagine many bodies and see through their various senses. The Lord, however, is not bound, because His power is knowledge.

ŚB 2.2.1

श्रीशुक उवाच
एवं पुरा धारणयात्मयोनि-
र्नष्टां स्मृतिं प्रत्यवरुध्य तुष्टात् ।
तथा ससर्जेदममोघद‍ृष्टि-
र्यथाप्ययात् प्राग् व्यवसायबुद्धि: ॥ १ ॥

Śrī Śukadeva Gosvāmī said: Formerly, prior to the manifestation of the cosmos, Lord Brahmā, by meditating on the virāṭ-rūpa, regained his lost consciousness by appeasing the Lord. Thus he was able to rebuild the creation as it was before.

Word-for-word
Bhāgavatam — Word-for-word
śrī-śukaḥ uvāca — Śrī Śukadeva Gosvāmī said; evam — just in the same way; purā — prior to the manifestation of the cosmos; dhāraṇayā — by such a conception; ātma-yoniḥ — of Brahmājī; naṣṭām — lost; smṛtim — remembrance; pratyavarudhya — by regaining consciousness; tuṣṭāt — because of appeasing the Lord; tathā — thereafter; sasarja — created; idam — this material world; amogha-dṛṣṭiḥ — one who has attained clear vision; yathā — as; apyayāt — created; prāk — as formerly; vyavasāya — ascertained; buddhiḥ — intelligence.
Śrīdhara — Word-for-word
dvitīye tu — now, in the second chapter; tataḥ sthūla-dhāraṇātaḥ — after that gross-form concentration; jitam manaḥ — the mind that has been conquered; sarva-sākṣiṇi — in the witness of all; sarveśe viṣṇau — in Viṣṇu, Lord of all; dhāryam — is to be fixed; dṛśya-ālambana-rūpā — having a visible support as its form; vairāja-dhāraṇā — meditation on the Virāṭ; tad-sādhyā — accomplished by that; sarva-antaryāmi-dhāraṇā — meditation on the indwelling Lord of all; avāntara-phalam — an intermediate result; naṣṭām sṛṣṭi-smṛtim — the lost memory of creation; pratyavarudhya labdhvā — regaining, having obtained; brahmā — Brahmā; apyayāt prāk — before the dissolution; yathā āsīt tathā sasarja — created it as it had been; vyavasāya-ātmikā buddhiḥ — resolute intelligence; amoghā dṛṣṭiḥ — unfailing vision; viśva-sṛṣṭi-sāmarthyam — capacity to create the universe.
Transliteration
Bhāgavatam — Transliteration
śrī-śuka uvāca
evaṁ purā dhāraṇayātma-yonir
naṣṭāṁ smṛtiṁ pratyavarudhya tuṣṭāt
tathā sasarjedam amogha-dṛṣṭir
yathāpyayāt prāg vyavasāya-buddhiḥ
Śrīdhara — Transliteration
dvitīye tu tataḥ sthūla-dhāraṇāto jitaṁ manaḥ | sarva-sākṣiṇi sarveśe viṣṇau dhāryam itīryate || dṛśyālambana-rūpaivam uktā vairāja-dhāraṇā | ihocyate tu tat-sādhyā sarvāntaryāmi-dhāraṇā || tatra tāvat pūrvokta-dhāraṇāyā avāntara-phalam āha—evaṁ yā dhāraṇā, tayā tuṣṭād dhareḥ purā pralaya-samaye naṣṭāṁ sṛṣṭi-smṛtiṁ pratyavarudhya labdhvā, brahmā apyayāt prāg idaṁ viśvaṁ yathā āsīt tathā sasarja | vyavasāyātmikā buddhir yasya saḥ | ata eva amoghā dṛṣṭir yasyeti | atas tayā dhāraṇayā viśva-sṛṣṭi-sāmarthyaṁ bhavati ||1||
Śrīdhara Sanskrit
द्वितीये तु ततः स्थूल-धारणातो जितं मनः । सर्व-साक्षिणि सर्वेशे विष्णौ धार्यम् इतीर्यते ॥ दृश्यालम्बन-रूपैवम् उक्ता वैराज-धारणा । इहोच्यते तु तत्-साध्या सर्वान्तर्यामि-धारणा ॥ तत्र तावत् पूर्वोक्त-धारणाया अवान्तर-फलम् आह—एवं या धारणा, तया तुष्टाद् धरेः पुरा प्रलय-समये नष्टां सृष्टि-स्मृतिं प्रत्यवरुध्य लब्ध्वा, ब्रह्मा अप्ययात् प्रागिदं विश्वं यथा आसीत् तथा ससर्ज व्यवसायात्मिका बुद्धिर् यस्य सः । अत एव अमोघा दृष्टिर् यस्येति। अतस् तया धारणया विश्व-सृष्टि-सामर्थ्यं भवति ॥१॥

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. In the second chapter, the mind conquered by the preceding meditation on the gross form is to be fixed on Viṣṇu, the Lord and witness of all. The meditation on the Virāṭ, which uses a visible support, has been described; now the meditation on the indwelling Lord of all, attained through it, is taught. First he states an intermediate fruit of the earlier meditation. At the dissolution Brahmā had lost his memory of creation. By this concentration, and through Hari’s satisfaction, he recovered that memory and created the universe just as it had existed before dissolution. His intelligence was resolute and his vision unfailing. Thus this concentration can bestow the capacity for cosmic creation.

ŚB 2.2.2

शाब्दस्य हि ब्रह्मण एष पन्था
यन्नामभिर्ध्यायति धीरपार्थै: ।
परिभ्रमंस्तत्र न विन्दतेऽर्थान्
मायामये वासनया शयान: ॥ २ ॥

The way of presentation of the Vedic sounds is so bewildering that it directs the intelligence of the people to meaningless things like the heavenly kingdoms. The conditioned souls hover in dreams of such heavenly illusory pleasures, but actually they do not relish any tangible happiness in such places.

Word-for-word
Bhāgavatam — Word-for-word
śābdasya — of the Vedic sound; hi — certainly; brahmaṇaḥ — of the Vedas; eṣaḥ — these; panthāḥ — the way; yat — what is; nāmabhiḥ — by different names; dhyāyati — ponders; dhīḥ — intelligence; apārthaiḥ — by meaningless ideas; paribhraman — wandering; tatra — there; na — never; vindate — enjoys; arthān — realities; māyā-maye — in illusory things; vāsanayā — by different desires; śayānaḥ — as if dreaming in sleep.
Śrīdhara — Word-for-word
upāsanā-phalāt api viraktasya — for one detached even from the fruit of worship; śuddha-ātma-dhāraṇāyām adhikāraḥ — there is qualification for meditation on the pure Self; vairāgya-artham — for the sake of detachment; sarvam karma-phalam nindati — he censures all results of ritual action; śābdam brahma — the Veda made of sound; asya eṣa panthāḥ — this is its mode; karma-phala-bodha-prakāraḥ — the manner of presenting ritual fruits; apārthaiḥ artha-śūnyaiḥ — by empty, meaningless terms; svarga-ādi-nāmabhiḥ — by names such as heaven; dhīḥ dhyāyati — the intelligence contemplates; tat-tad-icchām karoti — forms desire for those things; māyā-maye pathi — on the path made of illusion; vāsanayā śayānaḥ — lying asleep through desire; svapnān paśyan iva — as if seeing dreams; arthān na vindati — does not obtain real objects; niravadyam sukham na labhate — does not obtain faultless happiness.
Transliteration
Bhāgavatam — Transliteration
śābdasya hi brahmaṇa eṣa panthā
yan nāmabhir dhyāyati dhīr apārthaiḥ
paribhramaṁs tatra na vindate ’rthān
māyāmaye vāsanayā śayānaḥ
Śrīdhara — Transliteration
upāsanā-phalād api viraktasya śuddhātma-dhāraṇāyām adhikāraḥ | ato vairāgyārthaṁ sarvaṁ karma-phalaṁ nindati | śābdaṁ śabda-mayaṁ brahma vedaḥ | tasya eṣa panthāḥ karma-phala-bodha-prakāraḥ | ko ’sau? apārthair artha-śūnyair eva svargādi-nāmabhiḥ sādhakasya dhīr dhyāyati tat-tad-icchāṁ karotīti yat | apārthatvam evāha—tatra māyāmaye pathi sukham iti vāsanayā śayānaḥ svapnān paśyann iva paribhramann arthān na vindati | tat-tal-lokaṁ prāpto ’pi niravadyaṁ sukhaṁ na labhata ity arthaḥ ||2||
Śrīdhara Sanskrit
उपासना-फलाद् अपि विरक्तस्य शुद्धात्म-धारणायाम् अधिकारः । अतो वैराग्यार्थं सर्वं कर्म-फलं निन्दति । शाब्दं शब्द-मयं ब्रह्म वेद । तस्य एष पन्थाः कर्म-फल-बोध-प्रकारः । कोऽसौ ? अपार्थैर् अर्थ-शून्यैर् एव स्वर्गादि-नामभिः साधकस्य धीर् ध्यायति तत्-तद्-इच्छां करोतीति यत् । अपार्थत्वम् एवाह—तत्र मायामये पथि सुखम् इति वासनया शयानः स्वप्नान् पश्यन्न् इव परिभ्रमन्नर्थान् न विन्दति । तत्-तल्-लोकं प्राप्तोऽपि निरवद्यं सुखं न लभत इत्य् अर्थः ॥२॥

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. One who is detached even from the fruits of worship becomes qualified for meditation on the pure Self. Therefore, to establish detachment, he censures all fruits of ritual action. The “verbal Brahman” is the Veda, and this is its way of presenting ritual results: by names such as heaven, which in this context are empty of lasting substance, the practitioner’s intelligence contemplates them and forms desires for them. Wandering on that illusory path, asleep in the latent desire for happiness as if seeing dreams, one does not obtain the real object. Even after attaining those worlds, one does not obtain faultless happiness.

ŚB 2.2.3

अत: कविर्नामसु यावदर्थ:
स्यादप्रमत्तो व्यवसायबुद्धि: ।
सिद्धेऽन्यथार्थे न यतेत तत्र
परिश्रमं तत्र समीक्षमाण: ॥ ३ ॥

For this reason the enlightened person should endeavor only for the minimum necessities of life while in the world of names. He should be intelligently fixed and never endeavor for unwanted things, being competent to perceive practically that all such endeavors are merely hard labor for nothing.

Word-for-word
Bhāgavatam — Word-for-word
ataḥ — for this reason; kaviḥ — the enlightened person; nāmasu — in names only; yāvat — minimum; arthaḥ — necessity; syāt — must be; apramattaḥ — without being mad after them; vyavasāya-buddhiḥ — intelligently fixed; siddhe — for success; anyathā — otherwise; arthe — in the interest of; na — should never; yateta — endeavor for; tatra — there; pariśramam — laboring hard; tatra — there; samīkṣamāṇaḥ — one who sees practically.
Śrīdhara — Word-for-word
sarvathā karma-phala-tyāge — if all fruits of action are abandoned; sadyaḥ deha-pātaḥ syāt — immediate bodily collapse might be feared; ataḥ kaviḥ — therefore the wise person; nāmasu nāma-mātreṣu bhogyeṣu — among enjoyments that are mere names; yāvat-arthena arthaḥ — having only as much purpose as necessary; deha-nirvāhaḥ — maintenance of the body; apramattaḥ — not heedless; tāvat-mātre api anāsaktaḥ — unattached even to that minimum; vyavasāya-buddhiḥ — resolute intelligence; na idam sukham iti niścayavān — certain that “this is not happiness”; anyathā eva siddhe sati — when the purpose is achieved otherwise; tatra yatne — in effort for it; pariśramam paśyan — seeing only toil; na yateta — should not strive.
Transliteration
Bhāgavatam — Transliteration
ataḥ kavir nāmasu yāvad arthaḥ
syād apramatto vyavasāya-buddhiḥ
siddhe ’nyathārthe na yateta tatra
pariśramaṁ tatra samīkṣamāṇaḥ
Śrīdhara — Transliteration
tarhi sarvathā karma-phala-tyāge sadya eva deha-pātaḥ syād ity āśaṅkyāha | ataḥ kavir nāmasu nāma-mātreṣu bhogyeṣu yāvatārthenārtho deha-nirvāho yasya sa tathā-bhūtaḥ syāt | apramattas tāvan-mātre ’py anāsaktaḥ | vyavasāya-buddhir nedaṁ sukham iti niścayavān | tadāpi tatra tasminn arthe ’nyathaiva siddhe sati tatra yatne pariśramaṁ paśyan na yateta ||3||
Śrīdhara Sanskrit
तर्हि सर्वथा कर्म-फल-त्यागे सद्य एव देह-पातः स्याद् इत्य् आशङ्क्याह । अतः कविर् नामसु नाम-मात्रेषु भोग्येषु यावतार्थेनार्थो देह-निर्वाहो यस्य स तथा-भूतः स्यात् । अप्रमत्तस् तावन्-मात्रेऽप्य् अनासक्तः । व्यवसाय-बुद्धिर् नेदं सुखम् इति निश्चयवान्। तदापि तत्र तस्मिन्न् अर्थेऽन्यथैव सिद्धे सति तत्र यत्ने परिश्रमं पश्यन् न यतेत ॥३॥

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. One might object that abandoning all fruits of action would immediately cause the body to perish. He therefore says that the wise person should accept among these merely named enjoyments only as much as is required to maintain the body. Even toward that minimum he should remain unattached and resolutely know, “This is not happiness.” When the necessary purpose can be accomplished in another way, he should not strive for it after seeing that such striving is merely labor.

ŚB 2.2.4

सत्यां क्षितौ किं कशिपो: प्रयासै-
र्बाहौ स्वसिद्धे ह्युपबर्हणै: किम् ।
सत्यञ्जलौ किं पुरुधान्नपात्र्या
दिग्वल्कलादौ सति किं दुकूलै: ॥ ४ ॥

When there are ample earthly flats to lie on, what is the necessity of cots and beds? When one can use his own arms, what is the necessity of a pillow? When one can use the palms of his hands, what is the necessity of varieties of utensils? When there is ample covering, or the skins of trees, what is the necessity of clothing?

Word-for-word
Bhāgavatam — Word-for-word
satyām — being in possession; kṣitau — earthly flats; kim — where is the necessity; kaśipoḥ — of beds and cots; prayāsaiḥ — endeavoring for; bāhau — the arms; sva-siddhe — being self-sufficient; hi — certainly; upabarhaṇaiḥ — bed and bedstead; kim — what is the use; sati — being present; añjalau — the palms of the hands; kim — what is the use; purudhā — varieties of; anna — eatables; pātryā — by the utensils; dik — open space; valkala-ādau — skins of trees; sati — being existent; kim — what is the use of; dukūlaiḥ — clothes.
Śrīdhara — Word-for-word
anyathā-siddhim āha — he states that the need can be met otherwise; satyām iti dvābhyām — by two verses beginning satyām; kaśipoḥ — of a bed; śayyāyāḥ — of a couch; svataḥ siddhe bāhau — when one’s own arm is naturally available; upabarhaṇaiḥ — by pillows; ucchīrṣakaiḥ — head-rests; purudhā — in many ways; anna-pātryā — by a food vessel; bhojana-pātreṇa — by an eating utensil.
Transliteration
Bhāgavatam — Transliteration
satyāṁ kṣitau kiṁ kaśipoḥ prayāsair
bāhau svasiddhe hy upabarhaṇaiḥ kim
saty añjalau kiṁ purudhānna-pātryā
dig-valkalādau sati kiṁ dukūlaiḥ
Śrīdhara — Transliteration
anyathā-siddhim āha dvābhyām—satyām iti | kaśipoḥ śayyāyāḥ | svataḥ siddhe bāhau sati upabarhaṇair ucchīrṣakaiḥ | purudhā bahu-prakārayā anna-pātryā bhojana-pātreṇa ||4||
Śrīdhara Sanskrit
अन्यथा-सिद्धिम् आह द्वाभ्याम्—सत्याम् इति । कशिपोः शय्यायाः । स्वतः सिद्धे बाहौ सति उपबर्हणैर् उच्छीर्षकैः । पुरुधा बहु-प्रकारया अन्न-पात्र्या भोजन-पात्रेण ॥४॥

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. In this and the following verse he shows that these bodily needs can be met in other ways. Kaśipa means a bed or couch. When one’s own arm is naturally available, what need is there for pillows or head-rests? Purudhā means “in many ways,” and the food-vessel is simply an eating utensil.

ŚB 2.2.5

चीराणि किं पथि न सन्ति दिशन्ति भिक्षां
नैवाङ्‌घ्रिपा: परभृत: सरितोऽप्यशुष्यन् ।
रुद्धा गुहा: किमजितोऽवति नोपसन्नान्
कस्माद् भजन्ति कवयो धनदुर्मदान्धान् ॥ ५ ॥

Are there no torn clothes lying on the common road? Do the trees, which exist for maintaining others, no longer give alms in charity? Do the rivers, being dried up, no longer supply water to the thirsty? Are the caves of the mountains now closed? Or above all, does the Almighty Lord not protect the fully surrendered souls? Why then do the learned sages go to flatter those who are intoxicated by hard-earned wealth?

Word-for-word
Bhāgavatam — Word-for-word
cīrāṇi — torn clothes; kim — whether; pathi — on the road; na — not; santi — there is; diśanti — give in charity; bhikṣām — alms; na — not; eva — also; aṅghripāḥ — the trees; para-bhṛtaḥ — one who maintains others; saritaḥ — the rivers; api — also; aśuṣyan — have dried up; ruddhāḥ — closed; guhāḥ — caves; kim — whether; ajitaḥ — the Almighty Lord; avati — give protection; na — not; upasannān — the surrendered soul; kasmāt — what for, then; bhajanti — flatters; kavayaḥ — the learned; dhana — wealth; durmada-andhān — too intoxicated by.
Śrīdhara — Word-for-word
dik-sad-bhāvaḥ — the availability of the directions/open space; nāma nagna-tvam eva — would amount merely to nakedness; valkalam annam toyam vāsa-sthānam — bark, food, water, and a dwelling place; yācñā-prayatnam vinā — without effort in begging; cīrāṇi vastra-khaṇḍāni — torn pieces of cloth; parān bibhṛti puṣṇanti — they support and nourish others; phalādibhiḥ — with fruits and the like; guhāḥ giri-daryaḥ — caves are mountain hollows; ajitaḥ hariḥ — Hari, the unconquered Lord; upasannān śaraṇāgatān — those who approach Him in surrender; avati rakṣati — protects; dhana-durmadena andhān — blinded by the arrogance produced by wealth; naṣṭa-vivekān — those whose discrimination is lost.
Transliteration
Bhāgavatam — Transliteration
cīrāṇi kiṁ pathi na santi diśanti bhikṣāṁ
naivāṅghripāḥ para-bhṛtaḥ sarito ’py aśuṣyan
ruddhā guhāḥ kim ajito ’vati nopasannān
kasmād bhajanti kavayo dhana-durmadāndhān
Śrīdhara — Transliteration
nanu dik-sad-bhāvo nāma nagnatvam eva, valkalam annaṁ toyaṁ vāsa-sthānaṁ tu yācñā-prayatnaṁ vinā kathaṁ prāpyeta? tatrāha | cīrāṇi vastra-khaṇḍāni | parān bibhrati puṣṇanti phalādibhir ye | guhā giri-daryaḥ | nanu kadācid eṣām alābhe kiṁ kāryam? tatrāha—ajito harir upasannān śaraṇāgatān kiṁ nāvati na rakṣati? kiṁ-śabdasya pūrvatrāpi sambandhaḥ | dhanena yo durmadas tena andhān naṣṭa-vivekān ||5||
Śrīdhara Sanskrit
ननु दिक्-सद्-भावो नाम नग्नत्वम् एव, वल्कलम् अन्नं तोयं वास-स्थानं तु याच्ञा-प्रयत्नं विना कथं प्राप्येत ? तत्राह । चीराणि वस्त्र-खण्डानि । परान् बिभ्रति पुष्णन्ति फलादिभिर् ये । गुहाः गिरि-दर्यः । ननु कदाचिद् एषाम् अलाभे किं कार्यं ? तत्राह—अजितो हरिर् उपसन्नान् शरणागतान् किं नावति न रक्षति ? किं-शब्दस्य पूर्वत्रापि सम्बन्धः । धनेन यो दुर्मदस् तेन अन्धान् नष्ट-विवेकान् ॥५॥

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. One might object that merely having the directions available would amount to nakedness, and ask how bark, food, water, and shelter can be obtained without the effort of begging. He answers that cīrāṇi are discarded pieces of cloth; trees support others with fruits and the like; and caves are hollows in mountains. If even these are unavailable, does Hari, the unconquered Lord, not protect those who have approached Him in surrender? The interrogative “kim” is understood with the preceding clauses as well. The wealthy people in the final line are blinded by the arrogance produced by wealth and have lost discrimination.

ŚB 2.2.6

एवं स्वचित्ते स्वत एव सिद्ध
आत्मा प्रियोऽर्थो भगवाननन्त: ।
तं निर्वृतो नियतार्थो भजेत
संसारहेतूपरमश्च यत्र ॥ ६ ॥

Thus being fixed, one must render service unto the Supersoul situated in one’s own heart by His omnipotency. Because He is the Almighty Personality of Godhead, eternal and unlimited, He is the ultimate goal of life, and by worshiping Him one can end the cause of the conditioned state of existence.

Word-for-word
Bhāgavatam — Word-for-word
evam — thus; sva-citte — in one’s own heart; svataḥ — by His omnipotency; eva — certainly; siddhaḥ — fully represented; ātmā — the Supersoul; priyaḥ — very dear; arthaḥ — substance; bhagavān — the Supreme Personality of Godhead; anantaḥ — the eternal unlimited; tam — unto Him; nirvṛtaḥ — being detached from the world; niyata — permanent; arthaḥ — the supreme gain; bhajeta — one must worship; saṁsāra-hetu — the cause of the conditioned state of existence; uparamaḥ — cessation; ca — certainly; yatra — in which.
Śrīdhara — Word-for-word
tadā tena kim kartavyam — what then should such a person do?; hariḥ tu sevyaḥ — Hari alone is to be served; svacitte svataḥ eva siddhaḥ — naturally manifest in one’s own heart; yat ātmā — because He is the Self; ata eva priyaḥ — therefore dear; priyasya sevā sukha-rūpā eva — service to the beloved is itself happiness; arthaḥ satyaḥ — He is the real substance; na tu anātmavat mithyā — not unreal like what is non-self; bhagavān — the possessor of qualities worthy of worship; anantaḥ nityaḥ — unlimited and eternal; niyatārthaḥ — one whose true nature is ascertained; tad-anubhava-ānandena nirvṛtaḥ — satisfied by the bliss of experiencing Him; saṁsāra-hetoḥ avidyāyā uparamaḥ — the cessation of ignorance, the cause of saṁsāra.
Transliteration
Bhāgavatam — Transliteration
evaṁ sva-citte svata eva siddha
ātmā priyo ’rtho bhagavān anantaḥ
taṁ nirvṛto niyatārtho bhajeta
saṁsāra-hetūparamaś ca yatra
Śrīdhara — Transliteration
tadā tena kiṁ kartavyam? haris tu sevya ity āha—evaṁ viraktaḥ saṁs taṁ bhajet | bhajanīyatve hetavaḥ—sva-citte svata eva siddhaḥ | yat ātmā, ata eva priyaḥ | priyasya ca sevā sukha-rūpaiva | arthaḥ satyo na tv anātmavan mithyā | bhagavān bhajanīya-guṇaś ca anantaś ca nityaḥ | ya evaṁbhūtas taṁ bhajet | niyatārtho niścita-svarūpaḥ | tad-anubhavānandena nirvṛtaḥ sann iti svataḥ-sukhātmatvaṁ darśitam | kiṁ ca, yatra yasmin bhajane sati saṁsāra-hetoḥ avidyāyā uparamo nāśo bhavati ||6||
Śrīdhara Sanskrit
तदा तेन किं कर्तव्यं ? हरिस् तु सेव्य इत्य् आह—एवं विरक्तः संस् तं भजेत । भजनीयत्वे हेतवः—स्व-चित्ते स्वत एव सिद्धः । यत आत्मा, अत एव प्रियः । प्रियस्य च सेवा सुख-रूपैव । अर्थः सत्यो न त्व् अनात्मवन् मिथ्या । भगवान् भजनीय-गुणश् च अनन्तश् च नित्यः । य एवंभूतस् तं भजेत । नियतार्थो निश्चित-स्वरूपः । तद्-अनुभवानन्देन निर्वृतः सन्न् इति स्वतः-सुखात्मत्वं दर्शितम् । किं च, यत्र यस्मिन् भजने सति संसार-हेतोर् अविद्याया उपरमो नाशो भवति ॥६॥

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. What should such a detached person do? Hari alone should be served. The reasons are given: He is naturally manifest in one’s own heart; because He is the Self, He is dear, and service to the beloved is itself happiness. He is the real substance, not unreal like the non-self; He is Bhagavān, possessed of qualities worthy of worship, and He is unlimited and eternal. With His true nature firmly ascertained, one becomes satisfied in the bliss of experiencing Him. Moreover, in such worship ignorance—the cause of saṁsāra—comes to an end.

ŚB 2.2.7

कस्तां त्वनाद‍ृत्य परानुचिन्ता-
मृते पशूनसतीं नाम कुर्यात् ।
पश्यञ्जनं पतितं वैतरण्यां
स्वकर्मजान् परितापाञ्जुषाणम् ॥ ७ ॥

Who else but the gross materialists will neglect such transcendental thought and take to the nonpermanent names only, seeing the mass of people fallen in the river of suffering as the consequence of accruing the result of their own work?

Word-for-word
Bhāgavatam — Word-for-word
kaḥ — who else; tām — that; tu — but; anādṛtya — by neglecting; para-anucintām — transcendental thoughts; ṛte — without; paśūn — the materialists; asatīm — in the nonpermanent; nāma — name; kuryāt — will adopt; paśyan — seeing definitely; janam — the general mass of people; patitam — fallen; vaitaraṇyām — in Vaitaraṇī, the river of suffering; sva-karma-jān — produced from one’s own work; paritāpān — suffering; juṣāṇam — being overtaken by.
Śrīdhara — Word-for-word
etat eva — this very point; anya-cintā-nindayā dṛḍhayati — he strengthens by condemning other thought; parasyā hareḥ anucintām — meditation on the Supreme Hari; dhāraṇām — concentration; anādṛtya — disregarding; paśūn karma-jaḍān vinā — except animal-like people stupefied by action; asatīm viṣaya-cintām — unreal thought of sense objects; kaḥ nāma kuryāt — who indeed would perform it?; vaitaraṇyām patitam — fallen in Vaitaraṇī; svakarma-jān — born of one’s own deeds; ādhyātmikādi-kleśān — sufferings beginning with those arising from oneself; sevamānam janam — people undergoing them; saṁsṛtiḥ vaitaraṇī — saṁsāra is compared to Vaitaraṇī.
Transliteration
Bhāgavatam — Transliteration
kas tāṁ tv anādṛtya parānucintām
ṛte paśūn asatīṁ nāma kuryāt
paśyañ janaṁ patitaṁ vaitaraṇyāṁ
sva-karmajān paritāpāñ juṣāṇam
Śrīdhara — Transliteration
etad evānya-cintā-nindayā dṛḍhayati—ka iti | tāṁ tathā-bhūtāṁ parasya harer anucintāṁ dhāraṇām anādṛtya paśūn karma-jaḍān vinā | yathā, paśur evāyaṁ sa devānām iti śruteḥ | asatīṁ viṣaya-cintāṁ ko nāma kuryāt? tayā cintayā vaitaraṇyāṁ patitaṁ, tatra ca sva-karmajān ādhyātmikādi-kleśān sevamānaṁ janaṁ paśyan | vaitaraṇī yamasya dvāra-sthā nadī tat-tulyatvāt saṁsṛtir vaitaraṇī ||7||
Śrīdhara Sanskrit
एतद् एवान्य-चिन्ता-निन्दया द्रढयति—क इति । तां तथा-भूतां परस्य हरेर् अनुचिन्तां धारणाम् अनादृत्य पशून् कर्म-जडान् विना । यथा, पशुर् एवायं स देवानाम् इति श्रुतेः । असतीं विषय-चिन्तां को नाम कुर्यात् ? तया चिन्तया वैतरण्यां पतितं, तत्र च स्व-कर्मजान् आध्यात्मिकादि-क्लेशान् सेवमानं जनं पश्यन् । वैतरणी यमस्य द्वार-स्था नदी तत्-तुल्यत्वात् संसृतिर् वैतरणी ॥७॥

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. He strengthens the preceding point by condemning other kinds of thought. Who, apart from people made animal-like and inert by karma, would disregard this meditation on the Supreme Hari and instead cultivate unreal thought of sense objects? One can see people fallen into the Vaitaraṇī-like stream of saṁsāra and undergoing sufferings born from their own actions. Vaitaraṇī is the river at Yama’s gate; saṁsāra receives that name here because of its similarity to it.

ŚB 2.2.8

केचित् स्वदेहान्तर्हृदयावकाशे
प्रादेशमात्रं पुरुषं वसन्तम् ।
चतुर्भुजं कञ्जरथाङ्गशङ्ख-
गदाधरं धारणया स्मरन्ति ॥ ८ ॥

Others conceive of the Personality of Godhead residing within the body in the region of the heart and measuring only eight inches, with four hands carrying a lotus, a wheel of a chariot, a conchshell and a club respectively.

Word-for-word
Bhāgavatam — Word-for-word
kecit — others; sva-deha-antaḥ — within the body; hṛdaya-avakāśe — in the region of the heart; prādeśa-mātram — measuring only eight inches; puruṣam — the Personality of Godhead; vasantam — residing; catuḥ-bhujam — with four hands; kañja — lotus; ratha-aṅga — the wheel of a chariot; śaṅkha — conchshell; gadā-dharam — and with a club in the hand; dhāraṇayā — conceiving in that way; smaranti — do meditate upon Him.
Śrīdhara — Word-for-word
tām eva dhāraṇām — that very concentration; sa-viśeṣaṇām āha — he describes together with its attributes; svadehasya antar-madhye — within the interior of one’s own body; hṛdayam — the heart; tatra yaḥ avakāśaḥ — the space there; tasmin vasantam — dwelling in that space; prādeśaḥ — a span; tarjany-aṅguṣṭhayoḥ vistāraḥ — the distance between forefinger and thumb; sa eva mātrā pramāṇam — that is the measure; hṛdaya-parimāṇam tatra upacaryate — the heart’s measurement is figuratively applied to Him there; kañjam padmam — lotus; rathāṅgam cakram — the chariot wheel is the disc.
Transliteration
Bhāgavatam — Transliteration
kecit sva-dehāntar-hṛdayāvakāśe
prādeśa-mātraṁ puruṣaṁ vasantam
catur-bhujaṁ kañja-rathāṅga-śaṅkha-
gadā-dharaṁ dhāraṇayā smaranti
Śrīdhara — Transliteration
tām eva dhāraṇāṁ sa-viśeṣaṇām āha—kecid iti ṣaḍbhiḥ | sva-dehasyāntar madhye tad hṛdayaṁ tatra yo’vakāśas tasmin vasantam | prādeśas tarjany-aṅguṣṭhayor vistāraḥ, sa eva mātrā pramāṇaṁ yasyeti hṛdaya-parimāṇaṁ tatropacaryate | kañjaṁ padmam | rathāṅgaṁ cakram ||8||
Śrīdhara Sanskrit
ताम् एव धारणां स-विशेषणाम् आह—केचिद् इति षड्भिः । स्व-देहस्यान्तर् मध्ये तद् हृदयं तत्र योऽवकाशस् तस्मिन् वसन्तम् । प्रादेशस् तर्जन्य्-अङ्गुष्ठयोर् विस्तारः, स एव मात्रा प्रमाणं यस्येति हृदय-परिमाणं तत्रोपचर्यते । कञ्जं पद्मम् । रथाङ्गं चक्रम् ॥८॥

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. He now describes this same concentration together with its specific features, in six verses beginning with kecit. The Lord dwells in the space of the heart within one’s body. A prādeśa is the span between the forefinger and thumb; the measurement of the heart is figuratively attributed to the Lord who resides there. Kañja means lotus, and rathāṅga means the discus.

ŚB 2.2.9

प्रसन्नवक्त्रं नलिनायतेक्षणं
कदम्बकिञ्जल्कपिशङ्गवाससम् ।
लसन्महारत्नहिरण्मयाङ्गदं
स्फुरन्महारत्नकिरीटकुण्डलम् ॥ ९ ॥

His mouth expresses His happiness. His eyes spread like the petals of a lotus, and His garments, yellowish like the saffron of a kadamba flower, are bedecked with valuable jewels. His ornaments are all made of gold, set with jewels, and He wears a glowing headdress and earrings.

Word-for-word
Bhāgavatam — Word-for-word
prasanna — expresses happiness; vaktram — mouth; nalina-āyata — spread like the petals of a lotus; īkṣaṇam — eyes; kadamba — kadamba flower; kiñjalka — saffron; piśaṅga — yellow; vāsasam — garments; lasat — hanging; mahā-ratna — valuable jewels; hiraṇmaya — made of gold; aṅgadam — ornament; sphurat — glowing; mahā-ratna — valuable jewels; kirīṭa — head-dress; kuṇḍalam — earrings.
Śrīdhara — Word-for-word
nalinam padmam — lotus; tadvat prasanne āyate īkṣaṇe — eyes clear and elongated like it; kadamba-kusumasya kiñjalkāḥ — the filaments of a kadamba flower; kesarāḥ — its saffron-like stamens; tadvat piśaṅge pīte vāsasī — garments yellow like those stamens; lasanti mahā-ratnāni yeṣu — in which great jewels shine; suvarṇa-mayāni aṅgadāni — golden armlets; sphuranti mahā-ratnāni — great jewels sparkle; tan-mayāni kirīṭa-kuṇḍalāni — crown and earrings made with them.
Transliteration
Bhāgavatam — Transliteration
prasanna-vaktraṁ nalināyatekṣaṇaṁ
kadamba-kiñjalka-piśaṅga-vāsasam
lasan-mahā-ratna-hiraṇmayāṅgadaṁ
sphuran-mahā-ratna-kirīṭa-kuṇḍalam
Śrīdhara — Transliteration
nalinaṁ padmaṁ tadvat prasanne āyate dīrghe īkṣaṇe yasya | kadamba-kusumasya kiñjalkāḥ kesarās tadvat piśaṅge pīte vāsasī yasya | lasanti mahā-ratnāni yeṣu tāni suvarṇa-mayāny aṅgadāni yasya | sphuranti ca tāni mahā-ratnāni ca tan-mayāni kirīṭa-kuṇḍalāni yasya ||9||
Śrīdhara Sanskrit
नलिनं पद्मं तद्वत् प्रसन्ने आयते दीर्घे ईक्षणे यस्य । कदम्ब-कुसुमस्य किञ्जल्काः केसरास् तद्वत् पिशङ्गे पीते वाससी यस्य । लसन्ति महा-रत्नानि येषु तानि सुवर्णमयान्य् अङ्गदानि यस्य । स्फुरन्ति च तानि महा-रत्नानि च तन्-मयानि किरीट-कुण्डलानि यस्य ॥९॥

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. His eyes are clear and elongated like a lotus. The yellow garments resemble the saffron-colored filaments of a kadamba flower. Great jewels shine in His golden armlets, and His crown and earrings are fashioned with sparkling precious jewels.

ŚB 2.2.10

उन्निद्रहृत्पङ्कजकर्णिकालये
योगेश्वरास्थापितपादपल्लवम् ।
श्रीलक्षणं कौस्तुभरत्नकन्धर-
मम्‍लानलक्ष्म्या वनमालयाचितम् ॥ १० ॥

His lotus feet are placed over the whorls of the lotuslike hearts of great mystics. On His chest is the Kaustubha jewel, engraved with a beautiful calf, and there are other jewels on His shoulders. His complete torso is garlanded with fresh flowers.

Word-for-word
Bhāgavatam — Word-for-word
unnidra — blooming; hṛt — heart; paṅkaja — lotus flower; karṇikā-ālaye — on the surface of the whorl; yoga-īśvara — the great mystics; āsthāpita — placed; pāda-pallavam — lotus feet; śrī — the goddess of fortune, or a beautiful calf; lakṣaṇam — marked in that way; kaustubha — the Kaustubha jewel; ratna — other jewels; kandharam — on the shoulder; amlāna — quite fresh; lakṣmyā — beauty; vana-mālayā — by a flower garland; ācitam — spread over.
Śrīdhara — Word-for-word
unnidram — fully opened; vikasitam — blossomed; hṛt-paṅkajam — the lotus of the heart; tasya karṇikā — its pericarp; eva ālayaḥ sthānam — itself the abode, the place; tasmin — in it; yogaiśvaraiḥ — by masters of yoga; āsthāpitau — established; pāda-pallavau — the tender lotus feet; yasya — whose; śrīḥ eva lakṣma — Śrī herself is the mark; cihnam — sign; tad-yuktam — endowed with that; kaustubha-ratnam — the Kaustubha jewel; kandharāyām — on the neck/shoulder; yasya — whose; amlānā lakṣmīḥ — unfading beauty; śobhā — splendor; vanamālayā — by the forest-flower garland; ācitam vyāptam — covered, pervaded.
Transliteration
Bhāgavatam — Transliteration
unnidra-hṛt-paṅkaja-karṇikālaye
yogeśvarāsthāpita-pāda-pallavam
śrī-lakṣaṇaṁ kaustubha-ratna-kandharam
amlāna-lakṣmyā vana-mālayācitam
Śrīdhara — Transliteration
unnidraṁ vikasitaṁ yad hṛt-paṅkajaṁ, tasya karṇikaiva ālayaḥ sthānaṁ, tasmin | yogeśvarair āsthāpitau pāda-pallavau yasya | śrīr eva lakṣma cihnaṁ tad-yuktam | pāmādi-vihito matv-arthīyo na-pratyayaḥ | kaustubha-ratnaṁ kandharāyāṁ yasya | amlānā lakṣmīḥ śobhā yasyās tayā vana-mālayācitaṁ vyāptam ||10||
Śrīdhara Sanskrit
उन्निद्रं विकसितं यद् हृत्-पङ्कजं, तस्य कर्णिका एव आलयः स्थानं, तस्मिन् । योगेश्वरैर् आस्थापितौ पाद-पल्लवौ यस्य । श्रीर् एव लक्ष्म चिह्नं तद्-युक्तम् । पामादि-विहितो मत्व्-अर्थीयो न-प्रत्ययः । कौस्तुभ-रत्नं कन्धरायां यस्य । अम्लाना लक्ष्मीः शोभा यस्यास् तया वन-मालया आचितं व्याप्तम् ॥१०॥

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. The lotus of the heart is fully blossomed, and its pericarp is the Lord’s dwelling place. His tender lotus feet are established there by the great masters of yoga. He bears the auspicious mark of Śrī and the Kaustubha jewel upon His neck. His unfading splendor is spread over His form by the fresh forest-flower garland.

ŚB 2.2.11

विभूषितं मेखलयाङ्गुलीयकै-
र्महाधनैर्नूपुरकङ्कणादिभि: ।
स्निग्धामलाकुञ्चितनीलकुन्तलै-
र्विरोचमानाननहासपेशलम् ॥ ११ ॥

He is well decorated with an ornamental wreath about His waist and rings studded with valuable jewels on His fingers. His leglets, His bangles, His oiled hair, curling with a bluish tint, and His beautiful smiling face are all very pleasing.

Word-for-word
Bhāgavatam — Word-for-word

Śrīdhara Sanskrit
स्निग्धत्वादि-विशिष्टैः कुन्तलैर् विरोचमाने आनने यो हासस् तेन पेशलं सुन्दरम् ॥११॥

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. His face shines with locks of hair characterized by glossiness and the other described qualities; the smile upon that radiant face makes it especially charming and beautiful.

ŚB 2.2.12

अदीनलीलाहसितेक्षणोल्लसद्-
भ्रूभङ्गसंसूचितभूर्यनुग्रहम् ।
ईक्षेत चिन्तामयमेनमीश्वरं
यावन्मनो धारणयावतिष्ठते ॥ १२ ॥

The Lord’s magnanimous pastimes and the glowing glancing of His smiling face are all indications of His extensive benedictions. One must therefore concentrate on this transcendental form of the Lord, as long as the mind can be fixed on Him by meditation.

Word-for-word
Bhāgavatam — Word-for-word
adīna — very magnanimous; līlā — pastimes; hasita — smiling; īkṣaṇa — by glancing over; ullasat — glowing; bhrū-bhaṅga — signals of the eyebrow; saṁsūcita — indicated; bhūri — extensive; anugraham — benediction; īkṣeta — one must concentrate on; cintāmayam — transcendental; enam — this particular; īśvaram — the Supreme Lord; yāvat — as long as; manaḥ — the mind; dhāraṇayā — by meditation; avatiṣṭhate — can be fixed.
Śrīdhara — Word-for-word
adīnam — not mean, magnanimous; udāram — generous; yat-līlā-hasitam — whose sportive smile; tena yat īkṣaṇam — the glance accompanying it; tasmin ullasantaḥ — shining forth in that; bhrū-bhaṅgāḥ — movements of the brows; bhrū-vikṣepāḥ — gestures of the eyebrows; taiḥ saṁsūcitaḥ — indicated by them; bhūriḥ anugrahaḥ — abundant grace; yena — by whom; cintāmayam — made manifest through contemplation; cintayā āvirbhavantam — appearing through meditation.
Transliteration
Bhāgavatam — Transliteration
adīna-līlā-hasitekṣaṇollasad-
bhrū-bhaṅga-saṁsūcita-bhūry-anugraham
īkṣeta cintāmayam enam īśvaraṁ
yāvan mano dhāraṇayāvatiṣṭhate
Śrīdhara — Transliteration
adīnam udāraṁ yal-līlā-hasitaṁ tena yad īkṣaṇaṁ tasminn ullasanto yo bhrū-bhaṅgā bhrū-vikṣepās taiḥ saṁsūcito bhūrir anugraho yena | cintāmayaṁ cintayā āvirbhavantam ||12||
Śrīdhara Sanskrit
अदीनम् उदारं यल्-लीला-हसितं तेन यद् ईक्षणं तस्मिन्न् उल्लसन्तो यो भ्रू-भङ्गा भ्रू-विक्षेपास् तैः संसूचितो भूरिर् अनुग्रहो येन । चिन्तामयं चिन्तया आविर्भवन्तम् ॥१२॥

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. His sportive smile is magnanimous. The glance accompanying that smile shines together with movements of His eyebrows, and those gestures indicate His abundant grace. Cintāmayam means the Lord who becomes manifest through contemplation.

ŚB 2.2.13

एकैकशोऽङ्गानि धियानुभावयेत्
पादादि यावद्धसितं गदाभृत: ।
जितं जितं स्थानमपोह्य धारयेत्
परं परं शुद्ध्यति धीर्यथा यथा ॥ १३ ॥

The process of meditation should begin from the lotus feet of the Lord and progress to His smiling face. The meditation should be concentrated upon the lotus feet, then the calves, then the thighs, and in this way higher and higher. The more the mind becomes fixed upon the different parts of the limbs, one after another, the more the intelligence becomes purified.

Word-for-word
Bhāgavatam — Word-for-word
eka-ekaśaḥ — one to one, or one after another; aṅgāni — limbs; dhiyā — by attention; anubhāvayet — meditate upon; pāda-ādi — legs, etc.; yāvat — until; hasitam — smiling; gadā-bhṛtaḥ — the Personality of Godhead; jitam jitam — gradually controlling the mind; sthānam — place; apohya — leaving; dhārayet — meditate upon; param param — higher and higher; śuddhyati — purified; dhīḥ — intelligence; yathā yathā — as much as.
Śrīdhara — Word-for-word
asya eva dhyānam āha — he now describes this very meditation; ekaikaśaḥ — one by one; anubhāvayet dhyāyet — should contemplate, meditate upon; yat yat jitam — whichever has been mastered; ayatnataḥ sphuritam — which appears effortlessly; pāda-gulpha-ādi-sthānam avayavaḥ — a limb such as the foot, ankle and so on; tat tat apohya tyaktvā — leaving each of those behind; param param — the next higher one; jaṅghā-jānu-ādi — shank, knee and so forth; dhārayet dhyāyet — should fix upon, should meditate upon; śudhyati — becomes purified; tasmin niścalā bhavati — becomes motionless upon it.
Transliteration
Bhāgavatam — Transliteration
ekaikaśo ’ṅgāni dhiyānubhāvayet
pādādi yāvad dhasitaṁ gadābhṛtaḥ
jitaṁ jitaṁ sthānam apohya dhārayet
paraṁ paraṁ śuddhyati dhīr yathā yathā
Śrīdhara — Transliteration
asyaiva dhyānam āha—ekaikaśa iti | anubhāvayed dhyāyet | yad yaj jitam ayatnataḥ sphuritaṁ pāda-gulphādi-sthānam avayavas tat tad apohya tyaktvā paraṁ paraṁ jaṅghā-jānvādi dhārayed dhyāyet | śudhyati tasmin niścalā bhavati ||13||
Śrīdhara Sanskrit
अस्यैव ध्यानम् आह—एकैकश इति । अनुभावयेद् ध्यायेत् । यद् यज् जितम् अयत्नतः स्फुरितं पादगुल्फादि-स्थानम् अवयवस् तत् तद् अपोह्य त्यक्त्वा परं परं जङ्घाजान्वादि धारयेद् ध्यायेत् । शुध्यति तस्मिन् निश्चला भवति ॥१३॥

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. He now describes this very meditation. One should contemplate each limb one by one. When a particular limb—beginning with the feet and ankles—has been mastered and appears effortlessly, one should leave it and fix the mind on the next higher limb, such as the shanks or knees. As this proceeds, the intelligence is purified and becomes unwavering upon the object of meditation.

ŚB 2.2.14

यावन्न जायेत परावरेऽस्मिन्
विश्वेश्वरे द्रष्टरि भक्तियोग: ।
तावत् स्थवीय: पुरुषस्य रूपं
क्रियावसाने प्रयत: स्मरेत ॥ १४ ॥

Unless the gross materialist develops a sense of loving service unto the Supreme Lord, the seer of both the transcendental and material worlds, he should remember or meditate upon the universal form of the Lord at the end of his prescribed duties.

Word-for-word
Bhāgavatam — Word-for-word
yāvat — as long as; na — does not; jāyeta — develop; para — transcendental; avare — mundane; asmin — in this form of; viśva-īśvare — the Lord of all worlds; draṣṭari — unto the seer; bhakti-yogaḥ — devotional service; tāvat — so long; sthavīyaḥ — the gross materialist; puruṣasya — of the virāṭ-puruṣa; rūpam — universal form; kriyā-avasāne — at the end of one’s prescribed duties; prayataḥ — with proper attention; smareta — one should remember.
Śrīdhara — Word-for-word
pūrvokta-vairāja-dhāraṇāyāḥ — of the previously described Virāṭ meditation; etat dhāraṇā-aṅgatvam — this being a limb/preparatory part of the present meditation; pare brahma-ādayaḥ avare yasmāt — because Brahmā and the others are lower than Him; viśveśvare — in the Lord of the universe; draṣṭari — in the seer; na tu dṛśye — not in what is seen; caitanya-ghanatvāt — because He is concentrated consciousness; bhakti-yogaḥ — devotional yoga; prema-lakṣaṇaḥ — characterized by love; kriyā-avasāne — after the completion of necessary duties; āvaśyaka-karma-anuṣṭhāna-anantaram — after performing obligatory action; karma api bhakti-yoga-paryantam eva — action itself extends only up to devotional yoga; prayataḥ — disciplined; niyama-tat-paraḥ — intent upon regulation.
Transliteration
Bhāgavatam — Transliteration
yāvan na jāyeta parāvare ’smin
viśveśvare draṣṭari bhakti-yogaḥ
tāvat sthavīyaḥ puruṣasya rūpaṁ
kriyāvasāne prayataḥ smareta
Śrīdhara — Transliteration
pūrvokta-vairāja-dhāraṇāyā etad dhāraṇāṅgatvam āha—yāvad iti | pare brahmādayo ’vare yasmāt | kutaḥ? viśveśvare draṣṭari na tu dṛśye, caitanya-ghanatvāt | bhakti-yogaḥ prema-lakṣaṇaḥ | kriyāvasāne āvaśyaka-karmānuṣṭhānānantaram | anena karmāpi bhakti-yoga-paryantam evety uktam | prayato niyama-tat-paraḥ ||14||
Śrīdhara Sanskrit
पूर्वोक्त-वैराज-धारणाया एतद् धारणाङ्गत्वम् आह—यावद् इति । परे ब्रह्मादयोऽवरे यस्मात् । कुतः ? विश्वेश्वरे द्रष्टरि न तु दृश्ये, चैतन्य-घनत्वात् । भक्ति-योगः प्रेम-लक्षणः । क्रियावसाने आवश्यक-कर्मानुष्ठानानन्तरम् । अनेन कर्मापि भक्ति-योग-पर्यन्तम् एवेत्य् उक्तम् । प्रयतो नियम-तत्-परः ॥१४॥

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. He explains that the previously described meditation on the Virāṭ serves as a preparatory limb of this meditation. Brahmā and the other exalted beings are still lower than the Lord, who is the Lord of the universe and the seer rather than an object seen, because He is pure, concentrated consciousness. Here bhakti-yoga is characterized by love. One should remember the universal form after completing necessary duties, showing that prescribed action itself has devotional yoga as its proper culmination. Prayataḥ means disciplined and intent upon regulation.

ŚB 2.2.15

स्थिरं सुखं चासनमास्थितो यति-
र्यदा जिहासुरिममङ्ग लोकम् ।
काले च देशे च मनो न सज्जयेत्
प्राणान् नियच्छेन्मनसा जितासु: ॥ १५ ॥

O King, whenever the yogī desires to leave this planet of human beings, he should not be perplexed about the proper time or place, but should comfortably sit without being disturbed and, regulating the life air, should control the senses by the mind.

Word-for-word
Bhāgavatam — Word-for-word
sthiram — without being disturbed; sukham — comfortable; ca — also; āsanam — sitting accommodation; āsthitaḥ — being situated; yatiḥ — the sage; yadā — whenever; jihāsuḥ — desires to give up; imam — this; aṅga — O King; lokam — this body; kāle — in time; ca — and; deśe — in a proper place; ca — also; manaḥ — the mind; na — not; sajjayet — may not be perplexed; prāṇān — the senses; niyacchet — must control; manasā — by the mind; jita-asuḥ — conquering the life air.
Śrīdhara — Word-for-word
āsanna-mṛtyoḥ puṁsaḥ — for a person whose death is near; kṛtyam uktam — what is to be done has been stated; svayam deham tyaktum icchataḥ — for one wishing voluntarily to leave the body; kṛtyam āha — he states what is to be done; aṅga he rājan — O King; imam lokam deham — this world, namely the body; jihāsuḥ hātum icchati — wishing to abandon; deśe puṇya-kṣetre — to a holy place; kāle uttara-ayana-ādau — to a time such as the northern course; mano na sajjayet — should not attach the mind; na saṅgam prāpayet — should not become attached; na hi deśa-kālau yoginaḥ siddhi-hetū — place and time are not the causes of a yogī’s perfection; yogaḥ eva — yoga alone is; dṛḍha-niścayaḥ — firm conviction; sthiram sukha-karam āsanam — a steady, comfortable seat; prāṇān niyacchet — should restrain the life airs.
Transliteration
Bhāgavatam — Transliteration
sthiraṁ sukhaṁ cāsanam āsthito yatir
yadā jihāsur imam aṅga lokam
kāle ca deśe ca mano na sajjayet
prāṇān niyacchen manasā jitāsuḥ
Śrīdhara — Transliteration
evaṁ tāvad āsanna-mṛtyoḥ puṁsaḥ kṛtyam uktam, idānīṁ tasyaiva svayaṁ dehaṁ tyaktum icchataḥ kṛtyam āha—sthiram iti sārdhaiś caturbhiḥ | aṅga he rājan, evaṁbhūto yatir yadā imaṁ lokaṁ dehaṁ jihāsur hātum icchati, tadā deśe puṇya-kṣetre kāle cottarāyaṇādau mano na sajjayet saṅgaṁ na prāpayet | na hi deśa-kālau yoginaḥ siddhi-hetū, kiṁtu yoga eveti dṛḍha-niścayo bhūtvā sthiraṁ sukha-karaṁ cāsanam āsthitaḥ prāṇān niyacched ity arthaḥ ||15||
Śrīdhara Sanskrit
एवं तावद् आसन्न-मृत्योः पुंसः कृत्यम् उक्तम्, इदानीं तस्यैव स्वयं देहं त्यक्तुम् इच्छतः कृत्यम् आह—स्थिरम् इति सार्धैश् चतुर्भिः । अङ्ग हे राजन्, एवंभूतो यतिर् यदा इमं लोकं देहं जिहासुर् हातुम् इच्छति, तदा देशे पुण्य-क्षेत्रे काले चोत्तरायणादौ मनो न सज्जयेत् सङ्गं न प्रापयेत् । न हि देश-कालौ योगिनः सिद्धि-हेतू, किंतु योग एवेति दृढ-निश्चयो भूत्वा स्थिरं सुख-करं चासनम् आस्थितः प्राणान् नियच्छेद् इत्य् अर्थः ॥१५॥

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. The duties of a person whose death is near have now been explained. Śrīdhara next describes the procedure for one who wishes voluntarily to leave the body. Such a yogī should not attach the mind to a supposedly auspicious place or time, such as a holy region or the northern course of the sun, because place and time are not causes of yogic perfection; yoga itself is. Firm in this conviction, he should take a steady, comfortable seat and restrain the life airs.

ŚB 2.2.16

मन: स्वबुद्ध्यामलया नियम्य
क्षेत्रज्ञ एतां निनयेत् तमात्मनि ।
आत्मानमात्मन्यवरुध्य धीरो
लब्धोपशान्तिर्विरमेत कृत्यात् ॥ १६ ॥

Thereafter, the yogī should merge his mind, by his unalloyed intelligence, into the living entity, and then merge the living entity into the Superself. And by doing this, the fully satisfied living entity becomes situated in the supreme stage of satisfaction, so that he ceases from all other activities.

Word-for-word
Bhāgavatam — Word-for-word
manaḥ — the mind; sva-buddhyā — by his own intelligence; amalayā — unalloyed; niyamya — by regulating; kṣetra-jñe — unto the living entity; etām — all of them; ninayet — merge; tam — that; ātmani — the self; ātmānam — the self; ātmani — in the Superself; avarudhya — being locked up; dhīraḥ — the fully satisfied; labdha-upaśāntiḥ — one who has attained full bliss; virameta — ceases from; kṛtyāt — all other activities.
Śrīdhara — Word-for-word
tat gṛhīta-viṣayam manaḥ — the mind whose object has been grasped; buddhyā niścaya-rūpayā niyamya — regulating it by decisive intelligence; tan-mātram kṛtvā — reducing it to that alone; etām buddhim — this intelligence; kṣetra-jñe — in the knower of the field; buddhy-ādi-draṣṭari — the witness of intelligence and the other faculties; nilayet pravilāpayet — should dissolve, merge; tam ca kṣetra-jñam — and that knower of the field; ātmani śuddhe — in the pure Self; śuddham ātmānam — that pure self; ātmā eva aham iti — with the realization “I am that Self”; brahmaṇi avarudhya ekīkṛtya — confining and unifying it in Brahman; labdha-upaśāntiḥ — having obtained peace; prāpta-nirvṛtiḥ — having attained satisfaction; kṛtyāt virameta — should cease from what remains to be done; tataḥ param prāpya-abhāvāt — because nothing further remains to be attained.
Transliteration
Bhāgavatam — Transliteration
manaḥ sva-buddhyāmalayā niyamya
kṣetra-jña etāṁ ninayet tam ātmani
ātmānam ātmany avarudhya dhīro
labdhopaśāntir virameta kṛtyāt
Śrīdhara — Transliteration
tad gṛhīta-viṣayaṁ mano buddhyā niścaya-rūpayā niyamya tan-mātraṁ kṛtvā, etāṁ buddhiṁ kṣetra-jñe buddhy-ādi-draṣṭari nilayet pravilāpayet | taṁ ca kṣetra-jñam ātmani śuddhe | taṁ ca śuddham ātmānam ātmaivāham ity ātmani śuddhe brahmaṇy avarudhya ekīkṛtya labdhopaśāntiḥ prāpta-nirvṛtiḥ san kṛtyād virameta, tataḥ paraṁ prāpyābhāvāt ||16||
Śrīdhara Sanskrit
तद् गृहीत-विषयं मनो बुद्ध्या निश्चय-रूपया नियम्य तन्मात्रं कृत्वा, एतां बुद्धिं क्षेत्र-ज्ञे बुद्ध्य्-आदि-द्रष्टरि निलयेत् प्रविलापयेत् । तं च क्षेत्रज्ञम् आत्मनि शुद्धे । तं च शुद्धम् आत्मानम् आत्मैवाहम् इत्य् आत्मनि शुद्धे ब्रह्मण्य् अवरुध्य एकीकृत्य लब्धोपशान्तिः प्राप्त-निर्वृतिः सन् कृत्याद् विरमेत, ततः परं प्राप्याभावात् ॥१६॥

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. Having restrained the mind by decisive, purified intelligence and reduced it to its object alone, the yogī should dissolve that intelligence into the knower of the field, the witness of intelligence and the other faculties. He then dissolves the knower of the field into the pure Self, and finally unifies that pure self in Brahman with the realization “I am the Self.” Having thereby attained peace and complete satisfaction, he ceases from further activity because nothing more remains to be attained.

ŚB 2.2.17

न यत्र कालोऽनिमिषां पर: प्रभु:
कुतो नु देवा जगतां य ईशिरे ।
न यत्र सत्त्वं न रजस्तमश्च
न वै विकारो न महान् प्रधानम् ॥ १७ ॥

In that transcendental state of labdhopaśānti, there is no supremacy of devastating time, which controls even the celestial demigods who are empowered to rule over mundane creatures. (And what to speak of the demigods themselves?) Nor is there the mode of material goodness, nor passion, nor ignorance, nor even the false ego, nor the material Causal Ocean, nor the material nature.

Word-for-word
Bhāgavatam — Word-for-word
na — not; yatra — wherein; kālaḥ — destructive time; animiṣām — of the heavenly demigods; paraḥ — superior; prabhuḥ — controller; kutaḥ — where is there; nu — certainly; devāḥ — the demigods; jagatām — the mundane creatures; ye — those; īśire — rules; na — not; yatra — therein; sattvam — mundane goodness; na — nor; rajaḥ — mundane passion; tamaḥ — mundane ignorance; ca — also; na — nor; vai — certainly; vikāraḥ — transformation; na — nor; mahān — the material Causal Ocean; pradhānam — material nature.
Śrīdhara — Word-for-word
sarva-upādhi-rahitatvam āha — he states the absence of all limiting adjuncts; yatra yasmin ātmani — in that Self; kṛtārthānām kṛta-kṛtyānām — of those whose purposes are fulfilled and duties accomplished; svarga-loka-ādiṣu adhikāriṇaḥ devāḥ api — even gods ruling the heavenly worlds and the like; na prabhavaḥ — have no sovereignty; kutaḥ tad-adhīnasya kālasya — how much less destructive time, which is under Him; guṇānām sattva-ādīnām kutaḥ adhikāraḥ — how could the modes beginning with goodness have authority?; tad-dharmiṇaḥ pradhānasya — of primordial matter possessing those modes; mahat vikāraḥ — the great transformation, mahat.
Transliteration
Bhāgavatam — Transliteration
na yatra kālo ’nimiṣāṁ paraḥ prabhuḥ
kuto nu devā jagatāṁ ya īśire
na yatra sattvaṁ na rajas tamaś ca
na vai vikāro na mahān pradhānam
Śrīdhara — Transliteration
tatra sarvopādhi-rahitatvam āha—na yatreti | yatra yasminn ātmani kṛtārthānāṁ kṛtakṛtyānāṁ svarga-lokādiṣv adhikāriṇo devā api na prabhavaḥ | kutas tad-adhīnasya kālasya? guṇānāṁ ca sattvādīnāṁ kuto’dhikāraḥ? kutaś ca tad-dharmiṇaḥ pradhānasya? kuta eva mahān vikāraḥ? ||17||
Śrīdhara Sanskrit
तत्र सर्वोपाधि-रहितत्वम् आह—न यत्रेति । यत्र यस्मिन्न् आत्मनि कृतार्थानां कृतकृत्यानां स्वर्ग-लोकादिष्व् अधिकारिणो देवा अपि न प्रभवः । कुतस् तद् अधीनस्य कालस्य ? गुणानां च सत्त्वादीनां कुतोऽधिकारः ? कुतश् च तद्-धर्मिणः प्रधानस्य ? कुत एव महान् विकारः ? ॥१७॥

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. He describes the Self as free from every limiting adjunct. Even the gods who exercise authority in heavenly realms have no sovereignty there. How then could destructive time, which itself is subordinate, have any power? Nor can the modes of goodness, passion and ignorance, primordial matter that possesses them, or its great transformation, the mahat principle, exercise authority there.

ŚB 2.2.18

परं पदं वैष्णवमामनन्ति तद्
यन्नेति नेतीत्यतदुत्सिसृक्षव: ।
विसृज्य दौरात्म्यमनन्यसौहृदा
हृदोपगुह्यार्हपदं पदे पदे ॥ १८ ॥

The transcendentalists desire to avoid everything godless, for they know that supreme situation in which everything is related with the Supreme Lord Viṣṇu. Therefore a pure devotee who is in absolute harmony with the Lord does not create perplexities, but worships the lotus feet of the Lord at every moment, taking them into his heart.

Word-for-word
Bhāgavatam — Word-for-word
param — the supreme; padam — situation; vaiṣṇavam — in relation with the Personality of Godhead; āmananti — do they know; tat — that; yat — which; na iti — not this; na iti — not this; iti — thus; atat — godless; utsisṛkṣavaḥ — those who desire to avoid; visṛjya — giving it up completely; daurātmyam — perplexities; ananya — absolutely; sauhṛdāḥ — in good will; hṛdā upaguhya — taking them into his heart; arha — that which is only worshipable; padam — lotus feet; pade pade — at every moment.
Śrīdhara — Word-for-word
nirupādhitvam kutaḥ — whence comes this freedom from limiting adjuncts?; param vaiṣṇavam padam — the supreme Vaiṣṇava state; yat yasmāt — because; atat ātma-vyatiriktam — what is other than the Self; na iti na iti — “not this, not this”; utsraṣṭum icchavaḥ — those desiring to cast it off; daurātmyam deha-ādi-ātmatvam — the false identification of the body and the like as self; visṛjya — giving it up; arhasya pūjyasya śrī-viṣṇoḥ padam — the worshipable feet of Śrī Viṣṇu; pade pade kṣaṇe kṣaṇe — at every step, every moment; hṛdā upaguhya āśliṣya — embracing them in the heart; na anyasmin sauhṛdam — having affection for nothing else; tad vaiṣṇavam padam — that Vaiṣṇava state; sarvataḥ śreṣṭham — supreme above all.
Transliteration
Bhāgavatam — Transliteration
paraṁ padaṁ vaiṣṇavam āmananti tad
yan neti netīty atad utsisṛkṣavaḥ
visṛjya daurātmyam ananya-sauhṛdā
hṛdopaguhyārha-padaṁ pade pade
Śrīdhara — Transliteration
nirupādhitvaṁ kutaḥ? ity ata āha—param iti | yad yasmād atad ātma-vyatiriktaṁ neti netīty evam utsraṣṭum icchavo daurātmyaṁ dehādy-ātmatvaṁ visṛjya, arhasya pūjyasya śrī-viṣṇoḥ padaṁ pade pade kṣaṇe kṣaṇe hṛdā upaguhya āśliṣya, nānyasmin sauhṛdaṁ yeṣāṁ tathā-bhūtāḥ santaḥ, tad vaiṣṇavaṁ padam ata eva paraṁ sarvataḥ śreṣṭham āmananti ity anvayaḥ ||18||
Śrīdhara Sanskrit
निरुपाधित्वं कुतः ? इत्य् अत आह—परम् इति । यद् यस्मात् अतद् आत्म-व्यतिरिक्तं नेति नेतीत्य् एवम् उत्स्रष्टुम् इच्छवो दौरात्म्यं देहाद्य्-आत्मत्वं विसृज्य, अर्हस्य पूज्यस्य श्री-विष्णोः पदं पदे पदे क्षणे क्षणे हृदा उपगुह्य आश्लिष्य, नान्यस्मिन् सौहृदं येषां तथा-भूताः सन्तः, तद् वैष्णवं पदम् अत एव परं सर्वतः श्रेष्ठम् आमनन्ति इत्य् अन्वयः ॥१८॥

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. Why is that state free from all limiting adjuncts? Those who desire to abandon everything other than the Self through the discrimination “not this, not this” give up the false identification of body and the like as self. With affection for nothing else, they embrace in their hearts, at every moment, the worshipable lotus feet of Śrī Viṣṇu. Therefore they proclaim that Vaiṣṇava state to be the supreme state, superior to all.

ŚB 2.2.19

इत्थं मुनिस्तूपरमेद् व्यवस्थितो
विज्ञानद‍ृग्वीर्यसुरन्धिताशय: ।
स्वपार्ष्णिनापीड्य गुदं ततोऽनिलं
स्थानेषु षट्सून्नमयेज्जितक्लम: ॥ १९ ॥

By the strength of scientific knowledge, one should be well situated in absolute realization and thus be able to extinguish all material desires. One should then give up the material body by blocking the air hole [through which stool is evacuated] with the heel of one’s foot and by lifting the life air from one place to another in the six primary places.

Word-for-word
Bhāgavatam — Word-for-word
ittham — in this way; muniḥ — the philosopher; tu — but; uparamet — should retire; vyavasthitaḥ — being well situated; vijñāna-dṛk — by scientific knowledge; vīrya — strength; su-randhita — eternal; āśayaḥ — aim of life; sva-pārṣṇinā — with the heel of one’s own; āpīḍya — by blocking; gudam — air hole; tataḥ — thereafter; anilam — life air; sthāneṣu — in the places; ṣaṭsu — six primary; unnamayet — must lift; jita-klamaḥ — by stopping the duration of material activities.
Śrīdhara — Word-for-word
evam — thus; para-brahma-rūpam — the form/nature of the Supreme Brahman; tat adhyātma-viṣayam — that as the object of inner spiritual realization; proktvā — having described; tat-dhyāna-ādinā — through meditation on that and related practices; deha-tyāga-prakāram āha — he describes the method of leaving the body; ittham vyavasthitaḥ — situated in this manner; vijñāna-dṛśaḥ — of the vision of realization; jñāna-dṛṣṭeḥ vīryeṇa — by the power of the vision of knowledge; suṣṭhu randhitaḥ niruddhaḥ āśayaḥ manaḥ — whose inner mind is thoroughly restrained; sa muniḥ uparamet — that sage should cease; dehāt viramet — should withdraw from the body; sva-pārṣṇinā gudam āpīḍya — pressing the anus with his own heel; mūlādhārāt — from the mūlādhāra; prāṇam ūrdhvam — the life air upward; ṣaṭsu sthāneṣu unnamayet — should raise it through six places; jitaḥ klamaḥ yena — one by whom fatigue has been conquered.
Transliteration
Bhāgavatam — Transliteration
itthaṁ munis tūparamed vyavasthito
vijñāna-dṛg-vīrya-surandhitāśayaḥ
sva-pārṣṇināpīḍya gudaṁ tato ’nilaṁ
sthāneṣu ṣaṭsūnnamayej jita-klamaḥ
Śrīdhara — Transliteration
evaṁ para-brahma-rūpaṁ tad adhyātma-viṣayaṁ proktvā, tad-dhyānādinā deha-tyāga-prakāram āha—ittham iti | itthaṁ vyavasthito vijñāna-dṛśo jñāna-dṛṣṭer vīryeṇa suṣṭhu randhito niruddha āśayo mano yena sa munir uparamed dehād viramet | katham? sva-pārṣṇinā gudam āpīḍya tato mūlādhārāt prāṇam ūrdhvaṁ ṣaṭsu sthāneṣu unnamayet | jitaḥ klamo yena ||19||
Śrīdhara Sanskrit
एवं पर-ब्रह्म-रूपं तद् अध्यात्म-विषयं प्रोक्त्वा, तद्-ध्यानादिना देह-त्याग-प्रकारम् आह—इत्थम् इति । इत्थं व्यवस्थितो विज्ञान-दृशो ज्ञान-दृष्टेर् वीर्येण सुष्ठु रन्धितो निरुद्धः आशयो मनो येन स मुनिर् उपरमेद् देहाद् विरमेत् । कथम् ? स्व-पार्ष्णिना गुदम् आपीड्य ततो मूलाधारात् प्राणम् ऊर्ध्वं षट्सु स्थानेषु उन्नमयेत् । जितः क्लमो येन ॥१९॥

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. Having described the Supreme Brahman as the object of inner spiritual realization, he now explains the method of leaving the body through meditation upon it. The sage, established in this way, has thoroughly restrained his mind by the power of realized knowledge and should withdraw from the body. Pressing the anus with his own heel, he should raise the life air upward from the mūlādhāra through the six stations, having conquered fatigue.

ŚB 2.2.20

नाभ्यां स्थितं हृद्यधिरोप्य तस्मा-
दुदानगत्योरसि तं नयेन्मुनि: ।
ततोऽनुसन्धाय धिया मनस्वी
स्वतालुमूलं शनकैर्नयेत ॥ २० ॥

The meditative devotee should slowly push up the life air from the navel to the heart, from there to the chest, and from there to the root of the palate. He should search out the proper places with intelligence.

Word-for-word
Bhāgavatam — Word-for-word
nābhyām — on the navel; sthitam — situated; hṛdi — in the heart; adhiropya — by placing; tasmāt — from there; udāna — soaring; gatyā — by force; urasi — in the chest; tam — thereafter; nayet — should draw; muniḥ — the meditating devotee; tataḥ — thereafter; anusandhāya — just to search out; dhiyā — by intelligence; manasvī — the meditative; sva-tālu-mūlam — at the root of the palate; śanakaiḥ — slowly; nayeta — may be brought in.
Śrīdhara — Word-for-word
nābhyām — at the navel; maṇipūrake — in the Maṇipūra center; sthitam — situated; hṛdi — into the heart; anāhata-cakre — into the Anāhata center; adhiropya — having raised; urasi — in the chest; kaṇṭhāt adho-deśe — in the region below the throat; viśuddha-cakre — in the Viśuddha center; manasvī — one who has mastered the mind; jita-cittaḥ — whose mind is conquered; sva-tālu-mūlam — the root of his own palate; tasya eva cakrasya agra-deśam — the upper/front region of that very center; bahudhā gamana-sambhavāt — because movement may branch in many directions; śanakaiḥ — slowly.
Transliteration
Bhāgavatam — Transliteration
nābhyāṁ sthitaṁ hṛdy adhiropya tasmād
udāna-gatyorasi taṁ nayen muniḥ
tato ’nusandhāya dhiyā manasvī
sva-tālu-mūlaṁ śanakair nayeta
Śrīdhara — Transliteration
nābhyāṁ maṇipūrake sthitaṁ hṛdi anāhata-cakre’dhiropya | urasi kaṇṭhād adho-deśe sthite viśuddha-cakre | manasvī jita-cittaḥ | sva-tālu-mūlaṁ tasyaiva cakrasyāgra-deśam | tato bahudhā gamana-sambhavāc chanakair ity uktam ||20||
Śrīdhara Sanskrit
नाभ्यां मणिपूरके स्थितं हृदि अनाहत-चक्रेऽधिरोप्य । उरसि कण्ठाद् अधो-देशे स्थिते विशुद्ध-चक्रे । मनस्वी जित-चित्तः । स्व-तालु-मूलं तस्यैव चक्रस्याग्र-देशम् । ततो बहुधा गमन-संभवाच् छनकैर् इत्य् उक्तम् ॥२०॥

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. The life air situated at the navel, in the Maṇipūra center, is raised into the heart, the Anāhata center. It is then brought to the chest-region below the throat, the Viśuddha center. Manasvī means one whose mind is conquered. He should then slowly bring the life air to the root of the palate, the upper region of that center; “slowly” is specified because from there the current could move in several directions.

ŚB 2.2.21

तस्माद् भ्रुवोरन्तरमुन्नयेत्ततो
निरुध्य सप्तायतनान्यनन्यधी: ।
स्थित्वा मुहूर्तार्धमकुण्ठद‍ृष्टि-
र्निर्भिद्य मूर्धन् विसृजेत् परं गत: ॥ २१ ॥

Thereafter the bhakti-yogī should push the life air up between the eyebrows, and then, blocking the seven outlets of the life air, he should maintain his aim for going back home, back to Godhead. If he is completely free from all desires for material enjoyment, he should then reach the cerebral hole and give up his material connections, having gone to the Supreme.

Word-for-word
Bhāgavatam — Word-for-word
tasmāt — from there; bhruvoḥ — of the eyebrows; antaram — in between; unnayet — bring in; tataḥ — thereafter; nirudhya — blocking; sapta — seven; āyatanāni — outlets of the life air; ananya — not for any other purpose; dhīḥ — intelligence; sthitvā — being situated; muhūrta — twenty-eight minutes; ardham — half of; akuṇṭha — back home, back to Godhead; dṛṣṭiḥ — one whose aim is targeted like that; nirbhidya — punching; mūrdhan — the cerebral hole; visṛjet — should give up his body; param — the Supreme; gataḥ — having gone to.
Śrīdhara — Word-for-word
tasmāt — from there; bhruvoḥ antaram — the space between the eyebrows; ājñā-cakram — the Ājñā center; niruddhāni — blocked; śrotre — the two ears; netre — the two eyes; nāsike — the two nostrils; mukham — the mouth; sapta ayanāni — seven passages; prāṇa-mārgāḥ — paths of the life air; anapekṣaḥ cet — if free of further expectation/desire; param brahma gataḥ san — having attained the Supreme Brahman; mūrdhani — at the crown of the head; brahma-randhre — at the Brahmarandhra; nirbhidya — breaking through; deham indriyāṇi ca visṛjet — should release the body and senses.
Transliteration
Bhāgavatam — Transliteration
tasmād bhruvor antaram unnayet tato
nirudhya saptāyatanāny ananya-dhīḥ
sthitvā muhūrtārdham akuṇṭha-dṛṣṭir
nirbhidya mūrdhan visṛjet paraṁ gataḥ
Śrīdhara — Transliteration
tasmād bhruvor antaram ājñā-cakram | niruddhāni śrotre netre nāsike mukhaṁ cety evaṁ saptāsv ayanāni prāṇa-mārgā yena saḥ | anapekṣaś cet paraṁ brahma gataḥ san mūrdhan mūrdhani brahma-randhre nirbhidya deham indriyāṇi ca visṛjet ||21||
Śrīdhara Sanskrit
तस्माद् भ्रुवोर् अन्तरम् आज्ञा-चक्रम् । निरुद्धानि श्रोत्रे नेत्रे नासिके मुखं चेत्य् एवं सप्तास्व् अयनानि प्राण-मार्गा येन सः । अनपेक्षश् चेत् परं ब्रह्म गतः सन् मूर्धन् मूर्धनि ब्रह्म-रन्ध्रे निर्भिद्य देहम् इन्द्रियाणि च विसृजेत् ॥२१॥

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. From the palate the life air is raised to the space between the eyebrows, the Ājñā center. The seven passages of the life air are the two ears, two eyes, two nostrils and the mouth, and these are blocked. If the yogī has no further expectation or desire, then, having attained the Supreme Brahman, he breaks through the Brahmarandhra at the crown of the head and releases the body and senses.

ŚB 2.2.22

यदि प्रयास्यन् नृप पारमेष्ठ्यं
वैहायसानामुत यद् विहारम् ।
अष्टाधिपत्यं गुणसन्निवाये
सहैव गच्छेन्मनसेन्द्रियैश्च ॥ २२ ॥

However, O King, if a yogī maintains a desire for improved material enjoyments, like transference to the topmost planet, Brahmaloka, or the achievement of the eightfold perfections, travel in outer space with the Vaihāyasas, or a situation in one of the millions of planets, then he has to take away with him the materially molded mind and senses.

Word-for-word
Bhāgavatam — Word-for-word
yadi — however; prayāsyan — maintaining a desire; nṛpa — O King; pārameṣṭhyam — the governing planet of the material world; vaihāyasānām — of the beings known as the Vaihāyasas; uta — it is said; yat — what is; vihāram — place of enjoyment; aṣṭa-ādhipatyam — lording it over with eightfold achievements; guṇa-sannivāye — in the world of three modes of nature; saha — along with; eva — certainly; gacchet — should go; manasā — accompanied by the mind; indriyaiḥ — and the senses; ca — also.
Śrīdhara — Word-for-word
sadyo-muktim uktvā — having described immediate liberation; krama-mukti-prakāram āha — he describes the process of gradual liberation; pārameṣṭhyam padam — the station of Parameṣṭhin, Brahmaloka; prayāsyan — intending to go; vaihāyasānām — of those who move through the sky; khecarāṇām siddhānām — of the aerial Siddhas; vihāram — their place of sport; aṣṭau ādhipatyāni — the eight forms of mastery; aṇimādi-aiśvaryāṇi — powers beginning with aṇimā; guṇa-sannivāye — in the aggregate of the guṇas; guṇa-samudāya-rūpe brahmāṇḍe — in the universe formed of the aggregate of the modes; sarvatra — throughout it; manas ca indriyāṇi ca na tyajet — he should not abandon the mind and senses; taiḥ saha eva gacchet — he should go together with them; tat-tal-loka-bhoga-artham — for enjoyment in those respective worlds.
Transliteration
Bhāgavatam — Transliteration
yadi prayāsyan nṛpa pārameṣṭhyaṁ
vaihāyasānām uta yad vihāram
aṣṭādhipatyaṁ guṇa-sannivāye
sahaiva gacchen manasendriyaiś ca
Śrīdhara — Transliteration
