sadyo-muktim uktvā krama-mukti-prakāram āha—yadīti daśabhiḥ | yadi tu pārameṣṭhyaṁ padaṁ prayāsyan bhavati | uta vaihāyasānāṁ khecarāṇāṁ siddhānāṁ yad vihāraṁ krīḍā-sthānam | kīdṛśam? aṣṭāv ādhipatyāny aṇimādy-aiśvaryāṇi yasmiṁs tad api prayāsyan | kva? guṇa-sannivāye guṇa-samudāya-rūpe brahmāṇḍe | sarvatrety arthaḥ | tarhi deha-tyāgāvasare manaś cendriyāṇi ca na tyajet, kintu taiḥ sahaiva tat-tal-loka-bhogārthaṁ gacchet ||22||
Śrīdhara Sanskrit
सद्यो-मुक्तिम् उक्त्वा क्रम-मुक्ति-प्रकारम् आह—यदीति दशभिः । यदि तु पारमेष्ठ्यं पदं प्रयास्यन् भवति । उत वैहायसानां खेचराणां सिद्धानां यद् विहारं क्रीडा-स्थानम् । कीदृशम् ? अष्टाव् आधिपत्यान्य् अणिमाद्य्-ऐश्वर्याणि यस्मिंस् तद् अपि प्रयास्यन् । क्व ? गुण-सन्निवाये गुण-समुदाय-रूपे ब्रह्माण्डे । सर्वत्रेत्य् अर्थः । तर्हि देह-त्यागावसरे मनश् चेन्द्रियाणि च न त्यजेत्, किन्तु तैः सहैव तत्-तल्-लोक-भोगार्थं गच्छेत् ॥२२॥

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. Having described immediate liberation, Śrīdhara now begins the process of gradual liberation in ten verses. If the yogī intends to reach Brahmaloka, the sportive realms of the sky-moving Siddhas, or the eight mystic forms of mastery such as aṇimā anywhere within the universe constituted of the guṇas, then at the time of leaving the body he should not abandon the materially conditioned mind and senses. He goes together with them in order to experience those respective worlds.

ŚB 2.2.23

योगेश्वराणां गतिमाहुरन्त-
र्बहिस्त्रिलोक्या: पवनान्तरात्मनाम् ।
न कर्मभिस्तां गतिमाप्नुवन्ति
विद्यातपोयोगसमाधिभाजाम् ॥ २३ ॥

The transcendentalists are concerned with the spiritual body. As such, by the strength of their devotional service, austerities, mystic power and transcendental knowledge, their movements are unrestricted, within and beyond the material worlds. The fruitive workers, or the gross materialists, can never move in such an unrestricted manner.

Word-for-word
Bhāgavatam — Word-for-word
yoga-īśvarāṇām — of the great saints and devotees; gatim — destination; āhuḥ — it is said; antaḥ — within; bahiḥ — without; tri-lokyāḥ — of the three planetary systems; pavana-antaḥ — within the air; ātmanām — of the subtle body; na — never; karmabhiḥ — by fruitive activities; tām — that; gatim — speed; āpnuvanti — achieve; vidyā — devotional service; tapaḥ — austerities; yoga — mystic power; samādhi — knowledge; bhājām — of those who entertain.
Śrīdhara — Word-for-word
karma-gati-vat na yoga-gatiḥ paricchinnā — yogic movement is not limited as karmic movement is; yogeśvarāṇām gatim āhuḥ — they describe the movement of the masters of yoga; trilokyāḥ antar-bahiḥ — within and beyond the three worlds; mahar-loka-ādiṣu — in Maharloka and the higher worlds; brahmāṇḍāt bahiḥ ca — and outside the cosmic egg; pavanasya antaḥ ātmā — whose inner body is within/like the wind; liṅga-śarīram yeṣām — whose subtle body is such; vidyā upāsanā — vidyā means worship; tapaḥ bhagavad-dharmaḥ — tapas means duties directed to Bhagavān; yogaḥ aṣṭāṅgaḥ — yoga means eightfold yoga; samādhiḥ jñānam — samādhi means knowledge; tān ye bhajanti — those who cultivate these; teṣām yā gatiḥ — their manner of movement.
Transliteration
Bhāgavatam — Transliteration
yogeśvarāṇāṁ gatim āhur antar-
bahis-tri-lokyāḥ pavanāntar-ātmanām
na karmabhis tāṁ gatim āpnuvanti
vidyā-tapo-yoga-samādhi-bhājām
Śrīdhara — Transliteration
yataḥ karma-gativan na yoga-gatiḥ paricchinnety āha | yogeśvarāṇāṁ trilokyā antar-bahiś ca mahar-lokādiṣu brahmāṇḍād bahiś ca gatim āhuḥ | atra hetuḥ—pavanasyāntaḥ ātmā liṅga-śarīraṁ yeṣām iti | vidyā upāsanā, tapo bhagavad-dharmaḥ, yogo’ṣṭāṅgaḥ, samādhir jñānaṁ, tān ye bhajanti, teṣāṁ yā gatis tām ||23||
Śrīdhara Sanskrit
यतः कर्म-गतिवन् न योग-गतिः परिच्छिन्नेत्य् आह । योगेश्वराणां त्रिलोक्या अन्तर्-बहिश् च महर्-लोकादिषु ब्रह्माण्डाद् बहिश् च गतिम् आहुः । अत्र हेतुः—पवनस्यान्तः आत्मा लिङ्ग-शरीरं येषाम् इति । विद्या उपासना, तपो भगवद्-धर्मः, योगोऽष्टाङ्गः, समाधिर् ज्ञानं, तान् ये भजन्ति, तेषां या गतिस् ताम् ॥२३॥

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. Unlike the course produced by karma, yogic movement is not narrowly limited. The masters of yoga can move within the three worlds, into Maharloka and the higher realms, and even beyond the cosmic egg. Śrīdhara explains the four means in the verse: vidyā is worship, tapas is the discipline of Bhagavān-centered dharma, yoga is the eight-limbed system, and samādhi here signifies knowledge. This extraordinary movement belongs to those who cultivate these.

ŚB 2.2.24

वैश्वानरं याति विहायसा गत:
सुषुम्णया ब्रह्मपथेन शोचिषा ।
विधूतकल्कोऽथ हरेरुदस्तात्
प्रयाति चक्रं नृप शैशुमारम् ॥ २४ ॥

O King, when such a mystic passes over the Milky Way by the illuminating Suṣumṇā to reach the highest planet, Brahmaloka, he goes first to Vaiśvānara, the planet of the deity of fire, wherein he becomes completely cleansed of all contaminations, and thereafter he still goes higher, to the circle of Śiśumāra, to relate with Lord Hari, the Personality of Godhead.

Word-for-word
Bhāgavatam — Word-for-word
vaiśvānaram — the controlling deity of fire; yāti — goes; vihāyasā — by the path in the sky (the Milky Way); gataḥ — by passing over; suṣumṇayā — by the Suṣumṇā; brahma — Brahmaloka; pathena — on the way to; śociṣā — illuminating; vidhūta — being washed off; kalkaḥ — dirt; atha — thereafter; hareḥ — of Lord Hari; udastāt — upwards; prayāti — does reach; cakram — circle; nṛpa — O King; śaiśumāram — named Śiśumāra.
Śrīdhara — Word-for-word
tām āha — he describes that path; vaiśvānaram iti aṣṭabhiḥ — in eight verses beginning with vaiśvānaram; vihāyasā ākāśena — through the sky; brahma-loka-pathena gataḥ — traveling on the path to Brahmaloka; prathamam vaiśvānaram — first Vaiśvānara; agni-abhimāninīm devatām yāti — he reaches the deity presiding over fire; suṣumṇayā nāḍyā — through the Suṣumṇā channel; sā dehāt bahiḥ api vitatā — it extends even beyond the body; śociṣā jyotir-mayyā — by the luminous one; vidhūtaḥ tyaktaḥ kalkaḥ malaḥ — impurity and defilement cast off; kvāpi asajjamānaḥ — attached nowhere; udastāt upariṣṭāt vartamānam — situated above; hareḥ sambandhi tārā-rūpam — the stellar form connected with Hari; nārāyaṇa-adhiṣṭhitam — presided over by Nārāyaṇa; śaiśumāram cakram — the Śiśumāra circle; śiśumāra-āśrayam jyotiḥ-cakram — the stellar wheel in the form of Śiśumāra; cakra-sthāni āditya-ādi-dhruva-antāni padāni — the stations in the wheel from the sun through Dhruva; prayāti — he reaches them.
Transliteration
Bhāgavatam — Transliteration
vaiśvānaraṁ yāti vihāyasā gataḥ
suṣumṇayā brahma-pathena śociṣā
vidhūta-kalko ’tha harer udastāt
prayāti cakraṁ nṛpa śaiśumāram
Śrīdhara — Transliteration
tām āha—vaiśvānaram ity aṣṭabhiḥ | vihāyasā ākāśena, tatra ca brahma-loka-pathena gataḥ san prathamaṁ vaiśvānaram agny-abhimāninīṁ devatāṁ yāti | kenopāyena? suṣumṇayā nāḍyā | sā ca dehād bahir api vitatāstīty āha | śociṣā jyotir-mayyā vidhūtas tyaktaḥ kalko malo yena saḥ, kvāpy asajjamāna ity arthaḥ | udastād upariṣṭād vartamānaṁ hareḥ sambandhi tārā-rūpam, nārāyaṇādhiṣṭhitam ity arthaḥ | śaiśumāraṁ cakraṁ pañcama-skandhe vakṣyamāṇaṁ śiśumārāśrayaṁ jyotiś-cakram | cakra-sthāny ādity-ādi-dhruvāntāni padāni prayāti ity arthaḥ ||24||
Śrīdhara Sanskrit
ताम् आह—वैश्वानरम् इत्य् अष्टभिः । विहायसा आकाशेन, तत्र च ब्रह्म-लोक-पथेन गतः सन् प्रथमं वैश्वानरम् अग्न्य्-अभिमानिनीं देवतां याति । केनोपायेन ? सुषुम्नया नाड्या । सा च देहाद् बहिर् अपि विततास्तीत्य् आह । शोचिषा ज्योतिर्-मय्या विधूतस् त्यक्तः कल्को मलो येन सः, क्वाप्य् असज्जमान इत्य् अर्थः । उदस्ताद् उपरिष्टाद् वर्तमानं हरेः संबन्धि तारा-रूपम्, नारायणाधिष्ठितम् इत्य् अर्थः । शैशुमारं चक्रं पञ्चम-स्कन्धे वक्ष्यमाणं शिशुमाराश्रयं ज्योतिश् चक्रम् । चक्र-स्थान्य् आदित्य्-आदि-ध्रुवान्तानि पदानि प्रयाति इत्य् अर्थः ॥२४॥

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. Śrīdhara begins an eight-verse description of this route. Traveling through the sky on the path to Brahmaloka, the yogī first reaches Vaiśvānara, the deity presiding over fire. He proceeds by the luminous Suṣumṇā channel, which extends even beyond the body, and through it his impurity is cast off so that he is attached nowhere. Above lies the stellar form related to Hari and presided over by Nārāyaṇa: the Śiśumāra wheel, whose stations extend from the sun up to Dhruva.

ŚB 2.2.25

तद् विश्वनाभिं त्वतिवर्त्य विष्णो-
रणीयसा विरजेनात्मनैक: ।
नमस्कृतं ब्रह्मविदामुपैति
कल्पायुषो यद् विबुधा रमन्ते ॥ २५ ॥

This Śiśumāra is the pivot for the turning of the complete universe, and it is called the navel of Viṣṇu [Garbhodakaśāyī Viṣṇu]. The yogī alone goes beyond this circle of Śiśumāra and attains the planet [Maharloka] where purified saints like Bhṛgu enjoy a duration of life of 4,300,000,000 solar years. This planet is worshipable even for saints who are transcendentally situated.

Word-for-word
Bhāgavatam — Word-for-word
tat — that; viśva-nābhim — navel of the universal Personality of Godhead; tu — but; ativartya — crossing over; viṣṇoḥ — of Lord Viṣṇu, the Personality of Godhead; aṇīyasā — due to mystic perfection; virajena — by the purified; ātmanā — by the living entity; ekaḥ — alone; namaskṛtam — worshipable; brahma-vidām — by those who are transcendentally situated; upaiti — reaches; kalpa-āyuṣaḥ — a period of 4,300,000,000 solar years; yat — the place; vibudhāḥ — self-realized souls; ramante — do enjoy.
Śrīdhara — Word-for-word
tat viṣṇoḥ cakram — that is Viṣṇu’s circle; viśvasya nābhim — the navel of the universe; sūrya-ādi-āśraya-bhūtam — serving as the support of the sun and the others; āviṣṭa-liṅgatvāt — because the gender is determined by the word qualified; nābhi-śabdasya na ṣaṇḍhatvam — the word nābhi is not neuter here; ativartya atikramya — passing beyond, crossing over; parataḥ svargiṇām gati-abhāvāt — because the inhabitants of heaven do not travel beyond it; ekaḥ eva — he alone; nirmalena liṅga-śarīreṇa — with a purified subtle body; brahma-vidām sthānam — the abode of knowers of Brahman; anyaiḥ namaskṛtam — worshiped by others; mahar-lokam upaiti — reaches Maharloka; yat yasmin — in which; vibudhāḥ — the enlightened beings; mahā-kalpa-āyuṣaḥ — whose life lasts a great kalpa; bhṛgu-ādayaḥ — Bhṛgu and the others.
Transliteration
Bhāgavatam — Transliteration
tad viśva-nābhiṁ tv ativartya viṣṇor
aṇīyasā virajenātmanaikaḥ
namaskṛtaṁ brahma-vidām upaiti
kalpāyuṣo yad vibudhā ramante
Śrīdhara — Transliteration
tad viṣṇoś cakram | viśvasya nābhiṁ sūryādy-āśraya-bhūtam | āviṣṭa-liṅgatvān nābhi-śabdasya na ṣaṇḍhatvam | ativartya atikramya | parataḥ svargiṇāṁ gaty-abhāvād eka eva nirmalena liṅga-śarīreṇa brahmavidāṁ sthānam anyair namaskṛtaṁ mahar-lokam upaiti | yat yasmin vibudhā mahā-kalpāyuṣo bhṛgv-ādayaḥ ||25||
Śrīdhara Sanskrit
तद् विष्णोश् चक्रम् । विश्वस्य नाभिं सूर्याद्य्-आश्रय-भूतम् । आविष्ट-लिङ्गत्वान् नाभि-शब्दस्य न षण्ढत्वम् । अतिवर्त्य अतिक्रम्य । परतः स्वर्गिणां गत्य्-अभावाद् एक एव निर्मलेन लिङ्ग-शरीरेण ब्रह्मविदां स्थानम् अन्यैर् नमस्कृतं महर्-लोकम् उपैति। यत् यस्मिन् विबुधा महा-कल्पायुषो भृग्व्-आदयः ॥२५॥

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. That is Viṣṇu’s circle, the navel of the universe and the support of the sun and the other luminaries. Passing beyond it, the yogī alone, with a purified subtle body, reaches Maharloka, the abode of knowers of Brahman and a realm worshiped by others. Beyond this point the ordinary inhabitants of heaven have no course. In Maharloka dwell enlightened beings such as Bhṛgu, whose lives endure for a great kalpa.

ŚB 2.2.26

अथो अनन्तस्य मुखानलेन
दन्दह्यमानं स निरीक्ष्य विश्वम् ।
निर्याति सिद्धेश्वरयुष्टधिष्ण्यं
यद् द्वैपरार्ध्यं तदु पारमेष्ठ्यम् ॥ २६ ॥

At the time of the final devastation of the complete universe [the end of the duration of Brahmā’s life], a flame of fire emanates from the mouth of Ananta [from the bottom of the universe]. The yogī sees all the planets of the universe burning to ashes, and thus he leaves for Satyaloka by airplanes used by the great purified souls. The duration of life in Satyaloka is calculated to be 15,480,000,000,000 years.

Word-for-word
Bhāgavatam — Word-for-word
atho — thereupon; anantasya — of Ananta, the resting incarnation of Godhead; mukha-analena — by the fire emanating from His mouth; dandahyamānam — burning to ashes; saḥ — he; nirīkṣya — by seeing this; viśvam — the universe; niryāti — goes out; siddheśvara-yuṣṭa-dhiṣṇyam — airplanes used by the great purified souls; yat — the place; dvai-parārdhyam — 15,480,000,000,000 solar years; tat — that; u — the exalted; pārameṣṭhyam — Satyaloka, where Brahmā resides.
Śrīdhara — Word-for-word
atho anantaram — thereafter; kalpa-ante — at the end of the kalpa; saḥ — he; viśvam trilokyam — the universe, the three worlds; atiśayena dahyamānam — burning intensely; nirīkṣya — seeing; tatra api ūṣma-prāpteḥ — because the heat reaches even there; yat dvi-parārdha-sthāyi — that which endures for two parārdhas; tat pārameṣṭhyam padam — that station of Parameṣṭhin; prati niryāti — he departs toward; siddheśvaraiḥ juṣṭāni — frequented by great Siddhas; dhiṣṇyāni vimānāni — stations/vehicles, airplanes; yasmin — in which; u iti — the particle u; śraiṣṭhyam sūcitam — indicates its excellence.
Transliteration
Bhāgavatam — Transliteration
atho anantasya mukhānalena
dandahyamānaṁ sa nirīkṣya viśvam
niryāti siddheśvara-yuṣṭa-dhiṣṇyaṁ
yad dvai-parārdhyaṁ tad u pārameṣṭhyam
Śrīdhara — Transliteration
atho anantaraṁ kalpānte sa viśvaṁ trilokyam atiśayena dahyamānaṁ nirīkṣya, tatrāpy ūṣma-prāpteḥ | yad dvi-parārdha-sthāyi tat pārameṣṭhyaṁ padaṁ prati niryāti | siddheśvarair juṣṭāni dhiṣṇyāni vimānāni yasmiṁs tat | u iti śraiṣṭhyaṁ sūcitam ||26||
Śrīdhara Sanskrit
अथो अनन्तरं कल्पान्ते स विश्वं त्रैलोक्यम् अतिशयेन दह्यमानं निरीक्ष्य, तत्राप्य् ऊष्म-प्राप्तेः । यद् द्विपरार्ध-स्थायि तत् पारमेष्ठ्यं पदं प्रति निर्याति । सिद्धेश्वरैर् जुष्टानि धिष्ण्यानि विमानानि यस्मिंस् तत् । उ इति श्रैष्ठ्यं सूचितम् ॥२६॥

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. Thereafter, at the end of the kalpa, he sees the three worlds burning intensely, because the heat reaches even Maharloka. He therefore departs toward the exalted station of Parameṣṭhin, Satyaloka, which endures for two parārdhas. That realm contains aerial vehicles frequented by the great Siddhas. Śrīdhara notes that the particle u indicates the excellence of this destination.

ŚB 2.2.27

न यत्र शोको न जरा न मृत्यु-
र्नार्तिर्न चोद्वेग ऋते कुतश्चित् ।
यच्चित्ततोऽद: कृपयानिदंविदां
दुरन्तदु:खप्रभवानुदर्शनात् ॥ २७ ॥

In that planet of Satyaloka, there is neither bereavement, nor old age nor death. There is no pain of any kind, and therefore there are no anxieties, save that sometimes, due to consciousness, there is a feeling of compassion for those unaware of the process of devotional service, who are subjected to unsurpassable miseries in the material world.

Word-for-word
Bhāgavatam — Word-for-word
na — never; yatra — there are; śokaḥ — bereavement; na — nor; jarā — old age; na — nor; mṛtyuḥ — death; na — nor; artiḥ — pains; na — nor; ca — also; udvegaḥ — anxieties; ṛte — save and except; kutaścit — sometimes; yat — because of; cit — consciousness; tataḥ — therefore; adaḥ — compassion; kṛpayā — out of heartfelt sympathy; an-idam-vidām — of those who are ignorant of the process of devotional service; duranta — unsurpassable; duḥkha — misery; prabhava — repeated birth and death; anudarśanāt — by successive experience.
Śrīdhara — Word-for-word
nanu tatra api kāla-ādi-bhayam cet — if one fears time and the rest even there; tatra api na sthātavyam — then one should not remain there either; iti āśaṅkya — anticipating this objection; yatra satya-loke — in that Satyaloka; śoka-ādayaḥ na santi — grief and the rest do not exist; brahma-lokam prāptasya — for one who has reached Brahmaloka; sattva-śuddhyā — through purity of sattva; nitya-vastu-vivekāt — through discernment of the eternal reality; kadācit — sometimes; duranta-duḥkhānām jīvānām — for living beings undergoing endless suffering; śocatām — who suffer/grieve; karuṇayā duḥkham asti — there is sorrow through compassion; na tu sva-kṛtam — but none arising from oneself.
Transliteration
Bhāgavatam — Transliteration
na yatra śoko na jarā na mṛtyur
nārtir na codvega ṛte kutaścit
yac cit tato ’daḥ kṛpayānidaṁ-vidāṁ
duranta-duḥkha-prabhavānudarśanāt
Śrīdhara — Transliteration
nanu tatrāpi kālādi-bhayaṁ cet tarhi tatrāpi na sthātavyam ity āśaṅkyāha—na yatreti | yatra satya-loke śokādayo na santi | kutaḥ? yad brahma-lokaṁ prāptasya sattva-śuddhyā nitya-vastu-vivekāt | tatra kadācid duranta-duḥkhānāṁ jīvānāṁ śocatāṁ karuṇayā duḥkham asti, na tu sva-kṛtam ||27||
Śrīdhara Sanskrit
ननु तत्रापि कालादि-भयं चेत् तर्हि तत्रापि न स्थातव्यम् इत्य् आशङ्क्याह—न यत्रेति । यत्र सत्य-लोके शोकादयो न सन्ति । कुतः ? यद् ब्रह्म-लोकं प्राप्तस्य सत्त्व-शुद्ध्या नित्य-वस्तु-विवेकात् । तत्र कदाचिद् दुरन्त-दुःखानां जीवानां शोचतां करुणया दुःखम् अस्ति, न तु स्व-कृतम् ॥२७॥

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. One might ask whether fear of time and other afflictions remains there; if so, even Satyaloka would not be worth remaining in. Śrīdhara answers that grief, old age and the other personal sufferings do not exist there, because one who reaches Brahmaloka has purified sattva and discernment of the eternal reality. The only sorrow that may arise is compassionate sorrow on seeing living beings subjected to endless misery; it is not suffering produced by one’s own condition.

ŚB 2.2.28

ततो विशेषं प्रतिपद्य निर्भय-
स्तेनात्मनापोऽनलमूर्तिरत्वरन् ।
ज्योतिर्मयो वायुमुपेत्य काले
वाय्वात्मना खं बृहदात्मलिङ्गम् ॥ २८ ॥

After reaching Satyaloka, the devotee is specifically able to be incorporated fearlessly by the subtle body in an identity similar to that of the gross body, and one after another he gradually attains stages of existence from earthly to watery, fiery, glowing and airy, until he reaches the ethereal stage.

Word-for-word
Bhāgavatam — Word-for-word
tataḥ — thereafter; viśeṣam — particularly; pratipadya — by obtaining; nirbhayaḥ — without any doubt; tena — by that; ātmanā — pure self; āpaḥ — water; anala — fire; mūrtiḥ — forms; atvaran — by surpassing; jyotiḥ-mayaḥ — effulgent; vāyum — atmosphere; upetya — having reached there; kāle — in due course of time; vāyu-ātmanā — by the self as air; kham — ethereal; bṛhat-ātma-liṅgam — the real form of the self.
Śrīdhara — Word-for-word
bhagavad-bhaktasya brahmāṇḍa-bhedana-prakāram — the method by which the Lord’s devotee pierces the cosmic coverings; īśvara-adhiṣṭhitāyāḥ prakṛteḥ — from prakṛti presided over by the Lord; mahat-tattvam — the mahat principle; ahaṅkāraḥ — ego; śabda-tanmātra-dvārā nabhaḥ — ether through the subtle element of sound; sparśa-tanmātra-dvārā vāyuḥ — air through touch; rūpa-tanmātra-dvārā tejaḥ — fire through form; rasa-tanmātra-dvārā āpaḥ — water through taste; gandha-tanmātra-dvārā pṛthvī — earth through smell.
Transliteration
Bhāgavatam — Transliteration
tato viśeṣaṁ pratipadya nirbhayas
tenātmanāpo ’nala-mūrtir atvaran
jyotirmayo vāyum upetya kāle
vāyv-ātmanā khaṁ bṛhad ātma-liṅgam
Śrīdhara — Transliteration
tatra prastutasya bhagavad-bhaktasya brahmāṇḍa-bhedana-prakāram āha—tata ity-ādinā | tato viśeṣaṁ pratipadya liṅga-dehena pṛthivy-ātmatāṁ prāpya | nirbhayaḥ | tena ātmanā pṛthivī-rūpeṇa tan-nirantarā apaḥ pratipadya | evaṁ jyotirmayaḥ san | kāle bhogāvasāne | bṛhad ātmane liṅgam ||28||
Śrīdhara Sanskrit
तत्र प्रस्तुतस्य भगवद्-भक्तस्य ब्रह्माण्ड-भेदन-प्रकारम् आह—तत इत्य्-आदिना । ततो विशेषं प्रतिपद्य लिङ्ग-देहेन पृथिव्य्-आत्मतां प्राप्येत्य् अर्थः । निर्भयः । तेन आत्मना पृथिवी-रूपेण तन्-निरन्तरा अपः प्रतिपद्य । एवं ज्योतिर्मयः सन् । काले भोगावसाने । बृहद् आत्मने लिङ्गम् ॥२८॥

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. Śrīdhara explains how the devotee penetrates the coverings of the universe, successively assuming the corresponding elemental identity without fear and proceeding through earth, water, fire, air and ether.

ŚB 2.2.29

घ्राणेन गन्धं रसनेन वै रसं
रूपं च दृष्ट्या श्वसनं त्वचैव ।
श्रोत्रेण चोपेत्य नभोगुणत्वं
प्राणेन चाकूतिमुपैति योगी ॥ २९ ॥

The devotee thus surpasses the subtle objects of different senses like aroma by smelling, the palate by tasting, vision by seeing forms, touch by contacting, the vibrations of the ear by ethereal identification, and the sense organs by material activities.

Word-for-word
Bhāgavatam — Word-for-word
ghrāṇena — by smelling; gandham — aroma; rasanena — by taste; vai — exactly; rasam — palate; rūpam — forms; ca — also; dṛṣṭyā — by vision; śvasanam — contact; tvacā — touch; eva — as it were; śrotreṇa — by vibration of the ear; ca — also; upetya — by achieving; nabhaḥ-guṇatvam — identification of ether; prāṇena — by sense organs; ca — also; ākūtim — material activities; upaiti — attains; yogī — the devotee.
Śrīdhara — Word-for-word
indriya-arthānām — of the objects of the senses; bhūta-sūkṣmāṇām — of the subtle elements; atikramam — the transcending; āha — he describes; ghrāṇena adhiṣṭhitena — by the presiding sense of smell; gandham upetya — having reached odor; śvasanam — “śvasanam”; sparśam — touch; nabho-guṇatvam — the quality of ether; śabda-ātmatām — being of the nature of sound; prāṇena — by the vital/sensory power; tat-tat-karma-indriyeṇa — by each respective organ of action; ākūtim — activity; tat-tat-kriyām — each respective action.
Transliteration
Bhāgavatam — Transliteration
ghrāṇena gandhaṁ rasanena vai rasaṁ
rūpaṁ ca dṛṣṭyā śvasanaṁ tvacaiva
śrotreṇa copetya nabho-guṇatvaṁ
prāṇena cākūtim upaiti yogī
Śrīdhara — Transliteration
indriyārthānāṁ bhūta-sūkṣmāṇām atikramam āha | ghrāṇenādhiṣṭhitena gandham upetya | śvasanaṁ sparśam | nabho-guṇatvaṁ śabdātmatām | prāṇena tat-tat-karmendriyeṇa | ākūtiṁ tat-tat-kriyām ||29||
Śrīdhara Sanskrit
इन्द्रियार्थानां भूत-सूक्ष्माणाम् अतिक्रमम् आह । घ्राणेनाधिष्ठितेन गन्धम् उपेत्य । श्वसनं स्पर्शम् । नभो-गुणत्वं शब्दात्मताम् । प्राणेन तत्-तत्-कर्मेन्द्रियेण । आकूतिं तत्-तत्-क्रियाम् ॥२९॥

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. Śrīdhara says that the verse describes the yogī’s transcendence of the subtle elements that are the objects of the senses. Odor is passed through smell, touch through the tactile function, sound as the quality of ether, and the respective activities through their organs of action.

ŚB 2.2.30

स भूतसूक्ष्मेन्द्रियसंनिकर्षं
मनोमयं देवमयं विकार्यम् ।
संसाद्य गत्या सह तेन याति
विज्ञानतत्त्वं गुणसंनिरोधम् ॥ ३० ॥

The devotee, thus surpassing the gross and the subtle forms of coverings, enters the plane of egoism. And in that status he merges the material modes of nature [ignorance and passion] in this point of neutralization and thus reaches egoism in goodness. After this, all egoism is merged in the mahat-tattva, and he comes to the point of pure self-realization.

Word-for-word
Bhāgavatam — Word-for-word
saḥ — he (the devotee); bhūta — the gross; sūkṣma — and the subtle; indriya — senses; sannikarṣam — the point of neutralization; manaḥ-mayam — the mental plane; deva-mayam — in the mode of goodness; vikāryam — egoism; saṁsādya — surpassing; gatyā — by the progress; saha — along with; tena — them; yāti — goes; vijñāna — perfect knowledge; tattvam — truth; guṇa — the material modes; sannirodham — completely suspended.
Śrīdhara — Word-for-word
sthūla-sūkṣma-bhūta-atikramam — the passing beyond the gross and subtle elements; ahaṅkāra-prāptyā — by reaching ego; mahad-ādi-prāptim — attainment of mahat and the subsequent principles; vikāryam — that which has manifold products; ahaṅkāraḥ — ego; trividhaḥ — threefold; tāmasaḥ — in ignorance; rājasaḥ — in passion; sāttvikaḥ — in goodness; laya-sthānam — the place of dissolution; vijñāna-tattvam — the principle of intelligence; mahat-tattvam — the mahat principle; guṇānāṁ sannirodhaḥ — the dissolution of the modes; pradhānam — primordial nature.
Transliteration
Bhāgavatam — Transliteration
sa bhūta-sūkṣmendriya-sannikarṣaṁ
manomayaṁ devamayaṁ vikāryam
saṁsādya gatyā saha tena yāti
vijñāna-tattvaṁ guṇa-sannirodham
Śrīdhara — Transliteration
tad evaṁ sthūla-sūkṣma-bhūtātikramam uktvā tad-āvaraṇa-bhūtāhaṅkāra-prāptyā mahad-ādi-prāptim āha | sa yogī vikāryaṁ saṁsādya vijñāna-tattvaṁ yāti | vividhaṁ kāryam asyeti vikāryo ’haṅkāraḥ | sa trividhaḥ, tāmaso rājasaḥ sāttvika iti | tatra tāmasāj jaḍāni bhūta-sūkṣmāṇi jāyante | rājasād bahir-mukhāni daśendriyāṇi | sāttvikān mana indriya-devatāś ca | teṣāṁ layaś ca tat-tad-ahaṅkāre | tatra bhūta-sūkṣmāṇām indriyāṇāṁ ca sannikarṣaṁ laya-sthānaṁ tāmasaṁ, rājasaṁ ca manomayaṁ devamayaṁ ca sāttvikaṁ prāpya | gatyā evaṁ gamanena | tenāhaṅkāreṇa saha vijñāna-tattvaṁ mahat-tattvaṁ yāti | tato guṇānāṁ sannirodho layo yasmiṁs tat pradhānaṁ yāti ||30||
Śrīdhara Sanskrit
तद् एवं स्थूल-सूक्ष्म-भूतातिक्रमम् उक्त्वा तद्-आवरण-भूताहंकार-प्राप्त्या महद्-आदि-प्राप्तिम् आह । स योगी विकार्यं संसाद्य विज्ञान-तत्त्वं याति । विविधं कार्यम् अस्येति विकार्योऽहंकारः । स त्रिविधः, तामसो राजसः सात्त्विक इति । तत्र तामसाज् जडानि भूत-सूक्ष्माणि जायन्ते । राजसाद् बहिर्-मुखानि दशेन्द्रियाणि । सात्त्विकान् मन इन्द्रिय-देवताश् च । तेषां लयश् च तत्-तद्-अहंकारे । तत्र भूत-सूक्ष्माणाम् इन्द्रियाणां च सन्निकर्षं लय-स्थानं तामसं, राजसं च मनोमयं देवमयं च सात्त्विकं प्राप्य । गत्या एवं गमनेन । तेनाहंकारेण सह विज्ञान-तत्त्वं महत्-तत्त्वं याति । ततो गुणानां सन्निरोधो लयो यस्मिंस् तत् प्रधानं याति ॥३०॥

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. Having described transcendence of the gross and subtle elements, Śrīdhara explains the further ascent through ahaṅkāra and mahat. The subtle elements, senses, mind and their presiding deities dissolve into the corresponding threefold ego; the yogī then proceeds with ahaṅkāra into the mahat-tattva and finally into pradhāna, where the guṇas are brought to dissolution.

ŚB 2.2.31

तेनात्मनात्मानमुपैति शान्त-
मानन्दमानन्दमयोऽवसाने ।
एतां गतिं भागवतीं गतो यः
स वै पुनर्नेह विषज्जतेऽङ्ग ॥ ३१ ॥

Only the purified soul can attain the perfection of associating with the Personality of Godhead in complete bliss and satisfaction in his constitutional state. Whoever is able to renovate such devotional perfection is never again attracted by this material world, and he never returns.

Word-for-word
Bhāgavatam — Word-for-word
tena — by that purified; ātmanā — by the self; ātmānam — the Supersoul; upaiti — attains; śāntam — rest; ānandam — satisfaction; ānanda-mayaḥ — naturally so being; avasāne — being freed from all material contamination; etām — such; gatim — destination; bhāgavatīm — devotional; gataḥ — attained by; yaḥ — the person; saḥ — he; vai — certainly; punaḥ — again; na — never; iha — in this material world; viṣajjate — becomes attracted; aṅga — O Mahārāja Parīkṣit.
Śrīdhara — Word-for-word
tena ātmanā — with that self; pradhāna-rūpeṇa — in the form of primordial nature; ānandamayaḥ san — being full of bliss; upādhīnām avasāne — at the end of the limiting adjuncts; śāntam — peaceful; avikṛtam — unmodified; ānandam — bliss; param ātmānam — the Supreme Self; upaiti — attains; na viṣajjate — is not entangled; na āvartate — does not return; iti arthaḥ — this is the meaning.
Transliteration
Bhāgavatam — Transliteration
tenātmanātmānam upaiti śāntam
ānandam ānandamayo ’vasāne
etāṁ gatiṁ bhāgavatīṁ gato yaḥ
sa vai punar neha viṣajjate ’ṅga
Śrīdhara — Transliteration
tenātmanā pradhāna-rūpeṇa ānandamayaḥ sann upādhīnām avasāne śāntam avikṛtam ānandaṁ param ātmānam upaiti | na viṣajjate nāvartate ity arthaḥ ||31||
Śrīdhara Sanskrit
तेनात्मना प्रधान-रूपेण आनन्दमयः सन्न् उपाधीनाम् अवसाने शान्तम् अविकृतम् आनन्दं परम् आत्मानम् उपैति । न विषज्जते नावर्तते इत्य् अर्थः ॥३१॥

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. With that self in the form of pradhāna, being full of bliss, at the end of all limiting adjuncts the yogī attains the Supreme Self, peaceful, unmodified and blissful. “He is not entangled” means that he does not return.

ŚB 2.2.32

एते सृती ते नृप वेदगीते
त्वयाभिपृष्टे च सनातने च ।
ये वै पुरा ब्रह्मण आह तुष्ट
आराधितो भगवान् वासुदेवः ॥ ३२ ॥

Your Majesty Mahārāja Parīkṣit, know that all that I have described in reply to your proper inquiry is just according to the version of the Vedas, and it is eternal truth. This was described personally by Lord Kṛṣṇa unto Brahmā, with whom the Lord was satisfied upon being properly worshiped.

Word-for-word
Bhāgavatam — Word-for-word
ete — all that is described; sṛtī — way; te — unto you; nṛpa — O Mahārāja Parīkṣit; veda-gīte — according to the version of the Vedas; tvayā — by Your Majesty; abhipṛṣṭe — being properly inquired; ca — also; sanātane — in the matter of eternal truth; ca — verily; ye — which; vai — certainly; purā — before; brahmaṇe — unto Lord Brahmā; āha — said; tuṣṭaḥ — being satisfied; ārādhitaḥ — being worshiped; bhagavān — the Personality of Godhead; vāsudevaḥ — Lord Kṛṣṇa.
Śrīdhara — Word-for-word
sṛtī — the two courses; mārgau — paths; prakārau — modes or methods; sadyo-muktiḥ — immediate liberation; krama-muktiḥ — gradual liberation; vede gīte — taught by the Veda; na tu svotpṛkṣite — not imagined independently; tvayā abhipṛṣṭe — inquired into by you; mukti-viṣaye — concerning liberation; dakṣiṇa-mārgaḥ — the southern path.
Transliteration
Bhāgavatam — Transliteration
ete sṛtī te nṛpa veda-gīte
tvayābhipṛṣṭe ca sanātane ca
ye vai purā brahmaṇa āha tuṣṭa
ārādhito bhagavān vāsudevaḥ
Śrīdhara — Transliteration
sṛtī mārgau, prakārāv ity arthaḥ | he nṛpa! anapekṣo nirbhidya mūrdhan visṛjet paraṁ gataḥ iti yā sadyo-muktiḥ, saikā sṛtiḥ | yadi prayāsyann ity-ādinā krama-muktiś ca dvitīyā sṛtiḥ | ete sṛtī vedena gīte ukte, na tu svotpṛkṣite | tatra yadā sarve pramucyante kāmā ye ’sya hṛdi śritāḥ, atha martyo ’mṛto bhavaty atra brahma samaśnute iti sadyo-muktiḥ | te ’rcir abhisaṁbhavanti ity-ādinā krama-muktiś ca vedenoktā | yad vā, na karmabhis tāṁ gatim āpnuvanti iti dakṣiṇa-mārgasyāpi sūcitatvād ete sṛtī iti dvi-vacanam | tvayābhipṛṣṭe yac chrotavyam ity-ādi-praśnena, arthān mukti-viṣaye dve api sṛtī pṛṣṭe | brūhi yad vā viparyayam iti dakṣiṇa-mārgo ’py arthāt pṛṣṭaḥ | evaṁ tvayā ca ye pṛṣṭe ete sṛtī ity anvayaḥ ||32||
Śrīdhara Sanskrit
सृती मार्गौ, प्रकाराव् इत्य् अर्थः । हे नृप! अनपेक्षो निर्भिद्य मूर्धन् विसृजेत् परं गतः इति या सद्यो-मुक्तिः, सैका सृतिः । यदि प्रयास्यन्न् इत्य्-आदिना क्रम-मुक्तिश् च द्वितीया सृतिः । एते सृती वेदेन गीते उक्ते, न तु स्वोत्प्रेक्षिते । तत्र, यदा सर्वे प्रमुच्यन्ते कामा येऽस्य हृदि श्रिताः । अथ मर्त्योऽमृतो भवत्य् अत्र ब्रह्म समश्नुते इति सद्यो-मुक्तिः । तेऽर्चिर् अभिसंभवन्ति इत्य्-आदिना क्रम-मुक्तिश् च वेदेनोक्ता । यद् वा, न कर्मभिस् तां गतिम् आप्नुवन्ति इति दक्षिण-मार्गस्यापि सूचितत्वाद् एते सृती इति द्वि-वचनम् । त्वयाऽभिपृष्टे यच् छ्रोतव्यम् इत्य्-आदि-प्रश्नेन, अर्थान् मुक्ति-विषये द्वे अपि सृती पृष्टे । ब्रूहि यद् वा विपर्ययम् इति दक्षिण-मार्गोऽप्य् अर्थात् पृष्टः । एवं त्वया च ये पृष्टे एते सृती इत्य् अन्वयः ॥३२॥

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. Śrīdhara explains sṛtī as the two paths or methods: immediate liberation and gradual liberation. Both are taught by the Veda rather than invented independently, and both are contained within Parīkṣit’s inquiry concerning the destination and duty of one approaching death.

ŚB 2.2.33

न ह्यतोऽन्यः शिवः पन्था
विशतः संसृताविह ।
वासुदेवे भगवति
भक्तियोगो यतो भवेत् ॥ ३३ ॥

For those who are wandering in the material universe, there is no more auspicious means of deliverance than what is aimed at in the direct devotional service of Lord Kṛṣṇa.

Word-for-word
Bhāgavatam — Word-for-word
na — never; hi — certainly; ataḥ — beyond this; anyaḥ — any other; śivaḥ — auspicious; panthāḥ — means; viśataḥ — wandering; saṁsṛtau — in the material world; iha — in this life; vāsudeve — unto Lord Vāsudeva, Kṛṣṇa; bhagavati — the Personality of Godhead; bhakti-yogaḥ — direct devotional service; yataḥ — wherein; bhavet — may result in.
Śrīdhara — Word-for-word
saṁsarataḥ puṁsaḥ — for a person wandering in saṁsāra; bahavaḥ mokṣa-mārgāḥ — many paths to liberation; tapo-yoga-ādayaḥ — austerity, yoga and others; samīcīnaḥ — most fitting; ayam eva — this alone; anuṣṭhitāt — when practiced; bhakti-yogaḥ bhavet — bhakti-yoga arises; śivaḥ — auspicious and blissful; nirvighnaḥ — free from obstruction; nāsti eva — there is certainly no other.
Transliteration
Bhāgavatam — Transliteration
na hy ato ’nyaḥ śivaḥ panthā
viśataḥ saṁsṛtāv iha
vāsudeve bhagavati
bhakti-yogo yato bhavet
Śrīdhara — Transliteration
santi saṁsarataḥ puṁso bahavo mokṣa-mārgās tapo-yogādayaḥ, samīcīnas tv ayam evety āha—na hīti | yato ’nuṣṭhitād bhakti-yogo bhaved ato ’nyaḥ śivaḥ sukha-rūpo nirvighnaś ca nāsty eva ||33||
Śrīdhara Sanskrit
सन्ति संसरतः पुंसो बहवो मोक्ष-मार्गास् तपो-योगादयः, समीचीनस् त्व् अयम् एवेत्य् आह—न हीति । यतोऽनुष्ठिताद् भक्ति-योगो भवेदतोऽन्यः शिवः सुख-रूपो निर्विघ्नश् च नास्त्य् एव ॥३३॥

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. There are many paths to liberation for one moving through saṁsāra, such as austerity and yoga, but this path alone is fully fitting: from its practice bhakti-yoga to Bhagavān Vāsudeva arises. No other path is so auspicious, blissful and free from obstruction.

ŚB 2.2.34

भगवान् ब्रह्म कार्त्स्न्येन
त्रिरन्वीक्ष्य मनीषया ।
तदध्यवस्यत् कूटस्थो
रतिरात्मन् यतो भवेत् ॥ ३४ ॥

The great personality Brahmā, with great attention and concentration of the mind, studied the Vedas three times, and after scrutinizingly examining them, he ascertained that attraction for the Supreme Personality of Godhead Śrī Kṛṣṇa is the highest perfection of religion.

Word-for-word
Bhāgavatam — Word-for-word
bhagavān — the great personality Brahmā; brahma — the Vedas; kārtsnyena — by summarization; triḥ — three times; anvīkṣya — scrutinizingly examined; manīṣayā — with scholarly attention; tat — that; adhyavasyat — ascertained it; kūṭa-sthaḥ — with concentration of the mind; ratiḥ — attraction; ātman (ātmani) — unto the Supreme Personality of Godhead Śrī Kṛṣṇa; yataḥ — by which; bhavet — it so happens.
Śrīdhara — Word-for-word
bhagavān brahmā — the great Brahmā; kūṭa-sthaḥ — unchanging; nirvikāraḥ — free from modification; ekāgra-cittaḥ — with one-pointed mind; triḥ — three times; kārtsnyena — completely; brahma vedam — the Veda; anvīkṣya — having examined; vicārya — having deliberated; ātmani harau — in Hari, the Supreme Self; ratiḥ — loving attraction; mānīṣayā — by discernment; adhyavasyat — firmly determined.
Transliteration
Bhāgavatam — Transliteration
bhagavān brahma kārtsnyena
trir anvīkṣya manīṣayā
tad adhyavasyat kūṭa-stho
ratir ātman yato bhavet
Śrīdhara — Transliteration
kuta etat? ata āha—bhagavān brahmā | kūṭa-stho nirvikāraḥ, ekāgra-cittaḥ sann ity arthaḥ | tris trīn vārān kārtsnyena sākalyena brahma vedam anvīkṣya vicārya | yata ātmani harau ratir bhavet, tad eva manīṣayādhyavasyat niścitavān ||34||
Śrīdhara Sanskrit
कुत एतत् ? अत आह—भगवान् ब्रह्मा । कूटस्थो निर्विकारः, एकाग्र-चित्तः सन्न् इत्य् अर्थः । त्रिस् त्रीन् वारान् कार्त्स्न्येन साकल्येन ब्रह्म वेदम् अन्वीक्ष्य विचार्य । यत आत्मनि हरौ रतिर् भवेत्, तद् एव मनीषयाध्यवस्यत् निश्चितवान् ॥३४॥

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. Why is this conclusion authoritative? Brahmā, remaining steady and one-pointed, examined the Veda completely three times and deliberated upon it. He firmly determined that the true conclusion is that by which loving attraction toward Hari, the Supreme Self, arises.

ŚB 2.2.35

भगवान् सर्वभूतेषु लक्षितः स्वात्मना हरिः ।
दृश्यैर्बुद्ध्यादिभिर्द्रष्टा लक्षणैरनुमापकैः ॥ ३५ ॥

The Personality of Godhead Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa is in every living being along with the individual soul. And this fact is perceived and hypothesized in our acts of seeing and taking help from the intelligence.

Word-for-word
Bhāgavatam — Word-for-word
bhagavān — the Personality of Godhead; sarva — all; bhūteṣu — in the living entities; lakṣitaḥ — is visible; sva-ātmanā — along with the self; hariḥ — the Lord; dṛśyaiḥ — by what is seen; buddhi-ādibhiḥ — by intelligence; draṣṭā — one who sees; lakṣaṇaiḥ — by different signs; anumāpakaiḥ — by hypothesis.
Śrīdhara — Word-for-word
nanv anubhūte ’rthe — an objection: toward an experienced object; ratiḥ bhavati — affection arises; ananubhūte tu bhagavati — but toward Bhagavān who is not directly experienced; kathaṁ ratiḥ syāt — how could affection arise?; bhagavān lakṣitaḥ dṛṣṭaḥ — Bhagavān is indicated and perceived; svātmanā — by His own self; kṣetra-jñāntaryāmitayā — as the indwelling ruler of the knower of the field; dṛśyair buddhy-ādibhiḥ — through visible signs such as intelligence; draṣṭāraṁ vinā na ghaṭate — perception cannot occur without a self-luminous seer; anumāpakaiḥ — by inferential signs; svatantraḥ kartā — the independent agent; īśvara-siddhiḥ — the establishment of the Lord’s existence.
Transliteration
Bhāgavatam — Transliteration
bhagavān sarva-bhūteṣu
lakṣitaḥ svātmanā hariḥ
dṛśyair buddhy-ādibhir draṣṭā
lakṣaṇair anumāpakaiḥ
Śrīdhara — Transliteration
nanv anubhūte ’rthe ratir bhavati, ananubhūte tu bhagavati kathaṁ ratiḥ syāt? tatrāha—bhagavān lakṣito dṛṣṭaḥ | katham? svātmanā kṣetra-jñāntaryāmitayā | kaiḥ? dṛśyair buddhy-ādibhiḥ | tad eva dvedhā darśayati—dṛśyānāṁ jaḍānāṁ darśanaṁ sva-prakāśaṁ draṣṭāraṁ vinā na ghaṭata ity anupapatti-mukhena lakṣaṇaiḥ sva-prakāśāntaryāmi-lakṣakaiḥ | tathā buddhy-ādīni kartṛ-prayojyāni, karaṇatvād vāsyādivad iti vyāpti-mukhena anumāpakaiḥ | svatantraś ca kartety evam īśvara-siddhiḥ ||35||
Śrīdhara Sanskrit
नन्व् अनुभूतेऽर्थे रतिर् भवति, अननुभूते तु भगवति कथं रतिः स्यात् ? तत्राह—भगवान् लक्षितो दृष्टः । कथम् ? स्वात्मना क्षेत्र-ज्ञान्तर्यामितया । कैः ? दृश्यैर् बुद्ध्य्-आदिभिः । तद् एव द्वेधा दर्शयति—दृश्यानां जडानां दर्शनं स्व-प्रकाशं द्रष्टारं विना न घटत इत्य् अनुपपत्ति-मुखेन लक्षणैः स्व-प्रकाशान्तर्यामि-लक्षकैः । तथा बुद्ध्य्-आदीनि कर्तृ-प्रयोज्यानि, करणत्वाद् वास्यादिवद् इति व्याप्ति-मुखेन अनुमापकैः । स्वतन्त्रश् च कर्तेत्य् एवम् ईश्वर-सिद्धिः ॥३५॥

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. An objection may be raised: affection arises for something experienced, so how can it arise for Bhagavān if He has not been experienced? Śrīdhara answers that Bhagavān is indeed indicated and perceived as the indwelling ruler. The existence of inert objects and instruments such as intelligence presupposes a conscious seer and an independent directing agent; through these marks the existence of Īśvara is established.

ŚB 2.2.36

तस्मात् सर्वात्मना राजन् हरिः सर्वत्र सर्वदा ।
श्रोतव्यः कीर्तितव्यश्च स्मर्तव्यो भगवान्नृणाम् ॥ ३६ ॥

O King, it is therefore essential that every human being hear about, glorify and remember the Supreme Lord, the Personality of Godhead, always and everywhere.

Word-for-word
Bhāgavatam — Word-for-word
tasmāt — therefore; sarva — all; ātmanā — soul; rājan — O King; hariḥ — the Lord; sarvatra — everywhere; sarvadā — always; śrotavyaḥ — must be heard; kīrtitavyaḥ — glorified; ca — also; smartavyaḥ — be remembered; bhagavān — the Personality of Godhead; nṛṇām — by the human being.
Śrīdhara — Word-for-word
yac chrotavyam ity-ādi — the question beginning “what should be heard?”; praśna-uttaram — the answer to the question; upasaṁharati — he concludes; tasmād iti — with the word “therefore.”
Transliteration
Bhāgavatam — Transliteration
tasmāt sarvātmanā rājan
hariḥ sarvatra sarvadā
śrotavyaḥ kīrtitavyaś ca
smartavyo bhagavān nṛṇām
Śrīdhara — Transliteration
yac chrotavyam ity-ādi-praśnottaram upasaṁharati—tasmād iti ||36||
Śrīdhara Sanskrit
यच् छ्रोतव्यम् इत्य् आदि-प्रश्नोत्तरम् उपसंहरति—तस्माद् इति ॥३६॥

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. Śrīdhara says that with “therefore” Śukadeva concludes his answer to the question beginning, “What should be heard?”

ŚB 2.2.37

पिबन्ति ये भगवत आत्मनः सतां
कथामृतं श्रवणपुटेषु सम्भृतम् ।
पुनन्ति ते विषयविदूषिताशयं
व्रजन्ति तच्चरणसरोरुहान्तिकम् ॥ ३७ ॥

Those who drink through aural reception, fully filled with the nectarean message of Lord Kṛṣṇa, the beloved of the devotees, purify the polluted aim of life known as material enjoyment and thus go back to Godhead, to the lotus feet of Him [the Personality of Godhead].

Word-for-word
Bhāgavatam — Word-for-word
pibanti — who drink; ye — those; bhagavataḥ — of the Personality of Godhead; ātmanaḥ — of the most dear; satām — of devotees; kathā-amṛtam — the nectar of the messages; śravaṇa-puṭeṣu — within the earholes; sambhṛtam — fully filled; punanti — purify; te — their; viṣaya — material enjoyment; vidūṣita-āśayam — polluted aim of life; vrajanti — do go back; tat — the Lord’s; caraṇa — feet; saroruha-antikam — near the lotus.
Śrīdhara — Word-for-word
śravaṇa-ādi-phalam — the fruit of hearing and the other practices; abhinayena āha — he states with a vivid expression; pibanti iti — “they drink”; satām ātmanaḥ — of Him who is the very self of the saints; kathā eva amṛtam — the narration itself is nectar; viṣayaiḥ vidūṣitam — polluted by sense objects; malinīkṛtam āśayam — the heart made impure; punanti śodhayanti — they purify and cleanse; tasya caraṇa-padmāntikam — to the vicinity of His lotus feet; śrī-viṣṇu-padam — the abode of Śrī Viṣṇu; vrajanti — they go.
Transliteration
Bhāgavatam — Transliteration
pibanti ye bhagavata ātmanaḥ satāṁ
kathāmṛtaṁ śravaṇa-puṭeṣu sambhṛtam
punanti te viṣaya-vidūṣitāśayaṁ
vrajanti tac-caraṇa-saroruhāntikam
Śrīdhara — Transliteration
śravaṇādi-phalam abhinayenāha—pibantīti | satām ātmana ātmatvena prakāśamānasya kathaivāmṛtam | viṣayair vidūṣitaṁ malinīkṛtam āśayaṁ punanti śodhayanti | tasya caraṇa-padmāntikaṁ śrī-viṣṇu-padaṁ vrajanti ||37||
Śrīdhara Sanskrit
श्रवणादि-फलम् अभिनयेनाह—पिबन्तीति । सताम् आत्मन आत्मत्वेन प्रकाशमानस्य कथैवामृतम् । विषयैर् विदूषितं मलिनीकृतम् आशयं पुनन्ति शोधयन्ति । तस्य चरण-पद्मान्तिकं श्री-विष्णु-पदं व्रजन्ति ॥३७॥

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. He states the fruit of hearing and the other devotional practices with the vivid expression “they drink.” The narration of Him who shines as the very self of the saints is itself nectar. Those who hear it cleanse the heart polluted by sense objects and go to the vicinity of His lotus feet, the abode of Śrī Viṣṇu.

Chapter Three

ŚB 2.3.1

श्री शुक उवाच
एवमेतन्निगदितं पृष्टवान् यद्भवान् मम ।
नृणां यन्म्रियमाणानां मनुष्येषु मनीषिणाम् ॥ १ ॥

Śrī Śukadeva Gosvāmī said: Mahārāja Parīkṣit, as you have inquired from me as to the duty of the intelligent man who is on the threshold of death, so I have answered you.

Word-for-word
Bhāgavatam — Word-for-word
śrī-śukaḥ uvāca — Śrī Śukadeva Gosvāmī said; evam — so; etat — all these; nigaditam — answered; pṛṣṭavān — as you inquired; yat — what; bhavān — your good self; mama — unto me; nṛṇām — of the human being; yat — one; mriyamāṇānām — on the threshold of death; manuṣyeṣu — amongst the human beings; manīṣiṇām — of the intelligent men.
Śrīdhara — Word-for-word
anya-devatā-bhajanasya — worship of other deities; tuccha-phalatvena — because its fruit is trivial; heyatvam — its being fit to be rejected; mameti mām — “mama” means “me”; manīṣiṇaḥ — thoughtful persons; mriyamāṇāḥ — those approaching death; hari-kathā-śravaṇādikam — hearing Hari-kathā and the other devotional acts; nigaditam vihitam — has been declared and enjoined.
Transliteration
Bhāgavatam — Transliteration
śrī-śuka uvāca
evam etan nigaditaṁ
pṛṣṭavān yad bhavān mama
nṛṇāṁ yan mriyamāṇānāṁ
manuṣyeṣu manīṣiṇām
Śrīdhara — Transliteration
tṛtīye viṣṇu-bhakteṣu vaiśiṣṭyaṁ śṛṇvatāṁ muneḥ | bhakty-udrekeṇa tat-karma-śravaṇādara īryate || idānīm anya-devatā-bhajanasyāpi putrādi-bhajanavad eva tuccha-phalatvena heyatvaṁ vaktuṁ pūrvoktam anuvadati—evam iti | mameti mām | kadācid daiva-yogena manuṣyatvaṁ prāpteṣu jīveṣu ye manīṣiṇas teṣāṁ tatrāpi ye mriyamāṇās teṣāṁ viśeṣata evam etad dhari-kathā-śravaṇādikaṁ nigaditaṁ vihitam ity arthaḥ ||1||
Śrīdhara Sanskrit
तृतीये विष्णु-भक्तेषु वैशिष्ट्यं शृण्वतां मुनेः । भक्त्य्-उद्रेकेण तत्-कर्म-श्रवणादर ईर्यते ॥ इदानीम् अन्य-देवता-भजनस्यापि पुत्रादि-भजनवद् एव तुच्छ-फलत्वेन हेयत्वं वक्तुं पूर्वोक्तम् अनुवदति—एवम् इति । ममेति माम् । कदाचिद् दैव-योगेन मनुष्यत्वं प्राप्तेषु जीवेषु ये मनीषिणस् तेषां तत्रापि ये म्रियमाणास् तेषां विशेषत एवम् एतद् हरि-कथा-श्रवणादिकं निगदितं विहितम् इत्य् अर्थः ॥१॥

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. Śrīdhara introduces the special excellence of devotion to Viṣṇu. To show that worship of other deities is ultimately to be set aside because its fruits are comparatively small, Śukadeva restates the previous conclusion: among those who obtain human birth, the thoughtful—especially those approaching death—are particularly enjoined to hear Hari-kathā and perform the related devotional practices.

ŚB 2.3.2

ब्रह्मवर्चसकामस्तु यजेत ब्रह्मणः पतिम् ।
इन्द्रमिन्द्रियकामस्तु प्रजाकामः प्रजापतीन् ॥ २ ॥

One who desires to be absorbed in the impersonal brahmajyoti effulgence should worship the master of the Vedas [Lord Brahmā or Bṛhaspati, the learned priest], one who desires powerful sex should worship the heavenly King, Indra, and one who desires good progeny should worship the great progenitors called the Prajāpatis.

Word-for-word
Bhāgavatam — Word-for-word
brahma — the absolute; varcasa — effulgence; kāmaḥ tu — but one who desires in that way; yajeta — do worship; brahmaṇaḥ — of the Vedas; patim — the master; indram — the King of heaven; indriya-kāmaḥ tu — but one who desires strong sense organs; prajā-kāmaḥ — one who desires many offspring; prajāpatīn — the Prajāpatis.
Śrīdhara — Word-for-word
brahmaṇaspatim — Brahmā, lord of the Veda; vedasya patim — master of the Veda; brahmāṇam — Brahmā; indriya-pāṭava-kāmaḥ — one desiring acuity and strength of the senses; indram — Indra; prajā-kāmaḥ — one desiring progeny; prajāpatīn dakṣādīn — the Prajāpatis beginning with Dakṣa.
Transliteration
Bhāgavatam — Transliteration
brahma-varcasa-kāmas tu
yajeta brahmaṇaḥ patim
indram indriya-kāmas tu
prajā-kāmaḥ prajāpatīn
Śrīdhara — Transliteration
brahmaṇaspatiṁ vedasya patiṁ brahmāṇam | indriya-pāṭava-kāmas tv indram | prajā-kāmaḥ prajāpatīn dakṣādīn ||2||
Śrīdhara Sanskrit
ब्रह्मणस्पतिं वेदस्य पतिं ब्रह्माणम् । इन्द्रिय-पाटव-कामस् त्व् इन्द्रम् । प्रजा-कामः प्रजापतीन् दक्षादीन् ॥२॥

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. “Brahmaṇaspati” here means Brahmā, the lord of the Veda. One who desires powerful and acute senses should worship Indra, while one who desires offspring should worship the Prajāpatis such as Dakṣa.

ŚB 2.3.3

देवीं मायां तु श्रीकामस्तेजस्कामो विभावसुम् ।
वसुकामो वसून् रुद्रान् वीर्यकामोऽथ वीर्यवान् ॥ ३ ॥

One who desires good fortune should worship Durgādevī, the superintendent of the material world. One desiring to be very powerful should worship fire, and one who aspires only after money should worship the Vasus. One should worship the Rudra incarnations of Lord Śiva if he wants to be a great hero.

Word-for-word
Bhāgavatam — Word-for-word
devīm — the goddess; māyām — unto the mistress of the material world; tu — but; śrī-kāmaḥ — one who desires beauty; tejaḥ — power; kāmaḥ — one who so desires; vibhāvasum — the fire-god; vasu-kāmaḥ — one who wants wealth; vasūn — the Vasu demigods; rudrān — the Rudra expansions of Lord Śiva; vīrya-kāmaḥ — one who wants to be very strongly built; atha — therefore; vīryavān — the most powerful.
Śrīdhara — Word-for-word
māyām durgām — Māyā, Durgā; vibhāva-sutam agnim — the fire-god Agni; vasu-kāmaḥ — one desiring wealth; dhanārthī — a seeker of riches; vīryam — prowess; prabhāvaḥ — might or power; tat-kāmaḥ — one desiring that; rudrān yajet — should worship the Rudras.
Transliteration
Bhāgavatam — Transliteration
devīṁ māyāṁ tu śrī-kāmas
tejas-kāmo vibhāvasum
vasu-kāmo vasūn rudrān
vīrya-kāmo ’tha vīryavān
Śrīdhara — Transliteration
māyāṁ durgām | vibhāva-sutam agnim | vasukāmo dhanārthī | vīryaṁ prabhāvas tat-kāmo vīryavān san rudrān yajet ||3||
Śrīdhara Sanskrit
मायां दुर्गाम् । विभाव-सुतम् अग्निम् । वसुकामो धनार्थी । वीर्यं प्रभावस् तत्-कामो वीर्यवान् सन् रुद्रान् यजेत् ॥३॥

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. Māyā here means Durgā, and the fire-deity is Agni. A seeker of wealth worships the Vasus; one who seeks prowess and power, being desirous of such strength, worships the Rudras.

ŚB 2.3.4

अन्नाद्यकामस्त्वदितिं स्वर्गकामोऽदितेः सुतान् ।
विश्वान्देवान् राज्यकामः साध्यान्संसाधको विशाम् ॥ ४ ॥

One who wants a large stock of grains should worship Aditi. One who desires to attain the heavenly planets should worship the sons of Aditi. One who desires a worldly kingdom should worship Viśvadeva, and one who wants to be popular with the general mass of population should worship the Sādhya demigod.

Word-for-word
Bhāgavatam — Word-for-word
anna-adya — grains; kāmaḥ — one who so desires; tu — but; aditim — Aditi, mother of the demigods; svarga — heaven; kāmaḥ — so desiring; aditeḥ sutān — the sons of Aditi; viśvān — Viśvadeva; devān — demigods; rājya-kāmaḥ — those who hanker for kingdoms; sādhyān — the Sādhya demigods; saṁsādhakaḥ — what fulfills the wishes; viśām — of the mercantile community.
Śrīdhara — Word-for-word
annādyam — food and grain; bhojyam — what is eaten as food; bhakṣyam — what is chewed; aditeḥ sutān — the sons of Aditi; dvādaśādityān — the twelve Ādityas; viśām — of the people of a country; deśa-stha-prajānām — the subjects residing in the land; svādhīnatām icchan — desiring them to be under one’s authority; sādhyān yajet — one should worship the Sādhyas.
Transliteration
Bhāgavatam — Transliteration
annādya-kāmas tv aditiṁ
svarga-kāmo ’diteḥ sutān
viśvān devān rājya-kāmaḥ
sādhyān saṁsādhako viśām
Śrīdhara — Transliteration
annādyaṁ bhojyaṁ bhakṣyaṁ ca | aditeḥ sutān dvādaśādityān | viśāṁ deśa-stha-prajānāṁ svādhīnatām icchan sādhyān yajet ||4||
Śrīdhara Sanskrit
अन्नाद्यं भोज्यं भक्ष्यं च । अदितेः सुतान् द्वादशादित्यान् । विशां देश-स्थ-प्रजानां स्वाधीनताम् इच्छन् साध्यान् यजेत् ॥४॥

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. Śrīdhara explains that annādya comprises food to be eaten and chewed. The sons of Aditi are the twelve Ādityas. One who wants the people residing in a country to come under his authority should worship the Sādhyas.

ŚB 2.3.5

आयुष्कामोऽश्विनौ देवौ पुष्टिकाम इलां यजेत् ।
प्रतिष्ठाकामः पुरुषो रोदसी लोकमातरौ ॥ ५ ॥

One who desires a long span of life should worship the demigods known as the Aśvinī-kumāras, and a person desiring a strongly built body should worship the earth. One who desires stability in his post should worship the horizon and the earth combined.

Word-for-word
Bhāgavatam — Word-for-word
āyuḥ-kāmaḥ — desirous of long life; aśvinau — the two demigods known as the Aśvinī brothers; devau — the two demigods; puṣṭi-kāmaḥ — one who desires a strongly built body; ilām — the earth; yajet — must worship; pratiṣṭhā-kāmaḥ — one who desires good fame, or stability in a post; puruṣaḥ — such men; rodasī — the horizon; loka-mātarau — and the earth.
Śrīdhara — Word-for-word
ilām — Ilā; pṛthvīm — the earth; pratiṣṭhā — stability; sthānāt apracyutiḥ — not falling from one’s position; rodasī — the two worlds; dyāv-ābhūmī — heaven and earth.
Transliteration
Bhāgavatam — Transliteration
āyuṣ-kāmo ’śvinau devau
puṣṭi-kāma ilāṁ yajet
pratiṣṭhā-kāmaḥ puruṣo
rodasī loka-mātarau
Śrīdhara — Transliteration
ilāṁ pṛthvīm | pratiṣṭhā sthānād apracyutiḥ | rodasī dyāv-ābhūmī ||5||
Śrīdhara Sanskrit
इलां पृथ्वीम् । प्रतिष्ठा स्थानाद् अप्रच्युतिः । रोदसी द्यावा-भूमी ॥५॥

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. Ilā means the earth. Pratiṣṭhā means not being displaced from one’s established position, and rodasī refers to heaven and earth.

ŚB 2.3.6

रूपाभिकामो गन्धर्वान् स्त्रीकामोऽप्सर उर्वशीम् ।
आधिपत्यकामः सर्वेषां यजेत परमेष्ठिनम् ॥ ६ ॥

One who desires to be beautiful should worship the beautiful residents of the Gandharva planet, and one who desires a good wife should worship the Apsarās and the Urvaśī society girls of the heavenly kingdom. One who desires domination over others should worship Lord Brahmā, the head of the universe.

Word-for-word
Bhāgavatam — Word-for-word
rūpa — beauty; abhikāmaḥ — positively aspiring for; gandharvān — the residents of the Gandharva planet, who are very beautiful and are expert in singing; strī-kāmaḥ — one who desires a good wife; apsaraḥ urvaśīm — the society girls of the heavenly kingdom; ādhipatya-kāmaḥ — one who desires to dominate others; sarveṣām — everyone; yajeta — must worship; parameṣṭhinam — Brahmā, the head of the universe.
Śrīdhara — Word-for-word
apsarāḥ — an Apsarā; ca asau — and she indeed; urvaśī ca — is Urvaśī; tām — her.
Transliteration
Bhāgavatam — Transliteration
rūpābhikāmo gandharvān
strī-kāmo ’psara urvaśīm
ādhipatya-kāmaḥ sarveṣāṁ
yajeta parameṣṭhinam
Śrīdhara — Transliteration
apsarāś cāsāv urvaśī ca tām ||6||
Śrīdhara Sanskrit
अप्सराश् चासाव् उर्वशी च ताम् ॥६॥

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. Śrīdhara clarifies that the feminine expression here refers specifically to Urvaśī, who is herself one of the Apsarās.

ŚB 2.3.7

यज्ञं यजेद् यशस्कामः कोशकामः प्रचेतसम् ।
विद्याकामस्तु गिरिशं दाम्पत्यार्थ उमां सतीम् ॥ ७ ॥

One who desires tangible fame should worship the Personality of Godhead, and one who desires a good bank balance should worship the demigod Varuṇa. If one desires to be a greatly learned man he should worship Lord Śiva, and if one desires a good marital relation he should worship the chaste goddess Umā, the wife of Lord Śiva.

Word-for-word
Bhāgavatam — Word-for-word
yajñam — the Personality of Godhead; yajet — must worship; yaśaḥ-kāmaḥ — one who desires to be famous; kośa-kāmaḥ — one who desires a good bank balance; pracetasam — the treasurer of heaven, known as Varuṇa; vidyā-kāmaḥ tu — but one who desires education; giriśam — the lord of the Himalayas, Lord Śiva; dāmpatya-arthaḥ — and for conjugal love; umām satīm — the chaste wife of Lord Śiva, known as Umā.
Śrīdhara — Word-for-word
yajñam — Yajña; yajña-upādhim viṣṇum — Viṣṇu designated by the name Yajña; kośaḥ — treasury; vasu-sañcayaḥ — accumulation of wealth; vasu-kāma — desire for wealth; dhana-mātram — wealth in general; dāmpatyam — married life; anyonya-prītiḥ — mutual affection; tad eva arthaḥ — that itself is the desired object.
Transliteration
Bhāgavatam — Transliteration
yajñaṁ yajed yaśas-kāmaḥ
kośa-kāmaḥ pracetasam
vidyā-kāmas tu giriśaṁ
dāmpatyārtha umāṁ satīm
Śrīdhara — Transliteration
yajñaṁ yajñopādhiṁ viṣṇum | kośo vasu-sañcayaḥ | vasu-kāma ity atra dhana-mātram iti bhedaḥ | dāmpatyam anyonya-prītis tad evārtho yasya saḥ ||7||
Śrīdhara Sanskrit
यज्ञं यज्ञोपाधिं विष्णुम् । कोशो वसु-सञ्चयः । वसु-काम इत्य् अत्र धन-मात्रम् इति भेदः । दाम्पत्यम् अन्योन्य-प्रीतिस् तद् एवार्थो यस्य सः ॥७॥

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. Śrīdhara identifies Yajña here with Viṣṇu. A treasury is an accumulation of wealth; this is distinguished from the earlier general desire for wealth. In the final phrase, married happiness means the mutual affection of husband and wife.

ŚB 2.3.8

धर्मार्थ उत्तमश्लोकं तन्तुः तन्वन् पितॄन् यजेत् ।
रक्षाकामः पुण्यजनानोजस्कामो मरुद्गणान् ॥ ८ ॥

One should worship Lord Viṣṇu or His devotee for spiritual advancement in knowledge, and for protection of heredity and advancement of a dynasty one should worship the various demigods.

Word-for-word
Bhāgavatam — Word-for-word
dharma-arthaḥ — for spiritual advancement; uttama-ślokam — the Supreme Lord or persons attached to the Supreme Lord; tantuḥ — for offspring; tanvan — and for their protection; pitṝn — the residents of Pitṛloka; yajet — must worship; rakṣā-kāmaḥ — one who desires protection; puṇya-janān — pious persons; ojaḥ-kāmaḥ — one who desires strength should worship; marut-gaṇān — the demigods.
Śrīdhara — Word-for-word
dharma-arthaḥ — one desiring dharma; uttamaḥ-śloka-upādhim viṣṇum — Viṣṇu designated as Uttamaśloka; tantuṁ tanvan — extending the family line; santāna-vṛddhim anvicchan — seeking increase of descendants; rakṣā — protection; bādhā-nivṛttiḥ — cessation of obstacles; puṇya-janān — the Yakṣas; ojaḥ — strength; marud-gaṇān devān — the Marut demigods.
Transliteration
Bhāgavatam — Transliteration
dharmārtha uttama-ślokaṁ
tantuḥ tanvan pitṝn yajet
rakṣā-kāmaḥ puṇya-janān
ojas-kāmo marud-gaṇān
Śrīdhara — Transliteration
dharmārtho dharma-kāmaḥ | uttamaḥ-ślokopādhiṁ viṣṇum | tantuṁ tanvan santāna-vṛddhim anvicchan | rakṣā bādhā-nivṛttis tat-kāmaḥ | puṇya-janān yakṣān | ojo balaṁ tat-kāmo marud-gaṇān devān ||8||
Śrīdhara Sanskrit
धर्मार्थो धर्म-कामः । उत्तमः-श्लोकोपाधिं विष्णुम् । तन्तुं तन्वन् सन्तान-वृद्धिम् अन्विच्छन् । रक्षा बाधा-निवृत्तिस् तत्-कामः । पुण्य-जनान् यक्षान् । ओजो बलं तत्-कामो मरुद्-गणान् देवान् ॥८॥

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. Śrīdhara explains that one seeking dharma worships Viṣṇu under the designation Uttamaśloka. One extending the family line seeks the increase of descendants. Protection means the removal of obstacles; the Puṇya-janas are the Yakṣas, and one seeking strength worships the Marut demigods.

ŚB 2.3.9

राज्यकामो मनून् देवान् निरृतिं त्वभिचरन् यजेत् ।
कामकामो यजेत् सोममकामः पुरुषं परम् ॥ ९ ॥

One who desires domination over a kingdom or an empire should worship the Manus. One who desires victory over an enemy should worship the demons, and one who desires sense gratification should worship the moon. But one who desires nothing of material enjoyment should worship the Supreme Personality of Godhead.

Word-for-word
Bhāgavatam — Word-for-word
rājya-kāmaḥ — anyone desiring an empire or kingdom; manūn — the Manus, semi-incarnations of God; devān — demigods; nirṛtim — demons; tu — but; abhicaran — desiring victory over the enemy; yajet — should worship; kāma-kāmaḥ — one who desires sense gratification; yajet — should worship; somam — the demigod named Candra; akāmaḥ — one who has no material desires to be fulfilled; puruṣam — the Supreme Personality of Godhead; param — the Supreme.
Śrīdhara — Word-for-word
rājyam — kingship; rājatvam — the state of being king; manūn devān — the Manus, the divine rulers of Manvantaras; manvantara-pālān — protectors of the Manvantaras; abhicaran — acting with hostile rites; śatru-maraṇam icchan — desiring the death of an enemy; nirṛtim rākṣasam — Nirṛti, the Rākṣasa; kāma-kāmaḥ — desirous of enjoyment; bhogecchuḥ — one seeking enjoyment; akāmaḥ — one desiring detachment; vairāgya-kāmaḥ — desirous of renunciation; puruṣaṁ param — the Supreme Person; īśvaram — the Lord.
Transliteration
Bhāgavatam — Transliteration
rājya-kāmo manūn devān
nirṛtiṁ tv abhicaran yajet
kāma-kāmo yajet somam
akāmaḥ puruṣaṁ param
Śrīdhara — Transliteration
rājyaṁ rājatvaṁ tat-kāmo manūn devān manvantara-pālān | rājñaḥ karma rājyaṁ tat-kāmo viśvān devān iti viśeṣaḥ | abhicaran śatru-maraṇam icchan nirṛtiṁ rākṣasam | kāma-kāmo bhogecchuḥ | akāmo vairāgya-kāmaḥ | puruṣaṁ paraṁ prakṛti-vyatirekopādhim īśvaram ||9||
Śrīdhara Sanskrit
राज्यं राजत्वं तत्-कामो मनून् देवान् मन्वन्तर-पालान् । राज्ञः कर्म राज्यं तत्-कामो विश्वान् देवान् इति विशेषः । अभिचरन् शत्रु-मरणम् इच्छन् निरृतिं राक्षसम् । काम-कामो भोगेच्छुः । अकामो वैराग्य-कामः । पुरुषं परं प्रकृति-व्यतिरेकोपाधिम् ईश्वरम् ॥९॥

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. Śrīdhara distinguishes the desire for kingship here from the earlier desire for a kingdom. One seeking the death of an enemy worships Nirṛti; one seeking enjoyment worships Soma. One who is without such material desire and seeks detachment worships the Supreme Person, the Lord beyond material nature.

Chapter Four

The Process of Creation

ŚB 2.4.1

सूत उवाच—
वैयासकेर् इति वचस् तत्त्व-निश्चयम् आत्मनः ।
उपधार्य मतिं कृष्णे औत्तरेयः सतीं व्यधात् ॥ १ ॥

Sūta Gosvāmī said: Mahārāja Parīkṣit, the son of Uttarā, after hearing the speeches of Śukadeva Gosvāmī, which were all about the truth of the self, applied his concentration faithfully upon Lord Kṛṣṇa.

Word-for-word
Bhāgavatam — Word-for-word
sūtaḥ uvāca — Sūta Gosvāmī said; vaiyāsakeḥ — of Śukadeva Gosvāmī; iti — thus; vacaḥ — speeches; tattva-niścayam — that which verifies the truth; ātmanaḥ — in the self; upadhārya — just having realized; matim — concentration of the mind; kṛṣṇe — unto Lord Kṛṣṇa; auttareyaḥ — the son of Uttarā; satīm — chaste; vyadhāt — applied.
Śrīdhara — Word-for-word
turye — in the fourth chapter; parīkṣitā pṛṣṭam — what was asked by Parīkṣit; sṛṣṭy-ādi hari-ceṣṭitam — Hari’s acts beginning with creation; vaiyāsakeḥ śukasya — of Śuka, son of Vyāsa; tattvasya niścayaḥ — determination of the truth; upadhārya — having understood; satīm — faithful, chaste; kṛṣṇa eva sevyaḥ — Kṛṣṇa alone is to be served.
Transliteration
Bhāgavatam — Transliteration
sūta uvāca
vaiyāsaker iti vacas
tattva-niścayam ātmanaḥ
upadhārya matiṁ kṛṣṇe
auttareyaḥ satīṁ vyadhāt
Śrīdhara — Transliteration
turye parīkṣitā pṛṣṭaṁ sṛṣṭy-ādi hari-ceṣṭitam | śukena brahma-tat-putra-saṁvādenopavarṇyate ||1|| rājñaḥ praśnaṁ kathayituṁ tasya prāktanīṁ sthitim āha caturbhiḥ | vaiyāsakeḥ śukasya iti evaṁbhūtam ātmanaḥ tattvasya niścayo yasmāt tad-vaca upadhārya ākalayya | satīṁ kṛṣṇa eva sevyo nānya ity evaṁbhūtām ||1||
Śrīdhara Sanskrit
तुर्ये परीक्षिता पृष्टं सृष्ट्य्-आदि हरि-चेष्टितम् । शुकेन ब्रह्म-तत्-पुत्र-संवादेनोपवर्ण्यते ॥१॥ राज्ञः प्रश्नं कथयितुं तस्य प्राक्तनीं स्थितिम् आह चतुर्भिः । वैयासकेः शुकस्य इति एवंभूतम् आत्मनस् तत्त्वस्य निश्चयो यस्मात् तद्-वच उपधार्य आकलय्य । सतीं कृष्ण एव सेव्यो नान्य इत्य् एवंभूताम् ॥१॥

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. Śrīdhara opens the fourth chapter by saying that Parīkṣit’s questions about Hari’s acts beginning with creation will be narrated through the dialogue of Brahmā and Nārada. Sūta first recalls the King’s prior state: having grasped Śukadeva’s words as establishing the truth of the self, Parīkṣit fixed a chaste conviction that Kṛṣṇa alone is to be served.

ŚB 2.4.2

आत्म-जाया-सुतागार-पशु-द्रविण-बन्धुषु ।
राज्ये चाविकले नित्यं विरूढां ममतां जहौ ॥ २ ॥

Mahārāja Parīkṣit, as a result of his wholehearted attraction for Lord Kṛṣṇa, was able to give up all deep-rooted affection for his personal body, his wife, his children, his palace, his animals like horses and elephants, his treasury house, his friends and relatives, and his undisputed kingdom.

Word-for-word
Bhāgavatam — Word-for-word
ātma — body; jāyā — wife; suta — son; āgāra — palace; paśu — horses and elephants; draviṇa — treasury house; bandhuṣu — unto friends and relatives; rājye — in the kingdom; ca — also; avikale — without being disturbed; nityam — constant; virūḍhām — deep-rooted; mamatām — affinity; jahau — gave up.
Śrīdhara — Word-for-word
ātmā — the body; paśavaḥ — animals; gaja-ādayaḥ — elephants and the like.
Transliteration
Bhāgavatam — Transliteration
ātma-jāyā-sutāgāra-
paśu-draviṇa-bandhuṣu
rājye cāvikale nityaṁ
virūḍhāṁ mamatāṁ jahau
Śrīdhara — Transliteration
ātmā dehaḥ | paśavo gajādayaḥ ||2||
Śrīdhara Sanskrit
आत्मा देहः । पशवो गजादयः ॥२॥

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. Here ātman means the body, and the animals include elephants and similar possessions. The verse describes the King’s relinquishment of deeply rooted possessiveness toward all such bodily and royal attachments.

ŚB 2.4.3–4

पप्रच्छ चेमम् एवार्थं यन् मां पृच्छथ सत्तमाः ।
कृष्णानुभाव-श्रवणे श्रद्दधानो महा-मनाः ॥ ३ ॥

संस्थां विज्ञाय सन्न्यस्य कर्म त्रै-वर्गिकं च यत् ।
वासुदेवे भगवति आत्म-भावं दृढं गतः ॥ ४ ॥

O great sages, the great soul Mahārāja Parīkṣit, constantly rapt in thought of Lord Kṛṣṇa, knowing well of his imminent death, renounced all sorts of fruitive activities, namely acts of religion, economic development and sense gratification, and thus fixed himself firmly in his natural love for Kṛṣṇa and asked all these questions, exactly as you are asking me.

Word-for-word
Bhāgavatam — Word-for-word
papraccha — asked; ca — also; imam — this; eva — exactly like; artham — purpose; yat — that; mām — unto me; pṛcchatha — you are asking; sattamāḥ — O great sages; kṛṣṇa-anubhāva — rapt in thought of Kṛṣṇa; śravaṇe — in hearing; śraddadhānaḥ — full of faith; mahā-manāḥ — the great soul; saṁsthām — death; vijñāya — being informed; sannyasya — renouncing; karma — fruitive activities; trai-vargikam — the three principles religion, economic development and sense gratification; ca — also; yat — what it may be; vāsudeve — unto Lord Kṛṣṇa; bhagavati — the Personality of Godhead; ātma-bhāvam — attraction of love; dṛḍham — firmly fixed; gataḥ — achieved.
Śrīdhara — Word-for-word
imam evārtham — this very subject; hari-līlā-lakṣaṇam — characterized by the pastimes of Hari; saṁsthām — death; trai-vargikam — centered on dharma, artha and kāma; sannyasya — having abandoned; ātma-bhāvam — supreme love and identification with Bhagavān as one’s very self.
Transliteration
Bhāgavatam — Transliteration
papraccha cemam evārthaṁ
yan māṁ pṛcchatha sattamāḥ
kṛṣṇānubhāva-śravaṇe
śraddadhāno mahā-manāḥ

saṁsthāṁ vijñāya sannyasya
karma trai-vargikaṁ ca yat
vāsudeve bhagavati
ātma-bhāvaṁ dṛḍhaṁ gataḥ
Śrīdhara — Transliteration
imam evārthaṁ hari-līlā-lakṣaṇam ||3|| saṁsthāṁ mṛtyum | traivargikaṁ dharmārtha-kāma-pradhānam | sannyasya tyaktvā ātma-bhāvaṁ parama-premṇā bhagavad-ātmatvaṁ gataḥ prāptaḥ san papraccha ||4||
Śrīdhara Sanskrit
इमम् एवार्थं हरि-लीला-लक्षणम् ॥३॥ संस्थां मृत्युम् । त्रैवर्गिकं धर्मार्थ-काम-प्रधानम् । सन्न्यस्य त्यक्त्वा आत्म-भावं परम-प्रेम्णा भगवद्-आत्मत्वं गतः प्राप्तः सन् पप्रच्छ ॥४॥

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. The subject Parīkṣit asked about is precisely Hari’s līlā. Knowing death was near, he abandoned works aimed at the three worldly goals of dharma, artha and kāma and, established in supreme love for Vāsudeva, asked these questions.

ŚB 2.4.5

राजोवाच
समीचीनं वचो ब्रह्मन् सर्वज्ञस्य तवानघ ।
तमो विशीर्यते मह्यं हरेः कथयतः कथाम् ॥ ५ ॥

Mahārāja Parīkṣit said: O learned brāhmaṇa, you know everything because you are without material contamination. Therefore whatever you have spoken to me appears perfectly right. Your speeches are gradually destroying the darkness of my ignorance, for you are narrating the topics of the Lord.

Word-for-word
Bhāgavatam — Word-for-word
rājā uvāca — the King said; samīcīnam — perfectly right; vacaḥ — speeches; brahman — O learned brāhmaṇa; sarva-jñasya — one who knows all; tava — your; anagha — without any contamination; tamaḥ — the darkness of ignorance; viśīryate — gradually disappearing; mahyam — unto me; hareḥ — of the Lord; kathayataḥ — as you are speaking; kathām — topics.
Śrīdhara — Word-for-word
mahyam mama — for me, mine; tamaḥ ajñānam — the darkness, ignorance; viśīryate naśyati — is shattered, destroyed; tava kathayataḥ sataḥ — as you continue speaking; ataḥ samīcīnam — therefore it is perfectly right.
Transliteration
Bhāgavatam — Transliteration
rājovāca
samīcīnaṁ vaco brahman
sarva-jñasya tavānagha
tamo viśīryate mahyaṁ
hareḥ kathayataḥ kathām
Śrīdhara — Transliteration
mahyaṁ mama tamo ’jñānaṁ viśīryate naśyati tava kathayataḥ sataḥ | ataḥ samīcīnam ||5||
Śrīdhara Sanskrit
मह्यं मम तमोऽज्ञानं विशीर्यते नश्यति तव कथयतः सतः । अतः समीचीनम् ॥५॥

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. Śrīdhara explains that the darkness referred to is Parīkṣit’s ignorance, which is being shattered and destroyed as Śukadeva speaks. Therefore the King judges Śukadeva’s words to be perfectly appropriate.

ŚB 2.4.6

भूय एव विवित्सामि भगवानात्ममायया ।
यथेदं सृजते विश्वं दुर्विभाव्यमधीश्वरैः ॥ ६ ॥

I beg to know from you how the Personality of Godhead, by His personal energies, creates these phenomenal universes as they are, which are inconceivable even to the great demigods.

Word-for-word
Bhāgavatam — Word-for-word
bhūyaḥ — again; eva — also; vivitsāmi — I wish to learn; bhagavān — the Personality of Godhead; ātma — personal; māyayā — by the energies; yathā — as; idam — this phenomenal world; sṛjate — does create; viśvam — universe; durvibhāvyam — inconceivable; adhīśvaraiḥ — by the great demigods.
Śrīdhara — Word-for-word
punaḥ — again; veditum icchāmi — I desire to know; idam viśvam — this universe; durvibhāvyam — difficult to conceive; avitarkyam — beyond reasoning; yathā sṛjati — how He creates it.
Transliteration
Bhāgavatam — Transliteration
bhūya eva vivitsāmi
bhagavān ātma-māyayā
yathedaṁ sṛjate viśvaṁ
durvibhāvyam adhīśvaraiḥ
Śrīdhara — Transliteration
punaś ca veditum icchāmi | idaṁ durvibhāvyam avitarkyaṁ viśvaṁ yathā sṛjati ||6||
Śrīdhara Sanskrit
पुनश् च वेदितुम् इच्छामि । इदं दुर्विभाव्यम् अवितर्क्यं विश्वं यथा सृजति ॥६॥

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. Parīkṣit wishes to understand still further how the Lord creates this universe, which is exceedingly difficult to conceive and beyond ordinary reasoning.

ŚB 2.4.7

यथा गोपायति विभुर्यथा संयच्छते पुनः ।
यां यां शक्तिमुपाश्रित्य पुरुशक्तिः परः पुमान् ।
आत्मानं क्रीडयन् क्रीडन् करोति विकरोति च ॥ ७ ॥

Kindly describe how the Supreme Lord, who is all-powerful, engages His different energies and different expansions in maintaining and again winding up the phenomenal world in the sporting spirit of a player.

Word-for-word
Bhāgavatam — Word-for-word
yathā — as; gopāyati — maintains; vibhuḥ — the great; yathā — as; saṁyacchate — winds up; punaḥ — again; yām yām — as; śaktim — energies; upāśritya — by employing; puru-śaktiḥ — the all-powerful; paraḥ — the Supreme; pumān — Personality of Godhead; ātmānam — plenary expansion; krīḍayan — having engaged them; krīḍan — as also personally being engaged; karoti — does them; vikaroti — and causes to be done; ca — and.
Śrīdhara — Word-for-word
gopāyati — protects, maintains; pālayati — sustains; saṁyacchate — draws together; saṁharate — withdraws; puru-śaktiḥ — possessing many powers; bahu-śaktimān — endowed with manifold energies; krīḍan — playing; ātmānam brahmādi-rūpiṇam — Himself in forms such as Brahmā; vikaroti — diversifies; vividhaṁ karoti — makes manifold.
Transliteration
Bhāgavatam — Transliteration
yathā gopāyati vibhur
yathā saṁyacchate punaḥ
yāṁ yāṁ śaktim upāśritya
puru-śaktiḥ paraḥ pumān
ātmānaṁ krīḍayan krīḍan
karoti vikaroti ca
Śrīdhara — Transliteration
gopāyati pālayati | saṁyacchate saṁharate | puru-śaktir bahu-śaktimān | krīḍan yathā karoti | ātmānaṁ brahmādi-rūpiṇaṁ krīḍayan vikaroti vividhaṁ karoti ||7-8||
Śrīdhara Sanskrit
गोपायति पालयति । संयच्छते संहरते । पुरु-शक्तिर् बहु-शक्तिमान् । क्रीडन् यथा करोति । आत्मानं ब्रह्मादि-रूपिणं क्रीडयन् विकरोति विविधं करोति ॥७-८॥

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. The all-powerful Lord protects and withdraws the world through His many energies. Sporting freely, He manifests Himself through forms such as Brahmā and diversifies those manifestations in manifold ways.

ŚB 2.4.8

नूनं भगवतो ब्रह्मन् हरेरद्भुतकर्मणः ।
दुर्विभाव्यमिवाभाति कविभिश्चापि चेष्टितम् ॥ ८ ॥

O learned brāhmaṇa, the transcendental activities of the Lord are all wonderful, and they appear inconceivable because even great endeavors by many learned scholars have still proved insufficient for understanding them.

Word-for-word
Bhāgavatam — Word-for-word
nūnam — still insufficient; bhagavataḥ — of the Personality of Godhead; brahman — O learned brāhmaṇa; hareḥ — of the Lord; adbhuta — wonderful; karmaṇaḥ — one who acts; durvibhāvyam — inconceivable; iva — like that; ābhāti — appears; kavibhiḥ — even by the highly learned; ca — also; api — in spite of; ceṣṭitam — being endeavored for.
Śrīdhara — Word-for-word
gopāyati — protects and maintains; saṁyacchate — withdraws; puru-śaktiḥ — endowed with many powers; krīḍan — sporting; ātmānam brahmādi-rūpiṇam — Himself in forms such as Brahmā; vikaroti — makes manifold.
Transliteration
Bhāgavatam — Transliteration
nūnaṁ bhagavato brahman
harer adbhuta-karmaṇaḥ
durvibhāvyam ivābhāti

Śrīdhara — Transliteration (7–8)
gopāyati pālayati | saṁyacchate saṁharate | puru-śaktir bahu-śaktimān | krīḍan yathā karoti | ātmānaṁ brahmādi-rūpiṇaṁ krīḍayan vikaroti vividhaṁ karoti ||7-8||
Śrīdhara Sanskrit
गोपायति पालयति । संयच्छते संहरते । पुरु-शक्तिर् बहु-शक्तिमान् । क्रीडन् यथा करोति । आत्मानं ब्रह्मादि-रूपिणं क्रीडयन् विकरोति विविधं करोति ॥७-८॥

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. Śrīdhara treats verses 7–8 as one unit: the Lord, endowed with manifold powers, maintains and withdraws the cosmos while freely sporting, manifesting Himself through forms such as Brahmā and making those manifestations diverse.

ŚB 2.4.9

यथा गुणांस्तु प्रकृतेर्युगपत् क्रमशोऽपि वा ।
बिभर्ति भूरिशस्त्वेकः कुर्वन् कर्माणि जन्मभिः ॥ ९ ॥

The Supreme Personality of Godhead is one, whether He alone acts with the modes of material nature, or simultaneously expands in many forms, or expands consecutively to direct the modes of nature.

Word-for-word
Bhāgavatam — Word-for-word
yathā — as they are; guṇān — the modes of; tu — but; prakṛteḥ — of the material energy; yugapat — simultaneously; kramaśaḥ — gradually; api — also; vā — either; bibharti — maintains; bhūriśaḥ — many forms; tu — but; ekaḥ — the supreme one; kurvan — acting; karmāṇi — activities; janmabhiḥ — by incarnations.
Śrīdhara — Word-for-word
ekaḥ — the one Lord; puruṣa-rūpeṇa — in the form of the Puruṣa; yugapat — simultaneously; janmabhiḥ — through births or manifestations; brahmādi-avatāraiḥ — through incarnations such as Brahmā; kramaśaḥ — successively; prakṛteḥ guṇān — the modes of prakṛti; gṛhṇāti — assumes or takes up.
Transliteration
Bhāgavatam — Transliteration
yathā guṇāṁs tu prakṛter
yugapat kramaśo ’pi vā
bibharti bhūriśas tv ekaḥ

Śrīdhara — Transliteration
ekaḥ puruṣa-rūpeṇa yugapat, janmabhir brahmādy-avatāraiḥ kramaśo vā yathā prakṛter guṇān gṛhṇāti ||9||
Śrīdhara Sanskrit
एकः पुरुष-रूपेण युगपत्, जन्मभिर् ब्रह्माद्य्-अवतारैः क्रमशो वा यथा प्रकृतेर् गुणान् गृह्णाति ॥९॥

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. The one Lord, as the Puruṣa, relates to the modes of material nature simultaneously, and through manifestations such as Brahmā He does so successively as well.

ŚB 2.4.10

विचिकित्सितमेतन्मे ब्रवीतु भगवान् यथा ।
शाब्दे ब्रह्मणि निष्णातः परस्मिंश्च भवान्खलु ॥ १० ॥

Kindly clear up all these doubtful inquiries, because you are not only vastly learned in the Vedic literatures and self-realized in transcendence, but are also a great devotee of the Lord and are therefore as good as the Personality of Godhead.

Word-for-word
Bhāgavatam — Word-for-word
vicikitsitam — doubtful inquiry; etat — this; me — of me; bravītu — just clear up; bhagavān — powerful like the Lord; yathā — as much as; śābde — sound transcendental; brahmaṇi — Vedic literature; niṣṇātaḥ — fully realized; parasmin — in transcendence; ca — also; bhavān — your good self; khalu — as a matter of fact.
Śrīdhara — Word-for-word
vicikitsitam — doubtful; sandigdham — uncertain; śābde brahmaṇi — in the Vedic revelation; vicāreṇa niṣṇātaḥ — deeply versed through inquiry; parasmin — in the Supreme Brahman; anubhavena — through direct realization.
Transliteration
Bhāgavatam — Transliteration
vicikitsitam etan me
