Śrīdhara Sanskrit
विचिकित्सितं सन्दिग्धम् । शाब्दे ब्रह्मणि विचारेण निष्णातः परस्मिन्न् अनुभवेन ॥१०॥

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. Śrīdhara glosses the King’s inquiry as one requiring doubts to be resolved. Śukadeva is deeply versed in revealed Brahman through scriptural inquiry and in the Supreme Brahman through direct realization.

ŚB 2.4.11

सूत उवाच
इत्युपामन्त्रितो राज्ञा गुणानुकथने हरेः ।
हृषीकेशमनुस्मृत्य प्रतिवक्तुं प्रचक्रमे ॥ ११ ॥

Sūta Gosvāmī said: When Śukadeva Gosvāmī was thus requested by the King to describe the creative energy of the Personality of Godhead, he then systematically remembered the master of the senses [Śrī Kṛṣṇa], and to reply properly he spoke thus.

Word-for-word
Bhāgavatam — Word-for-word
sūtaḥ uvāca — Sūta Gosvāmī said; iti — thus; upāmantritaḥ — being requested; rājñā — by the King; guṇa-anukathane — in describing the transcendental attributes of the Lord; hareḥ — of the Personality of Godhead; hṛṣīkeśam — the master of the senses; anusmṛtya — properly remembering; prativaktum — just to reply; pracakrame — executed the preliminaries.
Śrīdhara — Word-for-word
upāmantritaḥ — requested; prārthitaḥ — entreated; pracakrame — began; devatā-guru-namaskāra-ādi-rūpam — in the form of obeisance to the deity and guru and the like; upakramam kṛtavān — he performed the introductory act.
Transliteration
Bhāgavatam — Transliteration
sūta uvāca
ity upāmantrito rājñā
guṇānukathane hareḥ
hṛṣīkeśam anusmṛtya
prativaktuṁ pracakrame
Śrīdhara — Transliteration
upāmantritaḥ prārthitaḥ | pracakrame devatā-guru-namaskārādi-rūpam upakramaṁ kṛtavān ity arthaḥ ||11||
Śrīdhara Sanskrit
उपामन्त्रितः प्रार्थितः । प्रचक्रमे देवता-गुरु-नमस्कारादि-रूपम् उपक्रमं कृतवान् इत्य् अर्थः ॥११॥

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. “Requested” means entreated by the King. Śukadeva then began the proper introduction to his reply by performing auspicious acts such as offering obeisance to the deity and to his guru.

ŚB 2.4.12

श्री शुक उवाच
नमः परस्मै पुरुषाय भूयसे
सदुद्भवस्थाननिरोधलीलया ।
गृहीतशक्तित्रितयाय देहिना-
मन्तर्भवायानुपलक्ष्यवर्त्मने ॥ १२ ॥

Śukadeva Gosvāmī said: Let me offer my respectful obeisances unto the Supreme Personality of Godhead, who, for the creation of the material world, accepts the three modes of nature. He is the complete whole residing within the body of everyone, and His ways are inconceivable.

Word-for-word
Bhāgavatam — Word-for-word
śrī-śukaḥ uvāca — Śrī Śukadeva Gosvāmī said; namaḥ — offering obeisances; parasmai — the Supreme; puruṣāya — Personality of Godhead; bhūyase — unto the complete whole; sat-udbhava — the creation of the material world; sthāna — its maintenance; nirodha — and its winding up; līlayā — by the pastime of; gṛhīta — having accepted; śakti — power; tritayāya — three modes; dehinām — of all who possess material bodies; antaḥ-bhavāya — unto He who resides within; anupalakṣya — inconceivable; vartmane — one who has such ways.
Śrīdhara — Word-for-word
parasmai — unto the Supreme; sarvottamāya — the highest of all; bhūyase — of immeasurable greatness; sat — the manifested cosmos; udbhava-ādiṣu — in its creation and other phases; śakti-tritayam — the three powers or guṇas; brahmādi-rūpeṇa — in forms such as Brahmā; antaḥ-bhavāya — the indwelling ruler; antaryāmiṇe — the inner controller; anupalakṣyaṁ vartma — whose way is imperceptible.
Transliteration
Bhāgavatam — Transliteration
śrī-śuka uvāca
namaḥ parasmai puruṣāya bhūyase
sad-udbhava-sthāna-nirodha-līlayā
gṛhīta-śakti-tritayāya dehinām
antarbhavāyānupalakṣya-vartmane
Śrīdhara — Transliteration
tad evāha trayodaśabhiḥ | parasmai sarvottamāya | tatra hetuḥ—bhūyase aparimita-mahimne | tad darśayati | sataḥ prapañcasya udbhavādiṣu nimitta-bhūtā yā līlā tayā gṛhītaṁ brahmādi-rūpeṇa raja-ādi śakti-tritayaṁ yena tasmai | antarbhavāya antaryāmiṇe | ata eva sarvāntaratvād anupalakṣyaṁ vartma yasya ||12||
Śrīdhara Sanskrit
तद् एवाह त्रयोदशभिः । परस्मै सर्वोत्तमाय । तत्र हेतुः—भूयसे अपरिमित-महिम्ने । तद् दर्शयति । सतः प्रपञ्चस्य उद्भवादिषु निमित्त-भूता या लीला तया गृहीतं ब्रह्मादि-रूपेण रज-आदि शक्ति-त्रितयं येन तस्मै । अन्तर्भवाय अन्तर्यामिणे । अत एव सर्वान्तरत्वाद् अनुपलक्ष्यं वर्त्म यस्य ॥१२॥

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. Śrīdhara begins a series of thirteen salutations. The Lord is supreme and of immeasurable greatness. Through His līlā He assumes the three powers associated with rajas and the other guṇas in forms such as Brahmā for the creation, maintenance and dissolution of the cosmos. As the indwelling controller of all beings, His way is beyond ordinary perception.

ŚB 2.4.13

भूयो नमः सद्वृजिनच्छिदेऽसता-
मसम्भवायाखिलसत्त्वमूर्तये ।
पुंसां पुनः पारमहंस्य आश्रमे
व्यवस्थितानामनुमृग्यदाशुषे ॥ १३ ॥

I again offer my respectful obeisances unto the form of complete existence and transcendence, who is the liberator of the pious devotees from all distresses and the destroyer of the further advances in atheistic temperament of the nondevotee-demons. For the transcendentalists who are situated in the topmost spiritual perfection, He grants their specific destinations.

Word-for-word
Bhāgavatam — Word-for-word
bhūyaḥ — again; namaḥ — my obeisances; sat — of the devotees or the pious; vṛjina — distresses; chide — the liberator; asatām — of the atheists, the nondevotee-demons; asambhavāya — cessation of further unhappiness; akhila — complete; sattva — goodness; mūrtaye — unto the Personality; puṁsām — of the transcendentalists; punaḥ — again; pāramahaṁsye — the highest stage of spiritual perfection; āśrame — in the status; vyavasthitānām — particularly situated; anumṛgya — the destination; dāśuṣe — one who delivers.
Śrīdhara — Word-for-word
vicitra-phala-dātṛtvam — His giving of diverse fruits; satām — of those who follow dharma; vṛjina-chide — destroyer of distress; duḥkha-hantre — remover of suffering; asatām — of the unrighteous; asambhavāya — cause of their non-arising or cessation; akhila-sattva-mūrtaye — embodiment of complete sattva; tat-tat-phala-dāya — giver of the corresponding fruits.
Transliteration
Bhāgavatam — Transliteration
bhūyo namaḥ sad-vṛjina-cchide ’satām
asambhavāyākhila-sattva-mūrtaye
puṁsāṁ punaḥ pāramahaṁsya āśrame
vyavasthitānām anumṛgya-dāśuṣe
Śrīdhara — Transliteration
vicitra-phala-dātṛtvam anusmaran praṇamati | bhūyaḥ punaś ca namaḥ | satāṁ dharma-vartināṁ vṛjina-chide duḥkha-hantre | asatām adharma-śīlānām asaṁbhavāyānudbhava-hetave | akhila-sattva-mūrtaye, tat-tad-devatādi-rūpeṇa tat-tat-phaladāyety arthaḥ | samagra-sattva-mūrtaye iti vā | punar iti pūrvoktobhaya-vailakṣaṇyam āha ||13||
Śrīdhara Sanskrit
विचित्र-फल-दातृत्वम् अनुस्मरन् प्रणमति । भूयः पुनश् च नमः । सतां धर्म-वर्तिनां वृजिन-च्छिदे दुःख-हन्त्रे । असताम् अधर्म-शीलानाम् असंभवायानुद्भव-हेतवे । अखिल-सत्त्व-मूर्तये, तत्-तद्-देवतादि-रूपेण तत्-तत्-फलदायेत्य् अर्थः । समग्र-सत्त्व-मूर्तये इति वा । पुनर् इति पूर्वोक्तोभय-वैलक्षण्यम् आह ॥१३॥

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. Remembering the Lord as the giver of diverse fruits, Śukadeva bows again. He destroys the suffering of the righteous and checks the further rise of the unrighteous. As the complete embodiment of sattva, He appears in the respective divine forms and grants the corresponding results.

ŚB 2.4.14

नमो नमस्तेऽस्त्वृषभाय सात्वतां
विदूरकाष्ठाय मुहुः कुयोगिनाम् ।
निरस्तसाम्यातिशयेन राधसा
स्वधामनि ब्रह्मणि रंस्यते नमः ॥ १४ ॥

Let me offer my respectful obeisances unto Him who is the associate of the members of the Yadu dynasty and who is always a problem for the nondevotees. He is the supreme enjoyer of both the material and spiritual worlds, yet He enjoys His own abode in the spiritual sky. There is no one equal to Him because His transcendental opulence is immeasurable.

Word-for-word
Bhāgavatam — Word-for-word
namaḥ namaḥ te — let me offer my obeisances unto You; astu — are; ṛṣabhāya — unto the great associate; sātvatām — of the members of the Yadu dynasty; vidūra-kāṣṭhāya — one who is far from mundane wranglers; muhuḥ — always; ku-yoginām — of the nondevotees; nirasta — vanquished; sāmya — equal status; atiśayena — by greatness; rādhasā — by opulence; sva-dhāmani — in His own abode; brahmaṇi — in the spiritual sky; raṁsyate — enjoys; namaḥ — I do bow down.
Śrīdhara — Word-for-word
sātvatām — of the devotees; ṛṣabhāya — to their protector and foremost; ku-yoginām — of those devoid of devotion; vidūrā kāṣṭhā — whose very direction is far away; durjñeya — difficult to know; nirastam sāmyam atiśayaś ca — equality and superiority are both excluded; rādhasā — by opulence; sva-dhāmani — in His own abode; sva-svarūpe brahmaṇi — in Brahman, His own essential nature; ramamāṇāya — unto Him who delights there.
Transliteration
Bhāgavatam — Transliteration
namo namas te ’stv ṛṣabhāya sātvatāṁ
vidūra-kāṣṭhāya muhuḥ kuyoginām
nirasta-sāmyātiśayena rādhasā
sva-dhāmani brahmaṇi raṁsyate namaḥ
Śrīdhara — Transliteration
sātvatāṁ bhaktānām ṛṣabhāya pālakāya | kuyogināṁ bhakti-hīnānāṁ vidūrā kāṣṭhā dig api yasya | durjñeyety arthaḥ | tad evaṁ vaiṣamya-pratītāv api nirdoṣatvāyācintyam aiśvaryam āha | nirastaṁ sāmyam atiśayaś ca yasya yad apekṣayā anyasya sāmyam atiśayaś ca nāsti, tena rādhasā aiśvaryeṇa sva-dhāmani sva-svarūpe brahmaṇi ramamāṇāya ||14||
Śrīdhara Sanskrit
सात्त्वतां भक्तानाम् ऋषभाय पालकाय । कुयोगिनां भक्ति-हीनानां विदूरा काष्ठा दिग् अपि यस्य । दुर्ज्ञेयेत्य् अर्थः । तद् एवं वैषम्य-प्रतीताव् अपि निर्दोषत्वायाचिन्त्यम् ऐश्वर्यम् आह । निरस्तं साम्यम् अतिशयश् च यस्य यद् अपेक्षया अन्यस्य साम्यम् अतिशयश् च नास्ति, तेन राधसा ऐश्वर्येण स्व-धामनि स्व-स्वरूपे ब्रह्मणि रममाणाय ॥१४॥

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. Śrīdhara identifies the Lord as the protector and foremost of the Sātvatas, while remaining difficult for those devoid of devotion to know. His inconceivable sovereignty admits neither an equal nor a superior, and He delights in His own abode, Brahman understood as His own essential nature.

ŚB 2.4.15

यत्कीर्तनं यत्स्मरणं यदीक्षणं
यद्वन्दनं यच्छ्रवणं यदर्हणम् ।
लोकस्य सद्यो विधुनोति कल्मषं
तस्मै सुभद्रश्रवसे नमो नमः ॥ १५ ॥

Let me offer my respectful obeisances unto the all-auspicious Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa, about whom glorification, remembrances, audience, prayers, hearing and worship can at once cleanse the effects of all sins of the performer.

Word-for-word
Bhāgavatam — Word-for-word
yat — whose; kīrtanam — glorification; yat — whose; smaraṇam — remembrances; yat — whose; īkṣaṇam — audience; yat — whose; vandanam — prayers; yat — whose; śravaṇam — hearing about; yat — whose; arhaṇam — worshiping; lokasya — of all people; sadyaḥ — forthwith; vidhunoti — specifically cleanses; kalmaṣam — effects of sins; tasmai — unto Him; subhadra — all-auspicious; śravase — one who is heard; namaḥ namaḥ — my due obeisances again and again.
Śrīdhara — Word-for-word
sarva-sādhanebhyaḥ — above all other means; bhakteḥ śraiṣṭhyam — the superiority of devotion; anusmaran praṇamati — remembering it, he bows; arhaṇam — worship; pūjanam — adoration; subhadram — auspicious; maṅgalam — blessed; śravaḥ — fame; yaśaḥ — glory.
Transliteration
Bhāgavatam — Transliteration
yat-kīrtanaṁ yat-smaraṇaṁ yad-īkṣaṇaṁ
yad-vandanaṁ yac-chravaṇaṁ yad-arhaṇam
lokasya sadyo vidhunoti kalmaṣaṁ
tasmai subhadra-śravase namo namaḥ
Śrīdhara — Transliteration
sarva-sādhanebhyo bhakteḥ śraiṣṭhyam anusmaran praṇamati—yat-kīrtanam iti dvābhyām | arhaṇaṁ pūjanam | subhadraṁ maṅgalaṁ śravo yaśo yasya tasmai ||15||
Śrīdhara Sanskrit
सर्व-साधनेभ्यो भक्तेः श्रैष्ट्यम् अनुस्मरन् प्रणमति—यत्-कीर्तनम् इति द्वाभ्याम् । अर्हणं पूजनम् । सुभद्रं मङ्गलं श्रवो यशो यस्य तस्मै ॥१५॥

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. Remembering the superiority of bhakti over all other disciplines, Śukadeva bows to the Lord. Arhaṇa means worship, and “Subhadra-śravas” signifies Him whose fame is wholly auspicious.

ŚB 2.4.16

विचक्षणा यच्चरणोपसादनात्
सङ्गं व्युदस्योभयतोऽन्तरात्मनः ।
विन्दन्ति हि ब्रह्मगतिं गतक्लमा-
स्तस्मै सुभद्रश्रवसे नमो नमः ॥ १६ ॥

Let me offer my respectful obeisances again and again unto the all-auspicious Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa. The highly intellectual, simply by surrendering unto His lotus feet, are relieved of all attachments to present and future existences and without difficulty progress toward spiritual existence.

Word-for-word
Bhāgavatam — Word-for-word
vicakṣaṇāḥ — highly intellectual; yat-caraṇa-upasādanāt — simply dedicating oneself unto the lotus feet; saṅgam — attachment; vyudasya — giving up completely; ubhayataḥ — for present and future existence; antaḥ-ātmanaḥ — of the heart and soul; vindanti — moves progressively; hi — certainly; brahma-gatim — toward spiritual existence; gata-klamāḥ — without difficulty; tasmai — unto Him; subhadra — all-auspicious; śravase — unto one who is heard; namaḥ namaḥ — my due obeisances again and again.
Śrīdhara — Word-for-word
vicakṣaṇāḥ — discerning persons; vivekinaḥ — those endowed with discrimination; caraṇayoḥ upasādanāt — by approaching His feet; upasatteḥ bhajanāt — by devotional worship; antarātmanaḥ — of the inner self, the mind; ubhayatra — in both places; iha ca paratra ca — here and hereafter; saṅgam vyudasya — casting away attachment; gata-klamāḥ — free from exertion; prayāsa-rahitāḥ — without laborious effort.
Transliteration
Bhāgavatam — Transliteration
vicakṣaṇā yac-caraṇopasādanāt
saṅgaṁ vyudasyobhayato ’ntar-ātmanaḥ
vindanti hi brahma-gatiṁ gata-klamās
tasmai subhadra-śravase namo namaḥ
Śrīdhara — Transliteration
vicakṣaṇā vivekino yasya caraṇayor upasādanā upasatter bhajanād antarātmano manasa ubhayatreha ca paratra ca saṅgaṁ vyudasya nirasya | gata-klamāḥ prayāsa-rahitāḥ ||16||
Śrīdhara Sanskrit
विचक्षणा विवेकिनो यस्य चरणयोर् उपसादना उपसत्तेर् भजनाद् अन्तरात्मनो मनस उभयत्रेह च परत्र च सङ्गं व्युदस्य निरस्य । गत-क्लमाः प्रयास-रहिताः ॥१६॥

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. The discerning attain this by approaching and worshiping the Lord’s feet. They cast away the mind’s attachments both here and hereafter and, freed from laborious exertion, proceed toward Brahman.

ŚB 2.4.17

तपस्विनो दानपरा यशस्विनो
मनस्विनो मन्त्रविदः सुमङ्गलाः ।
क्षेमं न विन्दन्ति विना यदर्पणं
तस्मै सुभद्रश्रवसे नमो नमः ॥ १७ ॥

Ascetics, generous givers, renowned persons, contemplatives, knowers of sacred mantras and people of exemplary conduct do not attain true welfare unless their accomplishments are offered to Him. To that Lord of auspicious fame I bow again and again.

Word-for-word
Bhāgavatam — Word-for-word
tapasvinaḥ — practitioners of austerity; dāna-parāḥ — devoted to charity; yaśasvinaḥ — renowned persons; manasvinaḥ — contemplatives or yogins; mantra-vidaḥ — knowers of sacred mantras; su-maṅgalāḥ — people of excellent conduct; kṣemam — welfare and secure attainment; na vindanti — do not obtain; vinā — without; yat-arpaṇam — offering to Him; tasmai — to Him; subhadra-śravase — whose fame is auspicious to hear; namo namaḥ — obeisance again and again.
Śrīdhara — Word-for-word
bhakti-śūnyānām — of those devoid of devotion; sarva-sādhana-vaiphalyam — the fruitlessness of every discipline; manasvinaḥ yoginaḥ — the thoughtful are yogins; sumaṅgalāḥ sadācārāḥ — the auspicious are those of good conduct; tapa-ādy-arpaṇam — offering austerity and the rest; prādhānya-jñāpanāya — to indicate the special importance.
Transliteration
Bhāgavatam — Transliteration
tapasvino dāna-parā yaśasvino
manasvino mantra-vidaḥ sumaṅgalāḥ
kṣemaṁ na vindanti vinā yad-arpaṇaṁ
tasmai subhadra-śravase namo namaḥ
Śrīdhara — Transliteration
bhakti-śūnyānāṁ sarva-sādhana-vaiphalyaṁ darśayan namati—tapasvina iti | manasvino yoginaḥ | sumaṅgalāḥ sadācārāḥ | yasmiṁs tapa-ādy-arpaṇaṁ vinā | subhadra-śravase ity āvṛttir yaśaḥ-śravaṇādeḥ prādhānya-jñāpanāya ||17||
Śrīdhara Sanskrit
भक्ति-शून्यानां सर्व-साधन-वैफल्यं दर्शयन् नमति—तपस्विन इति । मनस्विनो योगिनः । सुमङ्गलाः सदाचाराः । यस्मिंस् तप-आद्य्-अर्पणं विना । सुभद्र-श्रवसे इत्य् आवृत्तिर् यशः-श्रवणादेः प्राधान्य-ज्ञापनाय ॥१७॥

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. Śrīdhara uses the verse to show that every discipline is fruitless when devotion is absent. Austerity and the other accomplishments become meaningful through offering them to the Lord; the repeated salutation underscores the primacy of hearing His auspicious fame.

ŚB 2.4.18

किरातहूणान्ध्रपुलिन्दपुल्कशा
आभीरशुम्भा यवनाः खसादयः ।
येऽन्ये च पापा यदपाश्रयाश्रयाः
शुध्यन्ति तस्मै प्रभविष्णवे नमः ॥ १८ ॥

The Kirātas, Hūṇas, Āndhras, Pulindas, Pulkaśas, Ābhīras, Śumbhas, Yavanas, Khasas and others described as sinful become purified when they take shelter of those who themselves have taken shelter in Him. Obeisance to that all-powerful Viṣṇu.

Word-for-word
Bhāgavatam — Word-for-word
kirāta-hūṇa-āndhra-pulinda-pulkaśāḥ — the peoples named Kirāta, Hūṇa, Āndhra, Pulinda and Pulkaśa; ābhīra-śumbhāḥ yavanāḥ khasa-ādayaḥ — Ābhīras, Śumbhas, Yavanas, Khasas and others; ye anye ca — whoever else; pāpāḥ — of sinful conduct; yat-apāśraya-āśrayāḥ — taking shelter of those sheltered in Him; śudhyanti — become purified; tasmai — to Him; prabhaviṣṇave — to the supremely powerful Viṣṇu; namaḥ — obeisance.
Śrīdhara — Word-for-word
bhakteḥ parama-śuddhi-hetutvam — devotion as the supreme cause of purification; pāpa-jātayaḥ — groups regarded as sinful by birth; karmataḥ pāpa-rūpāḥ — those sinful by action; bhāgavatāḥ — devotees of Bhagavān; tad-āśrayāḥ — taking shelter of them; śudhyanti — they are purified; prabhavana-śīla — possessing effective sovereign power.
Transliteration
Bhāgavatam — Transliteration
kirāta-hūṇāndhra-pulinda-pulkaśā
ābhīra-śumbhā yavanāḥ khasādayaḥ
ye ’nye ca pāpā yad-apāśrayāśrayāḥ
śudhyanti tasmai prabhaviṣṇave namaḥ
Śrīdhara — Transliteration
bhakteḥ parama-śuddhi-hetutvaṁ darśayann āha | kirātādayo ye pāpa-jātayo’nye ca ye karmataḥ pāpa-rūpās te yad-upāśrayā bhāgavatās tad-āśrayāḥ santaḥ śudhyanti | asambhāvanā-śaṅkāṁ pariharati, prabhaviṣṇave prabhavana-śīlāyeti ||18||
Śrīdhara Sanskrit
भक्तेः परम-शुद्धि-हेतुत्वं दर्शयन्न् आह । किरातादयो ये पाप-जातयोऽन्ये च ये कर्मतः पाप-रूपास् ते यद्-उपाश्रया भागवतास् तद्-आश्रयाः सन्तः शुध्यन्ति । असम्भावना-शङ्कां परिहरति, प्रभविष्णवे प्रभवन-शीलायेति ॥१८॥

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. The point is the unsurpassed purifying power of devotion. Those considered impure by birth or action become purified by taking shelter of Bhagavān’s devotees. The title Prabhaviṣṇu removes any doubt about whether such purification is possible: the Lord’s power is fully effective.

ŚB 2.4.19

स एष आत्मात्मवतामधीश्वर-
स्त्रयीमयो धर्ममयस्तपोमयः ।
गतव्यलीकैरजशङ्करादिभि-
र्वितर्क्यलिङ्गो भगवान् प्रसीदताम् ॥ १९ ॥

May that Bhagavān be gracious to me. He is the Self and supreme Lord of those established in the self; He is present through the threefold Veda, through dharma and through austerity, and His wondrous form is contemplated by the guileless, including Brahmā, Śaṅkara and others.

Word-for-word
Bhāgavatam — Word-for-word
saḥ eṣaḥ — this very Lord; ātmā — the Self; ātmavatām — of the self-possessed; adhīśvaraḥ — supreme ruler; trayī-mayaḥ — embodied in the threefold Veda; dharma-mayaḥ — embodied in dharma; tapaḥ-mayaḥ — embodied in austerity; gata-vyalīkaiḥ — by those free of deceit; aja-śaṅkara-ādibhiḥ — by Brahmā, Śaṅkara and others; vitarkya-liṅgaḥ — whose manifest form is contemplated in wonder; bhagavān — Bhagavān; prasīdatām — may He be gracious.
Śrīdhara — Word-for-word
sarvopāsyatvam — His being worshipable by all; ātmavatāṁ dhīrāṇām ātmā — the Self of steadfast realized persons; ātmatvena upāsyaḥ — worshiped as the Self; tat-tan-mārgeṇa — through each respective path; niṣkapaṭaiḥ bhaktaiḥ — by guileless devotees; atyāścaryeṇa vīkṣaṇīyam — to be beheld with profound wonder; mūrtiḥ — form.
Transliteration
Bhāgavatam — Transliteration
sa eṣa ātmātmavatām adhīśvaras
trayīmayo dharmamayas tapomayaḥ
gata-vyalīkair aja-śaṅkarādibhir
vitarkya-liṅgo bhagavān prasīdatām
Śrīdhara — Transliteration
sarvopāsyatvam anusmaran prārthayate | sa eṣa ātmavatāṁ dhīrāṇām ātmā | ātmatvenopāsya iti arthaḥ | trayīmayatvādi-viśeṣaṇais tat-tan-mārgeṇopāsyatvaṁ vivakṣitam | gata-vyalīkair niṣkapaṭair bhaktair vitarkyam atyāścaryeṇa vīkṣaṇīyaṁ liṅgaṁ mūrtir yasya sa prasīdatu ||19||
Śrīdhara Sanskrit
सर्वोपास्यत्वम् अनुस्मरन् प्रार्थयते । स एष आत्मवतां धीराणाम् आत्मा । आत्मत्वेनोपास्य इति अर्थः । त्रयीमयत्वादि-विशेषणैस् तत्-तन्-मार्गेणोपास्यत्वं विवक्षितम् । गत-व्यलीकैर् निष्कपटैर् भक्तैर् वितर्क्यम् अत्याश्चर्येण वीक्षणीयं लिङ्गं मूर्तिर् यस्य स प्रसीदतु ॥१९॥

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. Remembering that the Lord is worshipable through every genuine sacred path, Śrīdhara says He is the very Self of the steadfast and is worshiped as such. The Veda, dharma and austerity indicate different approaches to Him, while guileless devotees behold His form with astonishment.

ŚB 2.4.20

श्रियः पतिर्यज्ञपतिः प्रजापति-
र्धियां पतिर्लोकपतिर्धरापतिः ।
पतिर्गतिश्चान्धकवृष्णिसात्वतां
प्रसीदतां मे भगवान् सतां पतिः ॥ २० ॥

May Bhagavān, Lord of the devotees, be gracious to me—the Lord of Śrī, of sacrifice, of creatures, of intelligence, of the worlds and of the earth, and the master and ultimate refuge of the Andhakas, Vṛṣṇis and Sātvatas.

Bhāgavatam — Word-for-word
śriyaḥ patiḥ — Lord of Śrī and prosperity; yajña-patiḥ — Lord of sacrifice; prajā-patiḥ — Lord of created beings; dhiyām patiḥ — Lord of intelligence; loka-patiḥ — Lord of the worlds; dharā-patiḥ — Lord of the earth; patiḥ — protector and master; gatiḥ — goal and refuge; andhaka-vṛṣṇi-sātvatām — of the Andhakas, Vṛṣṇis and Sātvatas; prasīdatām me — may He be gracious to me; bhagavān — Bhagavān; satām patiḥ — Lord of the devotees and righteous.
Śrīdhara — Word-for-word
sarva-pālakatvam — His universal protectorship; gatiḥ — refuge; sarva-āpatsu rakṣakaḥ — protector in every danger.
Transliteration
Bhāgavatam — Transliteration
śriyaḥ patir yajña-patiḥ prajā-patir
dhiyāṁ patir loka-patir dharā-patiḥ
patir gatiś cāndhaka-vṛṣṇi-sātvatāṁ
prasīdatāṁ me bhagavān satāṁ patiḥ
Śrīdhara — Transliteration
sarva-pālakatvam anusmarann āha—śriya iti | gatiś ca sarvāpatsu rakṣakaḥ ||20||
Śrīdhara Sanskrit
सर्व-पालकत्वम् अनुस्मरन्न् आह—श्रिय इति । गतिश् च सर्वापत्सु रक्षकः ॥२०॥

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. Remembering the Lord as the protector of all, Śrīdhara explains gati as the refuge who protects in every danger.

ŚB 2.4.21

यदङ्घ्र्यभिध्यानसमाधिधौतया
धियानुपश्यन्ति हि तत्त्वमात्मनः ।
वदन्ति चैतत् कवयो यथारुचं
स मे मुकुन्दो भगवान् प्रसीदताम् ॥ २१ ॥

May Mukunda, Bhagavān, be pleased with me. By intelligence cleansed through steady meditation on His feet, the wise directly behold the truth of the Self; yet thinkers describe that truth in differing ways according to their own insight.

Word-for-word
Bhāgavatam — Word-for-word
yat-aṅghri — whose feet; abhidhyāna — continuous contemplation; samādhi — absorbed meditation; dhautayā — cleansed by; dhiyā — by intelligence; anupaśyanti — directly perceive; hi — indeed; tattvam — truth; ātmanaḥ — of the Self; vadanti — describe; ca etat — and this; kavayaḥ — sages and thinkers; yathā-rucam — according to their inclination or insight; saḥ me mukundaḥ — that Mukunda; bhagavān prasīdatām — may Bhagavān be gracious to me.
Śrīdhara — Word-for-word
jñāna-pradatvam — His giving of knowledge; anudhyānam eva samādhiḥ — continuous meditation itself is samādhi; dhautayā śodhitayā — washed and purified; yathā-rucam — according to inclination; saguṇa-nirguṇa-ādi-bhedaiḥ — through distinctions such as qualified and unqualified conceptions; yathā-mati — according to understanding.
Transliteration
Bhāgavatam — Transliteration
yad-aṅghry-abhidhyāna-samādhi-dhautayā
dhiyānupaśyanti hi tattvam ātmanaḥ
vadanti caitat kavayo yathā-rucaṁ
sa me mukundo bhagavān prasīdatām
Śrīdhara — Transliteration
jñāna-pradatvam anusmarann āha—yad-aṅghrīti dvābhyām | yasyāṅghryor anudhyānam eva samādhis tena dhautayā śodhitayā | yathā-rucaṁ rucy-anusāreṇa saguṇa-nirguṇādi-bhedaiḥ | yad vā, ruk pratibhā | yathā-matīty arthaḥ ||21||
Śrīdhara Sanskrit
ज्ञान-प्रदत्वम् अनुस्मरन्न् आह—यद्-अङ्घ्रीति द्वाभ्याम् । यस्याङ्घ्र्योर् अनुध्यानम् एव समाधिस् तेन धौतया शोधितया । यथा-रुचं रुच्य्-अनुसारेण सगुण-निर्गुणादि-भेदैः । यद् वा, रुक् प्रतिभा । यथा-मतीत्य् अर्थः ॥२१॥

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. Śrīdhara sees this as remembrance of the Lord’s gift of knowledge. Meditation on His feet is the samādhi that purifies the intellect; sages then describe the realized truth according to their respective insight, including saguṇa and nirguṇa approaches.

ŚB 2.4.22

प्रचोदिता येन पुरा सरस्वती
वितन्वताजस्य सतीं स्मृतिं हृदि ।
स्वलक्षणा प्रादुरभूत् किलास्यतः
स मे ऋषीणामृषभः प्रसीदताम् ॥ २२ ॥

May that foremost of sages be gracious to me—the Lord who, at the beginning, awakened in unborn Brahmā’s heart a true memory concerning creation, thereby inspiring Sarasvatī, marked by her own characteristics, to issue forth from his mouth.

Word-for-word
Bhāgavatam — Word-for-word
pracoditā — inspired; yena — by whom; purā — in the beginning; sarasvatī — Sarasvatī, sacred speech; vitanvatā — by one expanding; ajasya — of unborn Brahmā; satīm smṛtim — true recollection; hṛdi — in his heart; sva-lakṣaṇā — bearing her own defining marks; prādurabhūt — manifested; kila — indeed; āsyataḥ — from his mouth; saḥ me — may He toward me; ṛṣīṇām ṛṣabhaḥ — foremost of sages; prasīdatām — be gracious.
Śrīdhara — Word-for-word
kalpādau — at the beginning of the kalpa; sṛṣṭi-viṣayām — concerning creation; tasya mukhataḥ — from his mouth; śikṣādi-uktāni — described in phonetics and related disciplines; jñāna-pradānām ṛṣabhaḥ — foremost among those who bestow knowledge.
Transliteration
Bhāgavatam — Transliteration
pracoditā yena purā sarasvatī
vitanvatājasya satīṁ smṛtiṁ hṛdi
sva-lakṣaṇā prādurabhūt kilāsyataḥ
sa me ṛṣīṇām ṛṣabhaḥ prasīdatām
Śrīdhara — Transliteration
kiṁ ca, purā kalpādāv ajasya hṛdi satīṁ sṛṣṭi-viṣayāṁ vitanvatā yena pracoditā satī sarasvatī tasya mukhataḥ kila prādurbhūtā | svāni lakṣaṇāni śikṣādy-uktāni yasyāḥ sā | ṛṣīṇāṁ jñāna-pradānām ṛṣabhaḥ śreṣṭhaḥ ||22||
Śrīdhara Sanskrit
किं च, पुरा कल्पादाव् अजस्य हृदि सतीं सृष्टि-विषयां वितन्वता येन प्रचोदिता सती सरस्वती तस्य मुखतः किल प्रादुर्भूता । स्वानि लक्षणानि शिक्षाद्य्-उक्तानि यस्याः सा । ऋषीणां ज्ञान-प्रदानाम् ऋषभः श्रेष्ठः ॥२२॥

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. According to Śrīdhara, at the beginning of the kalpa the Lord expanded true knowledge of creation in Brahmā’s heart; inspired by that, Sarasvatī manifested from Brahmā’s mouth with her proper phonetic characteristics. The Lord is therefore foremost among those who bestow knowledge.

ŚB 2.4.23

भूतैर्महद्भिर्य इमाः पुरो विभु-
र्निर्माय शेते यदमूषु पूरुषः ।
भुङ्क्ते गुणान् षोडश षोडशात्मकः
सोऽलङ्कृषीष्ट भगवान् वचांसि मे ॥ २३ ॥

May Bhagavān adorn my words. The all-pervading Puruṣa forms these bodily cities from the great elements and dwells within them; as the indwelling enlivener of the sixteen constituents, He manifests and sustains their functions.

Word-for-word
Bhāgavatam — Word-for-word
bhūtaiḥ mahadbhiḥ — with the great elements; yaḥ — who; imāḥ puraḥ — these bodily cities; vibhuḥ — the all-pervading Lord; nirmāya — having formed; śete — dwells; yat amūṣu — because within these; pūruṣaḥ — the Puruṣa; bhuṅkte — manifests, enjoys or sustains; guṇān ṣoḍaśa — the sixteen constituents or qualities; ṣoḍaśa-ātmakaḥ — the enlivening self of the sixteen; saḥ alaṅkṛṣīṣṭa — may He adorn; bhagavān — Bhagavān; vacāṁsi me — my words.
Śrīdhara — Word-for-word
śrotṛ-jana-āhlādinīm — delighting the listeners; śṛṅgāra-karuṇa-ādi-śobhām — beauty through aesthetic moods such as tenderness and compassion; antaryāmitām — indwelling rulership; ekādaśendriya-pañcabhūta — the eleven senses and five elements; ṣoḍaśānām ātmā cetayitā — the conscious enlivening self of the sixteen.
Transliteration
Bhāgavatam — Transliteration
bhūtair mahadbhir ya imāḥ puro vibhur
nirmāya śete yad amūṣu pūruṣaḥ
bhuṅkte guṇān ṣoḍaśa ṣoḍaśātmakaḥ
so ’laṅkṛṣīṣṭa bhagavān vacāṁsi me
Śrīdhara — Transliteration
idānīṁ sva-vācyaṁ śrotṛ-janāhlādinīṁ śṛṅgāra-karuṇādi-śobhāṁ prārthayate—bhūtair iti | sa me vacāṁsy alaṅkṛṣīṣṭa alaṅkarotu | anyasya vacasām anyonyālaṅkārāsambhavam āśaṅkya tasyāntaryāmitām āviṣkaroti | yo mahadbhir bhūtair imāḥ puraḥ śarīrāṇi sṛṣṭvā amūṣu pūrṣv antaryāmitayā śete vasati | atra puruṣa-samākhyāṁ pramāṇayati | yad yasmāt puruṣa iti | ata eva ya ekādaśendriya-pañcabhūta-rūpān ṣoḍaśa-guṇān kalā bhuṅkte prakāśayati pālayatīti vā | tadā tv ātmanepadam ārṣam | atra hetuḥ—yataḥ ṣoḍaśānām ātmā cetayitā | svārthe kaḥ | na tv atra jīvatvam ucyate prārthanā-virodhāt ||23||
Śrīdhara Sanskrit
इदानीं स्व-वाच्यं श्रोतृ-जनाह्लादिनीं शृङ्गार-करुणादि-शोभां प्रार्थयते—भूतैर् इति । स मे वचांस्य् अलङ्कृषीष्ट अलङ्करोतु । अन्यस्य वचसाम् अन्योन्यालङ्कारासम्भवम् आशङ्क्य तस्यान्तर्यामिताम् आविष्करोति । यो महद्भिर् भूतैर् इमाः पुरः शरीराणि सृष्ट्वा अमूषु पूर्ष्व् अन्तर्यामितया शेते वसति । अत्र पुरुष-समाख्यां प्रमाणयति । यद् यस्मात् पुरुष इति । अत एव य एकादशेन्द्रिय-पञ्चभूत-रूपान् षोडश-गुणान् कला भुङ्क्ते प्रकाशयति पालयतीति वा । तदा त्व् आत्मनेपदम् आर्षम् । अत्र हेतुः—यतः षोडशानाम् आत्मा चेतयिता । स्वार्थे कः । न त्व् अत्र जीवत्वम् उच्यते प्रार्थना-विरोधात् ॥२३॥

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. Śrīdhara reads this as a prayer that the indwelling Lord beautify Śukadeva’s speech so it delights the audience. The Puruṣa forms bodily “cities” from the great elements and dwells within as antaryāmin, enlivening and sustaining the sixteenfold complex of eleven senses and five elements.

ŚB 2.4.24

नमस्तस्मै भगवते वासुदेवाय वेधसे ।
पपुर्ज्ञानमयं सौम्या यन्मुखाम्बुरुहासवम् ॥ २४ ॥

I bow to that Bhagavān, Vāsudeva in the form of the sage-compiler Vyāsa. Gentle devotees drink the knowledge-filled nectar that flows from his lotus-like mouth.

Word-for-word
Bhāgavatam — Word-for-word
namaḥ tasmai bhagavate — obeisance to that Bhagavān; vāsudevāya — to Vāsudeva; vedhase — to the arranger or creator; papuḥ — drank; jñānam — knowledge; ayam — this; saumyāḥ — gentle and pure devotees; yat-mukha-amburuha — whose lotus-like mouth; āsavam — nectar or intoxicating essence.
Śrīdhara — Word-for-word
śrī-vyāsam namaskaroti — he bows to Śrī Vyāsa; saumyāḥ bhaktāḥ — the gentle ones are devotees; mukha-amburuha — lotus-mouth; āsavaḥ makarandaḥ — the nectar is honey.
Transliteration
Bhāgavatam — Transliteration
namas tasmai bhagavate vāsudevāya vedhase
papur jñānam ayaṁ saumyā yan-mukhāmburuhāsavam
Śrīdhara — Transliteration
śrī-vyāsaṁ namaskaroti—nama iti | saumyā bhaktāḥ | yasya mukhāmburuhe āsavo makaradas tam ||24||
Śrīdhara Sanskrit
श्री-व्यासं नमस्करोति—नम इति । सौम्या भक्ताः । यस्य मुखाम्बुरुहे आसवो मकरदस् तम् ॥२४॥

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. Śrīdhara takes the verse as a salutation to Śrī Vyāsa. The “gentle ones” are devotees, and the nectar of his lotus-mouth is the honey of spiritual knowledge.

ŚB 2.4.25

एतदेवात्मभू राजन् नारदाय विपृच्छते ।
वेदगर्भोऽभ्यधात् साक्षाद् यदाह हरिरात्मनः ॥ २५ ॥

O King, this very teaching was given by self-born Brahmā, who contained the Vedas within himself from the moment of creation, to Nārada when he questioned him—just as Hari Himself had directly taught it to Brahmā.

Word-for-word
Bhāgavatam — Word-for-word
etat eva — this very teaching; ātma-bhūḥ — self-born Brahmā; rājan — O King; nāradāya — to Nārada; vipṛcchate — who questioned thoroughly; veda-garbhaḥ — containing the Vedas within; abhyadhāt — explained; sākṣāt — directly; yat āha — that which was said; hariḥ — Hari; ātmanaḥ — to Brahmā himself.
Śrīdhara — Word-for-word
praśnottaratayā — in question-and-answer form; brahma-nārada-saṁvādam — the dialogue between Brahmā and Nārada; utpatti-samaye eva — at the very moment of creation; vedā garbhe yasya — in whose interior the Vedas were present; sākṣāt hariḥ yad āha — what Hari directly said.
Transliteration
Bhāgavatam — Transliteration
etad evātma-bhū rājan nāradāya vipṛcchate
veda-garbho ’bhyadhāt sākṣād yad āha harir ātmanaḥ
Śrīdhara — Transliteration
idānīṁ praśnottaratayā brahma-nārada-saṁvādaṁ prastauti—etad iti | utpatti-samaya eva vedā garbhe yasya saḥ | sākṣād dharir yad āha tad āha ||25||
Śrīdhara Sanskrit
इदानीं प्रश्नोत्तरतया ब्रह्म-नारद-संवादं प्रस्तौति—एतद् इति । उत्पत्ति-समय एव वेदा गर्भे यस्य सः । साक्षाद् हरिर् यद् आह तद् आह ॥२५॥

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. Śrīdhara says the text now introduces the Brahmā–Nārada dialogue in question-and-answer form. Brahmā possessed the Vedas from the moment of his creation and now repeats to Nārada what Hari directly taught him.
