Chapter 5 — The Cause of All Causes

ŚB 2.5.1

नारद उवाच ।
देवदेव नमस्तेऽस्तु भूतभावन पूर्वज ।
तद्विजानीहि यद् ज्ञानं आत्मतत्त्वनिदर्शनम् ॥ १ ॥

Nārada said: O God of gods, first-born creator of beings, my reverence to you. Please make known to me the knowledge that reveals the truth of the self.

Word-for-word
Bhāgavatam — Word-for-word
nāradaḥ — Nārada; uvāca — said; deva-deva — O God of gods; namaḥ te astu — obeisance be to you; bhūta-bhāvana — nourisher and creator of beings; pūrvaja — first-born; tat vijānīhi — make that known; yat jñānam — that knowledge which; ātma-tattva-nidarśanam — reveals the truth of the self.
Śrīdhara — Word-for-word
nārada-praśnam — Nārada’s question; bhūta-bhāvana — nourisher of beings; pūrvaja — first-born; jñānam — means of knowing; ātma-tattvam — truth of the self; viśeṣeṇa jñāpaya — explain distinctly.
Transliteration
Bhāgavatam — Transliteration
nārada uvāca
devadeva namaste'stu bhūtabhāvana pūrvaja
tadvijānīhi yad jñānaṃ ātmatattvanidarśanam || 1 ||
Śrīdhara — Transliteration
nārada-praśnam āha—deva-deveti | he bhūta-bhāvana, ata eva sarveṣāṃ pūrvaja anāde, jñāyate'neneti jñānaṃ tat sādhanaṃ yat tad vijānīhi | viśeṣeṇa jñāpayety arthaḥ | kathambhūtam? ātma-tattvaṃ nitarāṃ dṛśyate yena tat ||1||
Śrīdhara Sanskrit
नारद-प्रश्नम् आह—देव-देवेति । हे भूत-भावन, अत एव सर्वेषां पूर्वज अनादे, ज्ञायतेऽनेनेति ज्ञानं तत् साधनं यत् तद् विजानीहि । विशेषेण ज्ञापयेत्य् अर्थः । कथम्भूतम्? आत्म-तत्त्वं नितरां दृश्यते येन तत् ॥१॥

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. Śrīdhara says Nārada’s question seeks the knowledge by which the truth of the self is directly discerned, and asks Brahmā to explain it distinctly.

ŚB 2.5.2

यद् रूपं यद् अधिष्ठानं यतः सृष्टमिदं प्रभो ।
यत्संस्थं यत्परं यच्च तत् तत्त्वं वद तत्त्वतः ॥ २ ॥

O lord, tell me truly: what is this universe’s form and foundation, from what is it created, in what does it abide and dissolve, upon what does it depend, and what is its essential nature?

Word-for-word
Bhāgavatam — Word-for-word
yat rūpam — what form; yat adhiṣṭhānam — what foundation; yataḥ — from what; sṛṣṭam idam — this is created; prabho — O lord; yat-saṃstham — in what it abides or dissolves; yat-param — upon what it depends; yat ca — and what; tat tattvam — that essential truth; vada tattvataḥ — tell truly.
Śrīdhara — Word-for-word
viśvam — the universe; ātma-jñāna-sādhanam — means to knowledge of the self; rūpaṃ — form; adhiṣṭhānam — support; saṃstham — that in which it dissolves; param — that on which it depends; tattvam — reality; yāthārthyam — true nature.
Transliteration
Bhāgavatam — Transliteration
yad rūpaṃ yad adhiṣṭhānaṃ yataḥ sṛṣṭamidaṃ prabho
yatsaṃsthaṃ yatparaṃ yacca tat tattvaṃ vada tattvataḥ || 2 ||
Śrīdhara — Transliteration
upalakṣaṇa-bhūtaṃ viśvam evātma-jñāna-sādhanam atas tad-viśeṣaṃ pṛcchati | yad rūpaṃ yena rūpyate prakāśyate | yad adhiṣṭhānaṃ yad āśrayam | yato yena sṛṣṭam | yat saṃsthaṃ yasmiṃl līyate | yat paraṃ yad adhīnam | yac ceti yad ātmakam | svataḥ sat-kāraṇato vety arthaḥ | tasya tattvaṃ yāthārthyaṃ tattvato vada ||2||
Śrīdhara Sanskrit
उपलक्षण-भूतं विश्वम् एवात्म-ज्ञान-साधनम् अतस् तद्-विशेषं पृच्छति । यद् रूपं येन रूप्यते प्रकाश्यते । यद् अधिष्ठानं यद् आश्रयम् । यतो येन सृष्टम् । यत् संस्थं यस्मिंल् लीयते । यत् परं यद् अधीनम् । यच् चेति यद् आत्मकम् । स्वतः सत्-कारणतो वेत्य् अर्थः । तस्य तत्त्वं याथार्थ्यं तत्त्वतो वद ॥२॥

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. He treats the universe itself as a means to self-knowledge: Nārada therefore asks for its form, support, origin, dissolution, dependence and true nature.

ŚB 2.5.3

सर्वं ह्येतद् भवान् वेद भूतभव्यभवत्प्रभुः ।
करामलकवद् विश्वं विज्ञानावसितं तव ॥ ३ ॥

You know all this, ruler of what has been, will be, and is becoming; the universe rests fully within your knowledge like a fruit held in the palm.

Word-for-word
Bhāgavatam — Word-for-word
sarvam — all; hi etat — indeed this; bhavān veda — you know; bhūta-bhavya-bhavat-prabhuḥ — lord of past, future and present; kara-āmalaka-vat — like an āmalaka in the hand; viśvam — the universe; vijñāna-avasitam — fully settled in knowledge; tava — yours.
Śrīdhara — Word-for-word
bhūtam — what has arisen; bhavyam — what will arise; bhavat — what is arising; prabhuḥ — lord; viśiṣṭena jñānena — by distinguished knowledge; avasitam — ascertained; niścitam — settled.
Transliteration
Bhāgavatam — Transliteration
sarvaṃ hyetad bhavān veda bhūtabhavyabhavatprabhuḥ
karāmalakavad viśvaṃ vijñānāvasitaṃ tava || 3 ||
Śrīdhara — Transliteration
na jānāmīti na vaktavyam ity āha—sarvam iti | bhūtaṃ jātaṃ, bhavyaṃ janiṣyamāṇaṃ, bhavat jāyamānaṃ, teṣāṃ prabhur yataḥ, ato viśiṣṭena jñānenāvasitaṃ niścitam ||3||
Śrīdhara Sanskrit
न जानामीति न वक्तव्यम् इत्य् आह—सर्वम् इति । भूतं जातं, भव्यं जनिष्यमाणं, भवत् जायमानं, तेषां प्रभुर् यतः, अतो विशिष्टेन ज्ञानेनावसितं निश्चितम् ॥३॥

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. Brahmā cannot plead ignorance, Śrīdhara notes, because as lord of past, future and present he has definitively ascertained the universe through superior knowledge.

ŚB 2.5.4

यद् विज्ञानो यद् आधारो यत् परस्त्वं यदात्मकः ।
एकः सृजसि भूतानि भूतैरेवात्ममायया ॥ ४ ॥

From whom comes your knowledge, upon what do you rest, whom do you serve as supreme, and what is your own nature? Alone, through your own māyā, you create beings from the elements.

Word-for-word
Bhāgavatam — Word-for-word
yat-vijñānaḥ — having knowledge from whom; yat-ādhāraḥ — resting on whom; yat-paraḥ — devoted or subordinate to whom; tvam — you; yat-ātmakaḥ — of what nature; ekaḥ — alone; sṛjasi — you create; bhūtāni — beings; bhūtaiḥ — through the elements; eva — indeed; ātma-māyayā — by your own māyā.
Śrīdhara — Word-for-word
vijñāna-daḥ — giver of knowledge; ādhāraḥ — support; paraḥ — superior or object of service; ātmakam — essential nature; svatantraḥ — independent; parameśvaraḥ — Supreme Lord; ekaḥ — alone; asahāyaḥ — without assistance.
Transliteration
Bhāgavatam — Transliteration
yad vijñāno yad ādhāro yat parastvaṃ yadātmakaḥ
ekaḥ sṛjasi bhūtāni bhūtairevātmamāyayā || 4 ||
Śrīdhara — Transliteration
āstām idam, ādau tāvat tvām eva kathayety āha | yato vijñānaṃ yasya | kas tava vijñāna-da ity arthaḥ | yad-ādhāraḥ kas tavāśrayaḥ | yat-paro yad-adhīnaḥ | yad-ātmako yat-svarūpaḥ | mama tu tvam eva svatantraḥ parameśvara iti buddhiḥ | tava tapaś-caraṇena tu parāśaṅkayā pṛcchāmīty āha sārdhaiś caturbhiḥ | eko'sahāyaḥ ||4||
Śrīdhara Sanskrit
आस्ताम् इदम्, आदौ तावत् त्वाम् एव कथयेत्य् आह । यतो विज्ञानं यस्य । कस् तव विज्ञान-द इत्य् अर्थः । यद्-आधारः कस् तवाश्रयः । यत्-परो यद्-अधीनः । यद्-आत्मको यत्-स्वरूपः । मम तु त्वम् एव स्वतन्त्रः परमेश्वर इति बुद्धिः । तव तपश्-चरणेन तु पराशङ्कया पृच्छामीत्य् आह सार्धैश् चतुर्भिः । एकोऽसहायः ।।4।।

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. Śrīdhara reads Nārada’s questions as probing the source of Brahmā’s knowledge, support, allegiance and essential nature, despite Nārada’s initial belief that Brahmā is independent.

ŚB 2.5.5

आत्मन् भावयसे तानि न पराभावयन् स्वयम् ।
आत्मशक्तिमवष्टभ्य ऊर्णनाभिरिवाक्लमः ॥ ५ ॥

Abiding in yourself, you sustain them without being overcome, drawing upon your own power tirelessly, like a spider producing its web from itself.

Word-for-word
Bhāgavatam — Word-for-word
ātman — in yourself; bhāvayase — you sustain; tāni — them; na parābhāvayan — without being overcome; svayam — yourself; ātma-śaktim — your own power; avaṣṭabhya — taking support of; ūrṇanābhiḥ iva — like a spider; aklamaḥ — without fatigue.
Śrīdhara — Word-for-word
ātmani — in oneself; bhāvayase — you sustain; pālayasi — you protect; aklamaḥ — tireless; śrama-rahitaḥ — without fatigue; ūrṇanābhiḥ — spider; ātma-śaktim — own power; sṛjati — creates.
Transliteration
Bhāgavatam — Transliteration
ātman bhāvayase tāni na parābhāvayan svayam
ātmaśaktimavaṣṭabhya ūrṇanābhirivāklamaḥ || 5 ||
Śrīdhara — Transliteration
ātmani bhāvayase pālayasi svayam eva parābhāvam aprāpayan aklamaḥ śrama-rahitaḥ | yathorṇanābhir ātmana eva śaktim avaṣṭabhya sṛjati tadvat ||5||
Śrīdhara Sanskrit
आत्मनि भावयसे पालयसि स्वयम् एव पराभवम् अप्रापयन् अक्लमः श्रम-रहितः । यथोर्णनाभिर् आत्मन एव शक्तिम् अवष्टभ्य सृजति तद्वत् ।।5।।

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. The comparison with the spider shows effortless creation and maintenance from one’s own power, without dependence on an external material source.

ŚB 2.5.6

नाहं वेद परं ह्यस्मिन् नापरं न समं विभो ।
नामरूपगुणैर्भाव्यं सदसत् किञ्चिदन्यतः ॥ ६ ॥

In this universe I know nothing higher, lower, or equal to you, O mighty one, nor anything—gross or subtle, known by name, form, or quality—that arises from another source.

Word-for-word
Bhāgavatam — Word-for-word
na aham veda — I do not know; param — higher; hi asmin — in this; na aparam — nor lower; na samam — nor equal; vibho — O mighty one; nāma-rūpa-guṇaiḥ — by name, form and qualities; bhāvyam — brought into being; sat-asat — gross and subtle / manifest and unmanifest; kiñcit — anything; anyataḥ — from another source.
Śrīdhara — Word-for-word
param — superior; aparam — inferior; samam — equal; madhyamam — intermediate; nāma-rūpam — name and form; guṇaḥ — quality; sat-asat — gross and subtle; tvattaḥ eva — from you alone.
Transliteration
Bhāgavatam — Transliteration
nāhaṃ veda paraṃ hyasmin nāparaṃ na samaṃ vibho
nāmarūpaguṇairbhāvyaṃ sadasat kiñcidanyataḥ || 6 ||
Śrīdhara — Transliteration
tasmād ahaṃ tv asmin viśvasmin param uttamam aparam adhamaṃ samaṃ madhyamaṃ ca | tatrāpi nāma manuṣyādi-rūpaṃ dvipadatvādi-guṇaḥ śuklatvādis tair bhāvyaṃ sādhyaṃ, tatrāpi sad-asat-sthūlaṃ sūkṣmaṃ ca kiṃcid apy anyato na veda kintu tvatta eva sarvaṃ bhavatīti manye ||6||
Śrīdhara Sanskrit
तस्माद् अहं त्व् अस्मिन् विश्वस्मिन् परम् उत्तमम् अपरम् अधमं समं मध्यमं च । तत्रापि नाम मनुष्यादि-रूपं द्विपदत्वादि-गुणः शुक्लत्वादिस् तैर् भाव्यं साध्यं, तत्रापि सद्-असत्-स्थूलं सूक्ष्मं च किंचिद् अप्य् अन्यतो न वेद किन्तु त्वत्त एव सर्वं भवतीति मन्ये ।।6।।

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. Nārada sees nothing superior, inferior or equal to Brahmā, and supposes that all names, forms, qualities, gross things and subtle things arise from him alone.

ŚB 2.5.7

स भवानचरद् घोरं यत्तपः सुसमाहितः ।
तेन खेदयसे नस्त्वं पराशङ्कां च यच्छसि ॥ ७ ॥

Yet you, though so exalted, performed severe austerity with full concentration; by this you perplex us and make us suspect that there is someone higher than you.

Word-for-word
Bhāgavatam — Word-for-word
saḥ bhavān — that very you; acarat — performed; ghoram — severe; yat tapaḥ — that austerity; su-samāhitaḥ — fully concentrated; tena — by that; khedayase — you perplex; naḥ — us; tvam — you; para-āśaṅkām — suspicion of a higher being; ca yacchasi — and you give rise to.
Śrīdhara — Word-for-word
tathā-vidhaḥ api — although so exalted; tapaḥ acarat — performed austerity; khedayase — you perplex; mohayasi — you bewilder; parā-śaṅkām — suspicion of something higher; īśvara-antara-āśaṅkām — doubt about another Lord; prayacchasi — you produce.
Transliteration
Bhāgavatam — Transliteration
sa bhavānacarad ghoraṃ yattapaḥ susamāhitaḥ
tena khedayase nastvaṃ parāśaṅkāṃ ca yacchasi
Śrīdhara — Transliteration
sa tathā-vidho'pi bhavāṃs tapo'carad iti yat tena no'smān khedayase mohayasi | yataḥ parā-śaṅkām īśvarāntarāśaṅkāṃ prayacchasi ||7||
Śrīdhara Sanskrit
स तथा-विधोऽपि भवांस् तपोऽचरद् इति यत् तेन नोऽस्मान् खेदयसे मोहयसि । यतः परा-शङ्काम् ईश्वरान्तराशङ्कां प्रयच्छसि ।।7।।

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. Although Brahmā appears supreme within the universe, his austerity naturally raises Nārada’s question whether another Lord stands above him.

ŚB 2.5.8

एतन्मे पृच्छतः सर्वं सर्वज्ञ सकलेश्वर ।
विजानीहि यथैवेदं अहं बुध्येऽनुशासितः ॥ ८ ॥

Therefore, omniscient Lord of all, explain all this I ask so that, instructed by you, I may understand it exactly.

Word-for-word
Bhāgavatam — Word-for-word
etat — this; me — to me; pṛcchataḥ — who is asking; sarvam — all; sarva-jña — O omniscient one; sakala-īśvara — Lord of all; vijānīhi — explain distinctly; yathā eva — exactly so that; idam — this; aham budhye — I may understand; anuśāsitaḥ — instructed.
Śrīdhara — Word-for-word
anuśāsitaḥ — instructed; śikṣitaḥ — taught; budhye — may I understand; budhyeyam — I should comprehend; vijānīhi — make known; viśeṣeṇa jñāpaya — explain distinctly.
Transliteration
Bhāgavatam — Transliteration
etanme pṛcchataḥ sarvaṃ sarvajña sakaleśvara
vijānīhi yathaivedaṃ ahaṃ budhye'nuśāsitaḥ
Śrīdhara — Transliteration
yathaivāhaṃ tvayā'nuśāsitaḥ śikṣitaḥ san budhye budhyeyaṃ tathā vijānīhi viśeṣeṇa jñāpaya ||8||
Śrīdhara Sanskrit
यथैवाहं त्वयाऽनुशासितः शिक्षितः सन् बुध्ये बुध्येयं तथा विजानीहि विशेषेण ज्ञापय ।।8।।

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. Nārada asks not merely for information but for an explanation clear enough that, once instructed by Brahmā, he may truly understand.

ŚB 2.5.9

ब्रह्मोवाच ।
सम्यक् कारुणिकस्येदं वत्स ते विचिकित्सितम् ।
यदहं चोदितः सौम्य भगवद्वीर्यदर्शने ॥ ९ ॥

Brahmā said: My gentle son, your compassionate doubt is excellent, for by your question I am prompted to reveal the majesty of the Lord.

Word-for-word
Bhāgavatam — Word-for-word
brahmā uvāca — Brahmā said; samyak — excellent, proper; kāruṇikasya — of the compassionate one; idam — this; vatsa — O son; te — your; vicikitsitam — doubt or inquiry; yat aham coditaḥ — because I am prompted; saumya — O gentle one; bhagavat-vīrya-darśane — to reveal the Lord’s majesty.
Śrīdhara — Word-for-word
praśnam abhinandati — he praises the question; vatsa — son; vicikitsitam — doubt; samyak — proper; kāruṇikasya — of the compassionate; bhagavat-vīrya-prakāśane — in revealing the Lord’s glory; pravartitaḥ asmi — I am prompted; kṛpām — mercy.
Transliteration
Bhāgavatam — Transliteration
brahmovāca
samyak kāruṇikasyedaṃ vatsa te vicikitsitam
yadahaṃ coditaḥ saumya bhagavadvīryadarśane
Śrīdhara — Transliteration
praśnam abhinandati | vatsa! he putra! tavedaṃ vicikitsitaṃ sandehaḥ tat-pūrvakaḥ praśno'yaṃ samyag ity arthaḥ | yataḥ kāruṇikasya tavāyaṃ praśnaḥ | atra hetuḥ—yat yataḥ para-dharma-pradarśane bhagavad-vīrya-prakāśane pravartito'smi | atas tvaṃ jijñāsur api mayi kṛpām eva kṛtavān ity arthaḥ ||9||
Śrīdhara Sanskrit
प्रश्नम् अभिनन्दति । वत्स! हे पुत्र! तवेदं विचिकित्सितं सन्देहः तत्-पूर्वकः प्रश्नोऽयं सम्यग् इत्य् अर्थः । यतः कारुणिकस्य तवायं प्रश्नः । अत्र हेतुः—यत् यतः पर-धर्म-प्रदर्शने भगवद्-वीर्य-प्रकाशने प्रवर्तितोऽस्मि । अतस् त्वं जिज्ञासुर् अपि मयि कृपाम् एव कृतवान् इत्य् अर्थः ।।9।।

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. Śrīdhara says Brahmā welcomes the question because it gives him the opportunity to disclose Bhagavān’s glory; Nārada’s inquiry is therefore itself an act of compassion.

ŚB 2.5.10

नानृतं तव तच्चापि यथा मां प्रब्रवीषि भोः ।
अविज्ञाय परं मत्त एतावत्त्वं यतो हि मे ॥ १० ॥

What you say of me is not altogether false, because such power is indeed present in me; yet you speak without knowing the Supreme who is beyond me.

Word-for-word
Bhāgavatam — Word-for-word
na anṛtam — not false; tava tat ca api — that statement of yours also; yathā — as; mām prabravīṣi — you describe me; bhoḥ — O dear one; avijñāya — not knowing; param — the Supreme; mattaḥ — beyond me; etāvat-tvam — this measure of greatness; yataḥ — because; hi me — indeed is mine.
Śrīdhara — Word-for-word
īśvaratvena — as Lord; nātyantam anṛtam — not wholly false; prabhāvasya — of power; etāvat-tvam — such greatness; mattaḥ param īśvaram — the Lord beyond me; avijñāya — not knowing; sādṛśyāt — because of resemblance; bhrāntiḥ — mistaken understanding.
Transliteration
Bhāgavatam — Transliteration
nānṛtaṃ tava taccāpi yathā māṃ prabravīṣi bhoḥ
avijñāya paraṃ matta etāvattvaṃ yato hi me
Śrīdhara — Transliteration
nanu tvam eva bhagavān ity uktaṃ mayā ekaḥ sṛjasi bhūtāni ity-ādinā | satyaṃ, yathā mām īśvaratvena prabhāṣase tad api tava bhāṣaṇaṃ nātyantam anṛtam | yataḥ kāraṇād etāvat prabhāvasya bhāva etāvat tvaṃ me asti, kintu mattaḥ param īśvaram avijñāya brūṣe | ataḥ sādṛśyāt taveyaṃ bhrāntir na tu buddhi-pūrvam anṛtam ity arthaḥ ||10||
Śrīdhara Sanskrit
ननु त्वम् एव भगवान् इत्य् उक्तं मया एकः सृजसि भूतानि इत्य्-आदिना । सत्यं, यथा माम् ईश्वरत्वेन प्रभाषसे तद् अपि तव भाषणं नात्यन्तम् अनृतम् । यतः कारणाद् एतावत् प्रभावस्य भाव एतावत् त्वं मे अस्ति, किन्तु मत्तः परम् ईश्वरम् अविज्ञाय ब्रूषे । अतः सादृश्यात् तवेयं भ्रान्तिर् न तु बुद्धि-पूर्वम् अनृतम् इत्य् अर्थः ।।10।।

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. Brahmā possesses extraordinary creative power, so Nārada’s praise is understandable; the mistake lies only in not yet knowing the Supreme Lord beyond Brahmā.

ŚB 2.5.11

येन स्वरोचिषा विश्वं रोचितं रोचयाम्यहम् ।
यथार्कोऽग्निः यथा सोमो यथा ऋक्षग्रहतारकाः ॥ ११ ॥

By His own radiance the universe is illumined, and by that light I make it manifest—just as the sun, fire, moon, constellations, planets and stars shine by borrowed light.

Word-for-word
Bhāgavatam — Word-for-word
yena — by whom; sva-rociṣā — by His own radiance; viśvam — the universe; rocitam — illumined; rocayāmi aham — I make manifest; yathā arkaḥ — as the sun; agniḥ — fire; yathā somaḥ — as the moon; ṛkṣa-graha-tārakāḥ — constellations, planets and stars.
Śrīdhara — Word-for-word
īśvaraḥ — the Lord; bhaktyā — with devotion; sva-prakāśena — by His own radiance; prakāśitam — illumined; sṛṣṭyā abhivyaktam — manifested through creation; arkādayaḥ — the sun and others; caitanya-prakāśyam — illumined by consciousness.
Transliteration
Bhāgavatam — Transliteration
yena svarociṣā viśvaṃ rocitaṃ rocayāmyaham
yathārko'gniḥ yathā somo yathā ṛkṣagrahatārakāḥ
Śrīdhara — Transliteration
tarhi ko'sāv īśvara ity apekṣāyāṃ bhaktyā namaskurvann eva taṃ kathayati tribhiḥ | yena sva-prakāśena rocitaṃ prakāśitam eva prakāśayāmi sṛṣṭyā'bhivyaktaṃ karomi | yathā'rkādayaś caitanya-prakāśyam eva prakāśayanti ||11||
Śrīdhara Sanskrit
तर्हि कोऽसाव् ईश्वर इत्य् अपेक्षायां भक्त्या नमस्कुर्वन्न् एव तं कथयति त्रिभिः । येन स्व-प्रकाशेन रोचितं प्रकाशितम् एव प्रकाशयामि सृष्ट्याऽभिव्यक्तं करोमि । यथाऽर्कादयश् चैतन्य-प्रकाश्यम् एव प्रकाशयन्ति ।।11।।

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. Śrīdhara explains that Brahmā manifests only what is already illumined by the Lord’s own light, just as the luminous bodies reveal what ultimately depends upon a higher illumination.

ŚB 2.5.12

तस्मै नमो भगवते वासुदेवाय धीमहि ।
यन्मायया दुर्जयया मां वदन्ति जगद्‍गुरुम् ॥ १२ ॥

I bow in meditation to Bhagavān Vāsudeva; by His unconquerable māyā people call me the teacher and creator of the world.

Word-for-word
Bhāgavatam — Word-for-word
tasmai — unto Him; namaḥ — obeisance; bhagavate — to Bhagavān; vāsudevāya — Vāsudeva; dhīmahi — we meditate; yat-māyayā — by whose māyā; durjayayā — unconquerable; mām — me; vadanti — they call; jagat-gurum — teacher of the world.
Śrīdhara — Word-for-word
tasmai namaḥ — obeisance to Him; dhīmahi — we meditate; yasya māyayā — by whose māyā; vimohitāḥ — deluded; jagad-gurum — teacher of the world; jagat-kāraṇam — cause of the world; vadanti — they call.
Transliteration
Bhāgavatam — Transliteration
tasmai namo bhagavate vāsudevāya dhīmahi
yanmāyayā durjayayā māṃ vadanti jagad‍gurum
Śrīdhara — Transliteration
tasmai namo dhīmahi | yasya māyayā vimohitāḥ santo yuṣmad-ādayo māṃ jagad-guruṃ jagat-kāraṇaṃ vadanti tasmai ||12||
Śrīdhara Sanskrit
तस्मै नमो धीमहि । यस्य मायया विमोहिताः सन्तो युष्मद्-आदयो मां जगद्-गुरुं जगत्-कारणं वदन्ति तस्मै ।।12।।

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. Brahmā directs the honor away from himself toward Vāsudeva, explaining that māyā causes observers to mistake the secondary creator for the ultimate cause.

ŚB 2.5.13

विलज्जमानया यस्य स्थातुमीक्षापथेऽमुया ।
विमोहिता विकत्थन्ते ममाहमिति दुर्धियः ॥ १३ ॥

vilajjamānayā yasya
sthātum īkṣā-pathe ’muyā
vimohitā vikatthante
mamāham iti durdhiyaḥ

Word-for-word

vilajjamānayā — by her who is ashamed; yasya — whose; sthātum — to remain; īkṣā-pathe — within the range of sight; amuyā — by that illusory potency; vimohitāḥ — bewildered; vikatthante — they boast; mama — mine; aham — I; iti — thus; durdhiyaḥ — those of misguided understanding.

Translation

That illusory potency is ashamed to remain before His sight; yet those whose understanding is bewildered by her boast in terms of “I” and “mine.”

Śrīdhara Svāmī — Bhāvārthadīpikā

यन्माययेति मायासम्बन्धोक्तेस्तस्या दुर्जयत्वोक्तेश्च तस्यापि किमस्ति संसारो नैवेत्याह—विलज्जमानयेति । मत्कपटमसौ जानातीति यस्य दृष्टिपथे स्थातुं विलज्जमानयैव तस्मिन् स्वकार्यमकुर्वत्या अमुया मायया विमोहिता अस्मदादयो दुर्धियोऽविद्यावृतज्ञाना एव केवलं विकत्थन्ते श्लाघन्ते । अनेन यद्रूपमित्यादितृतीयप्रश्नस्योत्तरमुक्तं भवति ॥१३॥

Śrīdhara Svāmī — Translation

Having said that the Lord is related to māyā and that she is difficult to overcome, Brahmā now shows that the Lord Himself is not subject to worldly bondage. Māyā is as though ashamed to act before the Lord, knowing that He sees through her deception. Those such as ourselves, however, when our knowledge is covered by ignorance, become deluded by her and boast of “I” and “mine.” This also answers the earlier question concerning the Lord’s true nature.

ŚB 2.5.14

द्रव्यं कर्म च कालश्च स्वभावो जीव एव च ।
वासुदेवात्परो ब्रह्मन् न चान्योऽर्थोऽस्ति तत्त्वतः ॥ १४ ॥

dravyaṁ karma ca kālaś ca
svabhāvo jīva eva ca
vāsudevāt paro brahman
na cānyo ’rtho ’sti tattvataḥ

Word-for-word

dravyam — substance or material cause; karma — action and its consequence; ca — and; kālaḥ — time; svabhāvaḥ — inherent nature; jīvaḥ — the living being; eva — indeed; vāsudevāt — than Vāsudeva / apart from Vāsudeva; paraḥ — higher; brahman — O brāhmaṇa; na — not; anyaḥ arthaḥ — any other reality; asti — exists; tattvataḥ — in truth.

Translation

O brāhmaṇa, substance, action, time, inherent nature and the living being have no reality higher than Vāsudeva; in truth, nothing exists independently of Him.

Śrīdhara Svāmī — Bhāvārthadīpikā

तदेवं स्वस्मादन्यं परमेश्वरं निरूप्येदानीं यदधिष्ठानमित्यादिनवप्रश्नानां स एवाधिष्ठानादिकं सर्वमित्युत्तरं वक्तुं तद्व्यतिरेकेणान्यस्यासत्त्वमाह—द्रव्यमिति ॥१४॥

Śrīdhara Svāmī — Translation

Having established a Supreme Lord distinct from himself, Brahmā now prepares to answer Nārada’s questions beginning with “What is its foundation?” The answer is that the Lord Himself is the foundation and all the rest. Therefore he first states that nothing possesses independent existence apart from Him.

ŚB 2.5.15

नारायणपरा वेदा देवा नारायणाङ्गजाः ।
नारायणपरा लोका नारायणपरा मखाः ॥ १५ ॥

nārāyaṇa-parā vedā
devā nārāyaṇāṅgajāḥ
nārāyaṇa-parā lokā
nārāyaṇa-parā makhāḥ

Word-for-word

nārāyaṇa-parāḥ — having Nārāyaṇa as their highest object or source; vedāḥ — the Vedas; devāḥ — the gods; nārāyaṇa-aṅga-jāḥ — born from the limbs of Nārāyaṇa; lokāḥ — the worlds; makhāḥ — sacrifices.

Translation

The Vedas have Nārāyaṇa as their supreme object; the gods arise from His limbs. The worlds are directed toward Nārāyaṇa, and sacrifices likewise have Nārāyaṇa as their end.

Śrīdhara Svāmī — Bhāvārthadīpikā

तत्प्रपञ्चयति द्वाभ्याम् । नारायणः परः कारणं येषां ते । अनेनैव शास्त्रयोनित्वप्रतिपादनेनेश्वरे प्रमाणं सर्वज्ञत्वादिकं चोक्तम् । देवाश्च तदङ्गाज्जाता अतो न तद्व्यतिरिक्ताः । लोकाः स्वर्गादयस्तदानन्दांशाः । मखास्तत्साधनभूताः ॥१५॥

Śrīdhara Svāmī — Translation

Brahmā expands the point in two verses. Nārāyaṇa is the supreme cause of all these. By saying that scripture has Him as its source and object, His authority and omniscience are also indicated. The gods arise from His limbs and therefore are not independent of Him; the worlds participate in a portion of His bliss, and sacrifices are means directed to Him.

ŚB 2.5.16

नारायणपरो योगो नारायणपरं तपः ।
नारायणपरं ज्ञानं नारायणपरा गतिः ॥ १६ ॥

nārāyaṇa-paro yogo
nārāyaṇa-paraṁ tapaḥ
nārāyaṇa-paraṁ jñānaṁ
nārāyaṇa-parā gatiḥ

Word-for-word

nārāyaṇa-paraḥ — having Nārāyaṇa as the highest aim; yogaḥ — yoga; tapaḥ — austerity; jñānam — knowledge; gatiḥ — destination or final attainment.

Translation

Yoga culminates in Nārāyaṇa; austerity is for Nārāyaṇa; knowledge has Nārāyaṇa as its supreme object, and the final destination is Nārāyaṇa.

Śrīdhara Svāmī — Bhāvārthadīpikā

योगोऽष्टाङ्गः । ज्ञानं तत्त्वविवेकः । गतिः फलम् ॥१६॥

Śrīdhara Svāmī — Translation

“Yoga” means the eight-limbed discipline; “knowledge” means discrimination of reality; and “destination” means the final fruit or attainment.

ŚB 2.5.17

तस्यापि द्रष्टुरीशस्य कूटस्थस्याखिलात्मनः ।
सृज्यं सृजामि सृष्टोऽहमीक्षयैवाभिचोदितः ॥ १७ ॥

tasyāpi draṣṭur īśasya
kūṭa-sthasyākhilātmanaḥ
sṛjyaṁ sṛjāmi sṛṣṭo ’ham
īkṣayaivābhicoditaḥ

Word-for-word

tasya — of Him; api — indeed; draṣṭuḥ — the seer; īśasya — the Lord; kūṭa-sthasya — unchanging; akhila-ātmanaḥ — the Self of all; sṛjyam — what is to be created; sṛjāmi — I create; sṛṣṭaḥ aham — I myself was created; īkṣayā eva — by His glance alone; abhicoditaḥ — impelled.

Translation

I myself am created, and I create what is to be created only because I am impelled by the glance of that Lord—the seer, the unchanging one and the Self of all.

Śrīdhara Svāmī — Bhāvārthadīpikā

तर्हि त्वं कथं स्रष्टेत्याशङ्क्य स्वस्य तदधीनेश्वरत्वमाह—तस्येति । न विक्रियत इति कूटस्थः । तस्यैवेक्षया इच्छामात्रेणाभितः प्रेरितोऽहं सृष्टः सृज्यं जगत्सृजामि ॥१७॥

Śrīdhara Svāmī — Translation

One might ask, “Then how are you the creator?” Brahmā answers that his lordship is subordinate to the Supreme. “Unchanging” means that the Lord undergoes no transformation. By that Lord’s mere glance or will, Brahmā is created and impelled to create the world that is to be manifested.

ŚB 2.5.18

सत्त्वं रजस्तम इति निर्गुणस्य गुणास्त्रयः ।
स्थितिसर्गनिरोधेषु गृहीता मायया विभोः ॥ १८ ॥

sattvaṁ rajas tama iti
nirguṇasya guṇās trayaḥ
sthiti-sarga-nirodheṣu
gṛhītā māyayā vibhoḥ

Word-for-word

sattvam — goodness; rajaḥ — passion; tamaḥ — darkness or ignorance; iti — thus; nirguṇasya — of Him who is beyond the guṇas; guṇāḥ trayaḥ — the three guṇas; sthiti — maintenance; sarga — creation; nirodheṣu — and dissolution; gṛhītāḥ — taken up; māyayā — through māyā; vibhoḥ — by the all-powerful Lord.

Translation

Goodness, passion and ignorance are the three guṇas. Though the all-powerful Lord is beyond them, through māyā they are taken up for maintenance, creation and dissolution.

Śrīdhara Svāmī — Bhāvārthadīpikā

ननु कुतोऽयं जीवेश्वरविभागः, यतस्त्वं प्रेर्यः स च प्रेरकः स्यात्, इत्यपेक्षायां जीवेश्वरविभागहेतुमाह त्रिभिः—सत्त्वमिति । निर्गुणस्यापीश्वरस्यैते त्रयो गुणा बध्नन्तीत्युत्तरेणान्वयः । कथम्भूताः । तेनैव स्वातन्त्र्येण स्थित्याद्यर्थं मायया गृहीताः ॥१८॥

Śrīdhara Svāmī — Translation

If Brahmā is impelled while the Supreme impels him, what accounts for the distinction between the individual soul and the Lord? Śrīdhara says the next three verses explain it through the guṇas. The Lord is intrinsically beyond the guṇas; by His own sovereignty they are taken up through māyā as instruments for maintenance and the other cosmic functions.

ŚB 2.5.19

कार्यकारणकर्तृत्वे द्रव्यज्ञानक्रियाश्रयाः ।
बध्नन्ति नित्यदा मुक्तं मायिनं पुरुषं गुणाः ॥ १९ ॥

kārya-kāraṇa-kartṛtve
dravya-jñāna-kriyāśrayāḥ
badhnanti nityadā muktaṁ
māyinaṁ puruṣaṁ guṇāḥ

Word-for-word

kārya — effect; kāraṇa — instrument or cause; kartṛtve — in the condition of agency; dravya — material elements; jñāna — knowledge and its presiding powers; kriyā — action and the senses of action; āśrayāḥ — having these as their bases; badhnanti — bind; nityadā muktam — eternally free in itself; māyinam — associated with māyā; puruṣam — the living person; guṇāḥ — the modes.

Translation

The guṇas, taking materiality, cognition and activity as their bases, bind the living person through identification with effect, instrument and agency, although the self is in itself always free.

Śrīdhara Svāmī — Bhāvārthadīpikā

वस्तुतः सर्वदा मुक्तमपि मायिनं मायाविषयं पुरुषं जीवं बध्नन्ति क्व । कार्यमधिभूतं, कारणमध्यात्मं, कर्ताधिदैवतं तेषां भावस्तत्त्वं तस्मिन् । द्रव्यं महाभूतानि, ज्ञानशब्देन देवताः, क्रिया इन्द्रियाणि, तदाश्रयास्तेषां कारणभूतास्तत्तदभिमानेन बध्नन्ति ॥१९॥

Śrīdhara Svāmī — Translation

Though the living self is in reality always free, the guṇas bind it when it becomes subject to māyā. “Effect” refers to the adhibhūta aspect, “instrument” to the adhyātma aspect and “agent” to the presiding adhidevata. The material elements, their deities and the senses become the bases of bondage through identification with each of them.

ŚB 2.5.20

स एष भगवान् लिङ्गैस्त्रिभिरेतैरधोक्षजः ।
स्वलक्षितगतिर्ब्रह्मन् सर्वेषां मम चेश्वरः ॥ २० ॥

sa eṣa bhagavān liṅgais
tribhir etair adhokṣajaḥ
svalakṣita-gatir brahman
sarveṣāṁ mama ceśvaraḥ

Word-for-word

saḥ eṣaḥ — that very Lord; bhagavān — the Supreme Lord; liṅgaiḥ tribhiḥ etaiḥ — by these three characteristics or coverings; adhokṣajaḥ — He who is beyond material sense perception; su-alakṣita-gatiḥ — whose true nature is not touched or fully marked by them; brahman — O brāhmaṇa; sarveṣām — of everyone; mama ca — and of me; īśvaraḥ — the Lord.

Translation

That very Bhagavān, Adhokṣaja, remains untouched in His true nature by these three coverings. O brāhmaṇa, He is the Lord of everyone, including me.

Śrīdhara Svāmī — Bhāvārthadīpikā

अतः स एष वश्यमाय एभिर्गुणैर्लिङ्गैर्जीवानामावरकैरुपाधिभिः । एष नित्यमुक्तो भगवानधोक्षजः श्रीवासुदेवः । एतैस्त्रिभिर्लिङ्गैर्गुणैः स्वलक्षिता गतिः सुष्ठ्वलक्षिता अस्पृष्टा गतिस्तत्त्वं यस्य सः । सर्वेषां मम च ईश्वर इति त्वमपि जानासि, तथापि यत्पृष्टं तत्कथितम् ॥२०॥

Śrīdhara Svāmī — Translation

Thus the living beings are covered by these guṇas as limiting marks, whereas Bhagavān Adhokṣaja, Śrī Vāsudeva, is eternally free. His essential state is wholly untouched by the three guṇas. He is the Lord of all and of Brahmā himself; Nārada already knows this, but Brahmā states it in answer to his question.

ŚB 2.5.21

कालं कर्म स्वभावं च मायेशो मायया स्वया ।
आत्मन् यदृच्छया प्राप्तं विबुभूषुरुपाददे ॥ २१ ॥

kālaṁ karma svabhāvaṁ ca
māyeśo māyayā svayā
ātman yadṛcchayā prāptaṁ
vibubhūṣur upādade

Word-for-word

kālam — time; karma — accumulated action; svabhāvam — inherent disposition or transforming nature; ca — and; māyā-īśaḥ — the Lord of māyā; māyayā svayā — through His own māyā; ātman — within Himself; yadṛcchayā — by His own will; prāptam — present or obtained; vibubhūṣuḥ — desiring to become manifold; upādade — He accepted or took up.

Translation

Desiring to manifest Himself in manifold forms, the Lord of māyā, by His own will and through His own potency, took up time, karma and inherent nature, all present within Himself.

Śrīdhara Svāmī — Bhāvārthadīpikā

तस्य सृष्टिप्रकारमाह—कालमिति । कर्म जीवादृष्टम् । विबुभूषुर्विविधं भवितुमिच्छन् ॥२१॥

Śrīdhara Svāmī — Translation

This verse begins the account of His mode of creation. “Karma” means the unseen accumulated result belonging to the living beings. “Desiring to become manifold” means intending the manifestation of diversity.

ŚB 2.5.22

कालाद्गुणव्यतिकरः परिणामः स्वभावतः ।
कर्मणो जन्म महतः पुरुषाधिष्ठितादभूत् ॥ २२ ॥

kālād guṇa-vyatikaraḥ
pariṇāmaḥ svabhāvataḥ
karmaṇo janma mahataḥ
puruṣādhiṣṭhitād abhūt

Word-for-word

kālāt — from time; guṇa-vyatikaraḥ — disturbance or intermixture of the guṇas; pariṇāmaḥ — transformation; svabhāvataḥ — through inherent nature; karmaṇaḥ — through karma; janma — manifestation; mahataḥ — of the mahat-tattva; puruṣa-adhiṣṭhitāt — under the superintendence of the Puruṣa; abhūt — occurred.

Translation

Through time the equilibrium of the guṇas was disturbed; through inherent nature transformation occurred; and through karma the mahat-tattva came forth, all under the superintendence of the Puruṣa.

Śrīdhara Svāmī — Bhāvārthadīpikā

गुणानां व्यतिकरः क्षोभः साम्यत्यागः । स्वभावतः परिणामो रूपान्तरापत्तिः । पुरुष ईश्वरस्तेनाधिष्ठितत्वं त्रयाणां विशेषणम् । महतो महत्तत्त्वस्य ॥२२॥

Śrīdhara Svāmī — Translation

The “disturbance of the guṇas” is their agitation, the abandonment of equilibrium. Transformation means assuming another condition. The Puruṣa here is the Lord; His superintendence qualifies all three processes. “Mahat” means the mahat-tattva.

ŚB 2.5.23

महतस्तु विकुर्वाणाद् रजःसत्त्वोपबृंहितात् ।
तमःप्रधानस्त्वभवद् द्रव्यज्ञानक्रियात्मकः ॥ २३ ॥

mahatas tu vikurvāṇād
rajaḥ-sattvopabṛṁhitāt
tamaḥ-pradhānas tv abhavad
dravya-jñāna-kriyātmakaḥ

Word-for-word

mahataḥ — from the mahat-tattva; vikurvāṇāt — undergoing transformation; rajaḥ-sattva-upabṛṁhitāt — strengthened by rajas and sattva; tamaḥ-pradhānaḥ — predominantly tamas; tu — then; abhavat — arose; dravya-jñāna-kriyā-ātmakaḥ — possessing the potencies of substance, cognition and activity.

Translation

As the mahat-tattva transformed and was strengthened by rajas and sattva, there arose a principle predominantly of tamas, containing the powers of material substance, cognition and activity.

Śrīdhara Svāmī — Bhāvārthadīpikā

महत्तत्त्वाद्विकुर्वाणाद्विक्रियमाणात् तस्य च क्रियाज्ञानशक्तित्वात् त्रिगुणत्वेऽपि रजःसत्त्वाभ्यामुपबृंहितत्वम्, अहंकारस्य त्वावरणकत्वात् तमःप्रधानत्वम् ॥२३॥

Śrīdhara Svāmī — Translation

The mahat-tattva is here described as undergoing modification. Although it contains all three guṇas, rajas and sattva are emphasized because of its powers of activity and knowledge. Ahaṅkāra, by contrast, is predominantly tamas because of its covering function.

ŚB 2.5.24

सोऽहङ्कार इति प्रोक्तो विकुर्वन् समभूत्त्रिधा ।
वैकारिकस्तैजसश्च तामसश्चेति यद्भिदा ।
द्रव्यशक्तिः क्रियाशक्तिर्ज्ञानशक्तिरिति प्रभो ॥ २४ ॥

so ’haṅkāra iti prokto
vikurvan samabhūt tridhā
vaikārikas taijasaś ca
tāmasaś ceti yad-bhidā
dravya-śaktiḥ kriyā-śaktir
jñāna-śaktir iti prabho

Word-for-word

saḥ — that; ahaṅkāraḥ — ahaṅkāra, the principle of individuation; iti proktaḥ — is so called; vikurvan — transforming; samabhūt tridhā — became threefold; vaikārikaḥ — the sāttvika form; taijasaḥ — the rājasa form; tāmasaḥ — the tāmasa form; yad-bhidā — whose divisions; dravya-śaktiḥ — material-producing potency; kriyā-śaktiḥ — potency of action; jñāna-śaktiḥ — potency of knowledge; prabho — O master.

Translation

That principle is called ahaṅkāra. As it transformed, it became threefold: vaikārika, taijasa and tāmasa, corresponding to the potencies of knowledge, action and material manifestation, O master.

Śrīdhara Svāmī — Bhāvārthadīpikā

स च विकुर्वन् रूपान्तरं गच्छन् । त्रैविध्यमेवाह । वैकारिकः सात्त्विकः । तैजसो राजसः । यद्भिदा यस्य भेदः । द्रव्यशक्तिरित्यादीनि प्रातिलोम्येन त्रयाणां लक्षणानि । द्रव्ये महाभूताख्ये शक्तिर्यस्य । क्रियास्विन्द्रियेषु शक्तिर्यस्य । ज्ञानेषु वेदेषु शक्तिर्यस्य । हे प्रभो बोद्धुं शक्त ॥२४॥

Śrīdhara Svāmī — Translation

As ahaṅkāra undergoes transformation it assumes three forms. Vaikārika is sāttvika and taijasa is rājasa. The three potency-terms are to be connected in reverse order: the tāmasa form has potency in the great elements, the rājasa form in the senses and actions, and the sāttvika form in knowledge. “O master” addresses Nārada as one capable of understanding this.

ŚB 2.5.25

तामसादपि भूतादेर्विकुर्वाणादभून्नभः ।
तस्य मात्रा गुणः शब्दो लिङ्गं यद्द्रष्टृदृश्ययोः ॥ २५ ॥

tāmasād api bhūtāder
vikurvāṇād abhūn nabhaḥ
tasya mātrā guṇaḥ śabdo
liṅgaṁ yad draṣṭṛ-dṛśyayoḥ

Word-for-word

tāmasāt — from the tāmasa form; api — then; bhūta-ādeḥ — from ahaṅkāra as the source of the elements; vikurvāṇāt — undergoing transformation; abhūt — arose; nabhaḥ — space; tasya — its; mātrā — subtle element; guṇaḥ — quality; śabdaḥ — sound; liṅgam — indicating sign; yat — which; draṣṭṛ-dṛśyayoḥ — of the perceiver and the perceived.

Translation

From the transforming tāmasa aspect of ahaṅkāra, the source of the elements, space arose. Its subtle element and quality is sound, the sign by which the perceiver and the perceived are indicated.

Śrīdhara Svāmī — Bhāvārthadīpikā

भूतादेरिति तामसस्य विशेषणम् । तस्य नभसो मात्रा गुणः सूक्ष्मावस्था शब्दः । कीदृशः । द्रष्टृदृश्ययोर्लिङ्गमनुमानव्यञ्जकः । द्रष्टारं वक्तारं शरीरेणादृष्ट्वा शब्देनैवानुमिनोतीत्यर्थः ॥२५॥

Śrīdhara Svāmī — Translation

“Source of the elements” qualifies the tāmasa ahaṅkāra. Sound is the subtle condition and quality of space. It serves as an inferential sign of perceiver and perceived: even when the speaker is not seen bodily, the speaker can be inferred from sound.

ŚB 2.5.26

नभसोऽथ विकुर्वाणादभूत्स्पर्शगुणोऽनिलः ।
परान्वयाच्छब्दवांश्च प्राण ओजः सहो बलम् ॥ २६ ॥

nabhaso ’tha vikurvāṇād
abhūt sparśa-guṇo ’nilaḥ
parānvayāc chabdavāṁś ca
prāṇa ojaḥ saho balam

Word-for-word

nabhasaḥ — from space; atha — then; vikurvāṇāt — as it transformed; abhūt — arose; sparśa-guṇaḥ — having touch as its distinctive quality; anilaḥ — air; para-anvayāt — through inheritance from the preceding element; śabdavān — possessing sound; ca — also; prāṇaḥ — vital force; ojaḥ — vigor; sahaḥ — endurance; balam — strength.

Translation

As space transformed, air arose with touch as its distinctive quality. By inheriting the preceding quality, air also possesses sound, and it functions as vital force, vigor, endurance and bodily strength.

Śrīdhara Svāmī — Bhāvārthadīpikā

परान्वयात्पूर्वभूताकाशानुवृत्तेः शब्दवानपि । प्राण इन्द्रियदेहबलम् । दैहिकचेष्टाहेतुत्वात् । ओज इन्द्रियपाटवम् । सहो मानसबलं क्षुत्पिपासादिसहनसामर्थ्यम् । बलं दैहिकम् ॥२६॥

Śrīdhara Svāmī — Translation

Because the quality of the preceding element, space, continues into it, air also possesses sound. Prāṇa is the power that enables bodily and sensory activity; ojas is sensory vigor; sahas is mental endurance, such as the capacity to bear hunger and thirst; and bala is bodily strength.

ŚB 2.5.27

वायोरपि विकुर्वाणात् कालकर्मस्वभावतः ।
उदपद्यत तेजो वै रूपवत् स्पर्शशब्दवत् ॥ २७ ॥

vāyor api vikurvāṇāt
kāla-karma-svabhāvataḥ
udapadyata tejo vai
rūpavat sparśa-śabdavat

Word-for-word

vāyoḥ — from air; api — then; vikurvāṇāt — undergoing transformation; kāla-karma-svabhāvataḥ — through time, karma and inherent nature; udapadyata — arose; tejaḥ — fire; vai — indeed; rūpavat — possessing form; sparśa-vat — possessing touch; śabda-vat — possessing sound.

Translation

As air transformed under the influence of time, karma and inherent nature, fire arose, possessing form as well as the inherited qualities of touch and sound.

Śrīdhara Svāmī — Bhāvārthadīpikā

उदपद्यत तेज इति । यथा शब्दवान्नभसस्तथा शब्दवत् । एवं रूपवत् ॥२७॥

Śrīdhara Svāmī — Translation

Fire arises in this way. Just as the quality of sound continues from space into air, the qualities of preceding elements continue onward; fire is thus characterized by form while retaining the earlier qualities.

ŚB 2.5.28

तेजसस्तु विकुर्वाणादासीदम्भो रसात्मकम् ।
रूपवत् स्पर्शवच्चाम्भो घोषवच्च परान्वयात् ॥ २८ ॥

tejasas tu vikurvāṇād
āsīd ambho rasātmakam
rūpavat sparśavac cāmbho
ghoṣavac ca parānvayāt

Word-for-word

tejasaḥ — from fire; tu — then; vikurvāṇāt — undergoing transformation; āsīt — arose; ambhaḥ — water; rasa-ātmakam — characterized by taste; rūpavat — possessing form; sparśavat — possessing touch; ghoṣavat — possessing sound; para-anvayāt — because the preceding qualities continue into it.

Translation

From transforming fire came water, whose distinctive nature is taste. Through continuity with the preceding elements, water also possesses form, touch and sound.

Śrīdhara Svāmī — Bhāvārthadīpikā

तेजसो रूपाख्यमपि गुणं प्राप्तमिति रूपवत् । एवमम्भोऽपि घोषवच्छब्दवत् ॥२८॥

Śrīdhara Svāmī — Translation

Water receives the quality called form from fire and is therefore said to possess form; in the same way it also possesses sound inherited from the earlier elements.

ŚB 2.5.29

विशेषस्तु विकुर्वाणादम्भसो गन्धवानभूत् ।
परान्वयाद् रसस्पर्शशब्दरूपगुणान्वितः ॥ २९ ॥

viśeṣas tu vikurvāṇād
ambhaso gandhavān abhūt
parānvayād rasa-sparśa-
śabda-rūpa-guṇānvitaḥ

Word-for-word

viśeṣaḥ — the particular element, earth; tu — then; vikurvāṇāt — from the transformation; ambhasaḥ — of water; gandhavān — possessing smell; abhūt — arose; para-anvayāt — by inheritance from preceding elements; rasa — taste; sparśa — touch; śabda — sound; rūpa — form; guṇa-anvitaḥ — endowed with these qualities.

Translation

From the transformation of water arose the particular element earth, characterized by smell and, through inheritance from preceding elements, endowed also with taste, touch, sound and form.

Śrīdhara Svāmī — Bhāvārthadīpikā

विशेषः पृथ्वी । पृथिव्याश्च परान्वयाधिक्याद्गुणाधिक्यम् ॥२९॥

Śrīdhara Svāmī — Translation

The “particular” element is earth. Because earth inherits the qualities of all the preceding elements, it possesses the greatest number of qualities.

ŚB 2.5.30

वैकारिकान्मनो जज्ञे देवा वैकारिका दश ।
दिग्वातार्कप्रचेतोऽश्विवह्नीन्द्रोपेन्द्रमित्रकाः ॥ ३० ॥

vaikārikān mano jajñe
devā vaikārikā daśa
dig-vātārka-praceto ’śvi-
vahnīndropendra-mitrakāḥ

Word-for-word

vaikārikāt — from the vaikārika or sāttvika ahaṅkāra; manaḥ — the mind; jajñe — arose; devāḥ — presiding deities; vaikārikāḥ — produced from the same sāttvika principle; daśa — ten; dik — the directions; vāta — Vāyu; arka — Sūrya; pracetaḥ — Pracetas/Varuṇa; aśvi — the Aśvins; vahni — Agni; indra — Indra; upendra — Upendra; mitra — Mitra; kāḥ — Ka/Prajāpati.

Translation

From the vaikārika, or sāttvika, ahaṅkāra arose the mind and ten presiding deities: the directions, Vāyu, Sūrya, Pracetas, the Aśvins, Agni, Indra, Upendra, Mitra and Ka.

Śrīdhara Svāmī — Bhāvārthadīpikā

मनःशब्देनैव तदधिष्ठाता चन्द्रोऽपि द्रष्टव्यः । अन्ये च दश देवा वैकारिकाः सात्त्विकाहंकारकार्याः । तानाह । दिशश्च वातश्च अर्कश्च प्रचेताश्च अश्विनौ च, एते पञ्च श्रोत्रत्वक्चक्षुर्जिह्वाघ्राणानामधिष्ठातारः । वह्निश्च इन्द्रश्च उपेन्द्रश्च मित्रश्च कश्च प्रजापतिः, एते पञ्च वाक्पाणिपादपायूपस्थानामधिष्ठातारः ॥३०॥

Śrīdhara Svāmī — Translation

The word “mind” also includes its presiding deity, the Moon. The other ten deities are likewise products of sāttvika ahaṅkāra. The directions, Vāyu, Sūrya, Pracetas and the Aśvins preside respectively over hearing, touch, sight, taste and smell; Agni, Indra, Upendra, Mitra and Ka or Prajāpati preside over speech, hands, feet, anus and the generative organ.

ŚB 2.5.31

तैजसात् तु विकुर्वाणादिन्द्रियाणि दशाभवन् ।
ज्ञानशक्तिः क्रियाशक्तिर्बुद्धिः प्राणश्च तैजसौ ।
श्रोत्रं त्वग्घ्राणदृग्जिह्वा वाग्दोर्मेढ्राङ्घ्रिपायवः ॥ ३१ ॥

taijasāt tu vikurvāṇād
indriyāṇi daśābhavan
jñāna-śaktiḥ kriyā-śaktir
buddhiḥ prāṇaś ca taijasau
śrotraṁ tvag-ghrāṇa-dṛg-jihvā
vāg-dor-meḍhrāṅghri-pāyavaḥ

Word-for-word

taijasāt — from the taijasa, or rājasa, ahaṅkāra; tu — then; vikurvāṇāt — undergoing transformation; indriyāṇi — the senses; daśa — ten; abhavan — arose; jñāna-śaktiḥ — the power of cognition; kriyā-śaktiḥ — the power of action; buddhiḥ — intelligence; prāṇaḥ — vital force; ca — and; taijasau — both belonging to the taijasa principle; śrotram — hearing; tvak — touch; ghrāṇa — smell; dṛk — sight; jihvā — taste; vāk — speech; doḥ — the hand; meḍhra — the generative organ; aṅghri — the feet; pāyavaḥ — the organ of evacuation.

Translation

From the transforming taijasa, or rājasa, ahaṅkāra arose the ten senses. Intelligence and prāṇa, embodying the powers of cognition and action, are likewise taijasa. The ten are hearing, touch, smell, sight and taste, together with speech, the hands, the generative organ, the feet and the organ of evacuation.

Śrīdhara Svāmī — Bhāvārthadīpikā

यतो ज्ञानशक्तिर्बुद्धिः क्रियाशक्तिः प्राणश्च तैजसाहंकारकार्यौ । अतो ज्ञानक्रियाविशेषरूपाणीन्द्रियाण्यपि तैजसादभवन्नित्यर्थः । तान्याह—श्रोत्रमिति । दोः पाणिः । मेढ्रमुपस्थः । क्रमस्त्वत्र न विवक्षितः ॥३१॥

Śrīdhara Svāmī — Translation

Because intelligence, the power of cognition, and prāṇa, the power of action, are products of taijasa ahaṅkāra, the senses—which are particular forms of cognition and action—also arise from the taijasa principle. The verse then names them. “Doḥ” means the hand and “meḍhra” the generative organ; the sequence of the senses is not intended to be strictly paired here.

ŚB 2.5.32

यदैतेऽसङ्गता भावा भूतेन्द्रियमनोगुणाः ।
यदायतननिर्माणे न शेकुर्ब्रह्मवित्तम ॥ ३२ ॥

yadaite ’saṅgatā bhāvā
bhūtendriya-mano-guṇāḥ
yad-āyatana-nirmāṇe
na śekur brahma-vittama

Word-for-word

yadā — when; ete — these; asaṅgatāḥ — uncombined; bhāvāḥ — principles; bhūta — the elements; indriya — the senses; manaḥ — the mind; guṇāḥ — and their qualities or modes; yat-āyatana-nirmāṇe — in constructing that which serves as their abode; na śekuḥ — they were unable; brahma-vittama — O best knower of Brahman.

Translation

O best knower of Brahman, while these principles—the elements, senses, mind and their qualities—remained uncombined, they were unable to construct the bodily abode in which they could function.

Śrīdhara Svāmī — Bhāvārthadīpikā

एवं कारणसृष्टिमुक्त्वा कार्यसृष्टिमाह । यदा एते असङ्गता अमीलिता आसन्, अत एव यदा आयतनस्य शरीरस्य निर्माणे न शेकुः ॥३२॥

Śrīdhara Svāmī — Translation

Having described the creation of the causal principles, Brahmā now describes the creation of their effects. At first these principles were separate and had not combined; for that very reason they could not construct their abode, the body.

ŚB 2.5.33

तदा संहत्य चान्योन्यं भगवच्छक्तिचोदिताः ।
सदसत्त्वमुपादाय चोभयं ससृजुर्ह्यदः ॥ ३३ ॥

tadā saṁhatya cānyonyaṁ
bhagavac-chakti-coditāḥ
sad-asattvam upādāya
cobhayaṁ sasṛjur hy adaḥ

Word-for-word

tadā — then; saṁhatya — having combined; ca — and; anyonyam — with one another; bhagavat-śakti-coditāḥ — impelled by the power of Bhagavān; sat-asattvam — the condition of primary and secondary, or manifest and subtle existence; upādāya — taking up; ca — and; ubhayam — both; sasṛjuḥ — they produced; hi — indeed; adaḥ — that.

Translation

Then, impelled by Bhagavān’s power, they combined with one another. Taking up both primary and secondary, gross and subtle conditions, they produced the twofold cosmic embodiment.

Śrīdhara Svāmī — Bhāvārthadīpikā

तदा सदसत्त्वं प्रधानगुणभावमुपादाय स्वीकृत्य । उभयं समष्टिव्यष्ट्यात्मकं शरीरम् ॥३३॥

Śrīdhara Svāmī — Translation

Here “sat-asattva” is taken as the condition of principal and subordinate manifestation. Accepting that condition, the principles produced the twofold body consisting of the collective and individual forms.

ŚB 2.5.34

वर्षपूगसहस्रान्ते तदण्डमुदके शयम् ।
कालकर्मस्वभावस्थो जीवोऽजीवमजीवयत् ॥ ३४ ॥

varṣa-pūga-sahasrānte
tad aṇḍam udake śayam
kāla-karma-svabhāva-stho
jīvo ’jīvam ajīvayat

Word-for-word

varṣa-pūga-sahasra-ante — after thousands of groups of years; tat aṇḍam — that cosmic egg; udake — in the water; śayam — lying; kāla — time; karma — karma; svabhāva-sthaḥ — established in or working through inherent nature; jīvaḥ — the life-giving Supreme Person; ajīvam — lifeless; ajīvayat — caused to live, animated.

Translation

After thousands upon thousands of years, the cosmic egg lay in the waters. The life-giving Supreme Person, working through time, karma and inherent nature, animated that which had been lifeless.

Śrīdhara Svāmī — Bhāvārthadīpikā

कालकर्मस्वभावानधिष्ठाय स्थितो जीवयतीति जीवः परमात्मा अजीवमचेतनमजीवयच्चेतयति स्म ॥३४॥

Śrīdhara Svāmī — Translation

The Supreme Self is called “jīva” here in the sense of the One who gives life. Established as the ruler of time, karma and inherent nature, He caused the lifeless and unconscious cosmic egg to become conscious and animated.

ŚB 2.5.35

स एव पुरुषस्तस्मादण्डं निर्भिद्य निर्गतः ।
सहस्रोर्वङ्घ्रिबाह्वक्षः सहस्राननशीर्षवान् ॥ ३५ ॥

sa eva puruṣas tasmād
aṇḍaṁ nirbhidya nirgataḥ
sahasrorv-aṅghri-bāhv-akṣaḥ
sahasrānana-śīrṣavān

Word-for-word

saḥ eva — that very; puruṣaḥ — Cosmic Person; tasmāt — from that; aṇḍam — cosmic egg; nirbhidya — having broken or divided it open; nirgataḥ — emerged; sahasra — thousands; ūru — thighs; aṅghri — feet; bāhu — arms; akṣaḥ — eyes; ānana — faces; śīrṣavān — possessing heads.

Translation

That very Puruṣa broke open the cosmic egg and emerged, possessing thousands of thighs and feet, arms and eyes, and thousands of faces and heads.

Śrīdhara Svāmī — Bhāvārthadīpikā

निर्भिद्य पृथक्कृत्य स्थित इत्यर्थः ॥३५॥

Śrīdhara Svāmī — Translation

“Having broken it open and emerged” means that He divided it and then remained established distinctly.

ŚB 2.5.36

यस्येहावयवैर्लोकान् कल्पयन्ति मनीषिणः ।
कट्यादिभिरधः सप्त सप्तोर्ध्वं जघनादिभिः ॥ ३६ ॥

yasyehāvayavair lokān
kalpayanti manīṣiṇaḥ
kaṭy-ādibhir adhaḥ sapta
saptordhvaṁ jaghanādibhiḥ

Word-for-word

yasya — whose; iha — here in the universe; avayavaiḥ — by the limbs; lokān — the worlds; kalpayanti — conceive or arrange; manīṣiṇaḥ — the wise; kaṭi-ādibhiḥ — beginning from the waist; adhaḥ — downward; sapta — seven; sapta ūrdhvam — seven upward; jaghana-ādibhiḥ — beginning from the front of the hips and regions above.

Translation

Wise seers conceive the worlds in relation to His limbs: seven lower realms from the waist downward and seven higher realms from the hips and regions above.

Śrīdhara Svāmī — Bhāvārthadīpikā

तदवयवैर्लोकरचनामाह—यस्येति । कटिरित्यूर्मूलयोः पश्चाद्भागः । जघनं पुरोभागः । अधः सप्तलोकानतलादीन् । ऊर्ध्वं भूरादीन् सप्त ॥३६॥

Śrīdhara Svāmī — Translation

Brahmā now describes the arrangement of the worlds upon the limbs of that form. The waist is the rear portion at the roots of the thighs, while the jaghana is the front portion. The seven worlds below are Atala and the others; the seven above are Bhū and the higher worlds.

ŚB 2.5.37

पुरुषस्य मुखं ब्रह्म क्षत्रमेतस्य बाहवः ।
ऊर्वोर्वैश्यो भगवतः पद्भ्यां शूद्रो व्यजायत ॥ ३७ ॥

puruṣasya mukhaṁ brahma
kṣatram etasya bāhavaḥ
ūrvor vaiśyo bhagavataḥ
padbhyāṁ śūdro vyajāyata

Word for word

puruṣasya — of the Puruṣa; mukham — the mouth; brahma — the brāhmaṇa order; kṣatram — the kṣatriya order; etasya — of this Lord; bāhavaḥ — the arms; ūrvoḥ — from the thighs; vaiśyaḥ — the vaiśya; bhagavataḥ — of the Blessed Lord; padbhyām — from the feet; śūdraḥ — the śūdra; vyajāyata — was manifested.

Translation

The brāhmaṇa is represented by the Puruṣa’s mouth, the kṣatriya by His arms, and the vaiśya by His thighs; from the feet of the Lord the śūdra was manifested.

Śrīdhara Svāmī — Bhāvārthadīpikā

वर्णानां तत उत्पत्तिं दर्शयति—पुरुषस्येति । ब्रह्म ब्राह्मणो मुखम् इति कार्य-कारणयोर् अभेद-विवक्षयोक्तम् । बाहव इति च । क्षत्रं क्षत्रियः ॥३७॥

Śrīdhara Svāmī — English

He now shows the origin of the social orders from Him. In saying that the brāhmaṇa is the mouth, the effect and its cause are spoken of as non-different; the same construction applies to the arms. “Kṣatram” means the kṣatriya.

ŚB 2.5.38

भूर्लोकः कल्पितः पद्भ्यां भुवर्लोकोऽस्य नाभितः ।
हृदा स्वर्लोक उरसा महर्लोको महात्मनः ॥ ३८ ॥

bhūrlokaḥ kalpitaḥ padbhyāṁ
bhuvarloko ’sya nābhitaḥ
hṛdā svarloka urasā
maharloko mahātmanaḥ

Word for word

bhūrlokaḥ — Bhūrloka; kalpitaḥ — is conceived; padbhyām — from the feet/legs; bhuvarlokaḥ — Bhuvarloka; asya — of Him; nābhitaḥ — from the navel; hṛdā — by the heart; svarlokaḥ — Svarloka; urasā — by the chest; maharlokaḥ — Maharloka; mahātmanaḥ — of the Great Being.

Translation

Bhūrloka is conceived in relation to His legs, Bhuvarloka to His navel, Svarloka to His heart, and Maharloka to the chest of the Great Being.

Śrīdhara Svāmī — Bhāvārthadīpikā

इदानीम् उपासनार्थं लोक-कल्पना-भेदान् दर्शयन् सप्त-लोक-पक्षम् आह द्वाभ्याम् । भूर्-लोकः पातालम् आरभ्य । पद्भ्यां कटि-पर्यन्ताभ्याम् ॥३८॥

Śrīdhara Svāmī — English

Now, for purposes of meditation, he presents differing ways of mapping the worlds onto the cosmic form. In these two verses he gives the scheme of seven worlds. Here Bhūrloka is reckoned beginning with Pātāla, and “from the legs” means the region extending from the feet up to the waist.

ŚB 2.5.39

ग्रीवायां जनलोकोऽस्य तपोलोकः स्तनद्वयात् ।
मूर्धभिः सत्यलोकस्तु ब्रह्मलोकः सनातनः ॥ ३९ ॥

grīvāyāṁ janaloko ’sya
tapolokaḥ stana-dvayāt
mūrdhabhiḥ satyalokas tu
brahmalokaḥ sanātanaḥ

Word for word

grīvāyām — at the neck; janalokaḥ — Janaloka; asya — of Him; tapolokaḥ — Tapoloka; stana-dvayāt — from the pair of breasts; mūrdhabhiḥ — by the head; satyalokaḥ — Satyaloka; tu — but; brahmalokaḥ — Brahmaloka; sanātanaḥ — eternal.

Translation

Janaloka is placed at His neck, Tapoloka in relation to His two breasts, and Satyaloka at His head; Brahmaloka, however, is eternal.

Śrīdhara Svāmī — Bhāvārthadīpikā

स्तन-द्वयाद् इत्य् उपासनार्थत्वाद् ऊर्ध्वाधो-भाग-वैपरीत्यं न दोषः । यद् वा, स्तनच्-छब्दं कुर्वद् यद् ओष्ठ-द्वयं तस्माद् इत्य् अर्थः । ब्रह्म-लोको वैकुण्ठाख्यः, सनातनो नित्यः, न तु सृज्य-प्रपञ्चान्तर्वर्तीत्य् अर्थः ॥३९॥

Śrīdhara Svāmī — English

Because this arrangement is for meditation, a reversal of higher and lower bodily positions is no defect. Alternatively, the expression can be understood with reference to the pair of lips that makes the sound “stana.” Brahmaloka here is the realm called Vaikuṇṭha: it is sanātana, eternal, and does not belong within the created material cosmos.

ŚB 2.5.40

तत्कट्यां चातलं क्लृप्तमूरुभ्यां वितलं विभोः ।
जानुभ्यां सुतलं शुद्धं जङ्घाभ्यां तु तलातलम् ॥ ४० ॥

tat-kaṭyāṁ cātalaṁ kḷptam
ūrubhyāṁ vitalaṁ vibhoḥ
jānubhyāṁ sutalaṁ śuddhaṁ
jaṅghābhyāṁ tu talātalam

Word for word

tat-kaṭyām — at His waist; ca — and; atalam — Atala; kḷptam — is assigned; ūrubhyām — to the thighs; vitalam — Vitala; vibhoḥ — of the all-pervading Lord; jānubhyām — to the knees; sutalam — Sutala; śuddham — pure; jaṅghābhyām — to the shanks; tu — and; talātalam — Talātala.

Translation

Atala is assigned to the waist of the all-pervading Lord, Vitala to His thighs, pure Sutala to His knees, and Talātala to His shanks.

Śrīdhara Svāmī — Bhāvārthadīpikā (2.5.40–41)

इदानीं चतुर्दश-लोक-पक्षं दर्शयति । तत्र जघनादिभिर् भूरादयः पूर्वोक्ता एव सप्त, कट्य्-आदिभिर् अधः सप्त-लोकान् आह—तत्-कट्याम् इति द्वाभ्याम् । शुद्धं हरि-भक्त-निवासत्वात् ॥४०-४१॥

Śrīdhara Svāmī — English

He now sets out the fourteen-world scheme. The seven worlds beginning with Bhū, already described, are associated with the higher portions beginning from the hips; in these two verses the seven lower worlds are assigned beginning from the waist. Sutala is called pure because it is the dwelling place of devotees of Hari.

ŚB 2.5.41

महातलं तु गुल्फाभ्यां प्रपदाभ्यां रसातलम् ।
पातालं पादतलत इति लोकमयः पुमान् ॥ ४१ ॥

mahātalaṁ tu gulphābhyāṁ
prapadābhyāṁ rasātalam
pātālaṁ pāda-talata
iti lokamayaḥ pumān

Word for word

mahātalam — Mahātala; tu — and; gulphābhyām — at the ankles; prapadābhyām — at the foreparts of the feet; rasātalam — Rasātala; pātālam — Pātāla; pāda-talataḥ — at the soles of the feet; iti — thus; lokamayaḥ — constituted as the worlds; pumān — the Cosmic Person.

Translation

Mahātala is placed at His ankles, Rasātala at the fronts of His feet, and Pātāla at their soles. In this way the Cosmic Person is contemplated as encompassing the worlds.

Śrīdhara Svāmī — Bhāvārthadīpikā

Śrīdhara’s commentary on this verse is given jointly with 2.5.40 immediately above.

ŚB 2.5.42

भूर्लोकः कल्पितः पद्भ्यां भुवर्लोकोऽस्य नाभितः ।
स्वर्लोकः कल्पितो मूर्ध्ना इति वा लोककल्पना ॥ ४२ ॥

bhūrlokaḥ kalpitaḥ padbhyāṁ
bhuvarloko ’sya nābhitaḥ
svarlokaḥ kalpito mūrdhnā
iti vā loka-kalpanā

Word for word

bhūrlokaḥ — Bhūrloka; kalpitaḥ — is conceived; padbhyām — at the legs; bhuvarlokaḥ — Bhuvarloka; asya — of Him; nābhitaḥ — from the navel; svarlokaḥ — Svarloka; kalpitaḥ — is conceived; mūrdhnā — at the head/upper region; iti — thus; vā — alternatively; loka-kalpanā — the conception of the worlds.

Translation

Alternatively, Bhūrloka is conceived in His legs, Bhuvarloka at His navel, and Svarloka in the upper region culminating in His head. This is another way of contemplating the arrangement of the worlds.

Śrīdhara Svāmī — Bhāvārthadīpikā

त्रि-लोक-पक्षम् आह । भूर्-लोकः पातालादि-सहितः ॥४२॥

Śrīdhara Svāmī — English

He gives the three-world scheme. Here Bhūrloka includes Pātāla and the other lower worlds.

Viveka Dṛṣṭi
